# Daily Summary: 2025-05-10

### The Aloe Family Recipe for Open and Specialized Healthcare LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04388v1)
- **Authors**: Dario Garcia-Gasulla, Jordi Bayarri-Planas, Ashwin Kumar Gururajan, Enrique Lopez-Cuena, Adrian Tormos, Daniel Hinjos, Pablo Bernabeu-Perez, Anna Arias-Duart, Pablo Agustin Martin-Torres, Marta Gonzalez-Mallo, Sergio Alvarez-Napagao, Eduard Ayguadé-Parra, Ulises Cortés
- **Abstract**: Purpose: With advancements in Large Language Models (LLMs) for healthcare, the need arises for competitive open-source models to protect the public interest. This work contributes to the field of open medical LLMs by optimizing key stages of data preprocessing and training, while showing how to improve model safety (through DPO) and efficacy (through RAG). The evaluation methodology used, which includes four different types of tests, defines a new standard for the field. The resultant models, shown to be competitive with the best private alternatives, are released with a permisive license. Methods: Building on top of strong base models like Llama 3.1 and Qwen 2.5, Aloe Beta uses a custom dataset to enhance public data with synthetic Chain of Thought examples. The models undergo alignment with Direct Preference Optimization, emphasizing ethical and policy-aligned performance in the presence of jailbreaking attacks. Evaluation includes close-ended, open-ended, safety and human assessments, to maximize the reliability of results. Results: Recommendations are made across the entire pipeline, backed by the solid performance of the Aloe Family. These models deliver competitive performance across healthcare benchmarks and medical fields, and are often preferred by healthcare professionals. On bias and toxicity, the Aloe Beta models significantly improve safety, showing resilience to unseen jailbreaking attacks. For a responsible release, a detailed risk assessment specific to healthcare is attached to the Aloe Family models. Conclusion: The Aloe Beta models, and the recipe that leads to them, are a significant contribution to the open-source medical LLM field, offering top-of-the-line performance while maintaining high ethical requirements. This work sets a new standard for developing and reporting aligned LLMs in healthcare.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "The Aloe Family Recipe for Open and Specialized Healthcare LLMs":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces the Aloe Family of open-source Large Language Models (LLMs) specifically designed for healthcare applications. The authors emphasize the importance of competitive, open models to ensure public interest and accessibility in this crucial domain. They focus on optimizing data preprocessing and training methodologies, demonstrating how to enhance model safety through Direct Preference Optimization (DPO) and effectiveness through Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG). Key contributions include:

*   A custom dataset tailored for healthcare LLMs, enhancing public data with synthetic Chain of Thought (CoT) examples.
*   Multi-stage training process (instruct tuning, model merging, and alignment) building upon strong base models like Llama 3.1 and Qwen 2.5.
*   Rigorous evaluation methodology including closed-ended, open-ended, safety, and human assessments, with particular attention to bias, toxicity, and risk.
*   Release of the Aloe Beta models with a permissive license and a healthcare-specific risk assessment to promote responsible use.
*   In-context learning integration, with experiments indicating the models are capable of matching the performance of closed services through retrieval augmented generation (RAG).

The authors claim the Aloe Family represents a significant contribution to open-source medical LLMs, achieving top-tier performance while maintaining high ethical standards.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** While the paper tackles an important problem (the need for open and safe medical LLMs), the *individual components* of the Aloe Family recipe are not entirely novel. Fine-tuning, CoT data augmentation, model merging, DPO alignment, and RAG have all been explored in previous LLM research. However, the *combination* of these techniques, the specific data curation strategy, and the focus on healthcare-specific safety and evaluation are areas of considerable novelty.
*   **Significance:** The paper has a high degree of practical significance. Releasing competitive open-source medical LLMs is valuable for research, development, and deployment in real-world healthcare settings. The release of datasets and a healthcare-specific risk assessment further enhances the paper's value. The demonstration of performance competitive with closed models is a strong point.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Approach:** The paper covers the entire pipeline, from data curation to model deployment and evaluation.
    *   **Focus on Safety:** The emphasis on ethical considerations and the inclusion of a safety evaluation are crucial for responsible LLM development in healthcare.
    *   **Open Resources:** The release of models, datasets, and training details promotes reproducibility and further research.
    *   **Extensive Evaluation:** The paper uses a diverse range of evaluation methods, covering different aspects of model performance.
    *   Demonstration of RAG integration as a route to near state of the art in the field.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Detail on Synthetic Data Generation:** While the paper mentions using synthetic CoT examples, it would be beneficial to have more detail on the specific prompts and methods used, including a more rigourous comparison against other approaches.
    *   **Generalization of Human Evaluation:** Although the human evaluation is valuable, its small scale and potential for bias limit the generalizability of the findings. It would be useful to include a comparison against other open medical models with large-scale, and therefore expensive, human trials.
    *   **RAG implementation is not clearly specified.** Although RAG is included, the methodology is somewhat unclear.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to have a positive influence on the field of open-source medical LLMs. It provides a concrete recipe for building competitive models and establishes a new standard for evaluating and reporting aligned LLMs in healthcare. The availability of the Aloe Family models and datasets will facilitate further research and development. The paper could inspire more research into safety and ethical considerations for medical LLMs.

**Rationale for Score:**

I am assigning a score of **8** to this paper.

*   It addresses an important problem with a practical and well-executed solution.
*   The specific combination of techniques and the emphasis on healthcare-specific safety justify a high novelty score.
*   The extensive evaluation and the release of open resources significantly enhance the paper's value.
*   The weaknesses related to synthetic data, RAG implementation and the limited human evaluation warrant a slightly lower score. Despite these limitations, the paper is likely to have a significant impact on the field.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 0/10

### Large Means Left: Political Bias in Large Language Models Increases with Their Number of Parameters
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04393v1)
- **Authors**: David Exler, Mark Schutera, Markus Reischl, Luca Rettenberger
- **Abstract**: With the increasing prevalence of artificial intelligence, careful evaluation of inherent biases needs to be conducted to form the basis for alleviating the effects these predispositions can have on users. Large language models (LLMs) are predominantly used by many as a primary source of information for various topics. LLMs frequently make factual errors, fabricate data (hallucinations), or present biases, exposing users to misinformation and influencing opinions. Educating users on their risks is key to responsible use, as bias, unlike hallucinations, cannot be caught through data verification. We quantify the political bias of popular LLMs in the context of the recent vote of the German Bundestag using the score produced by the Wahl-O-Mat. This metric measures the alignment between an individual's political views and the positions of German political parties. We compare the models' alignment scores to identify factors influencing their political preferences. Doing so, we discover a bias toward left-leaning parties, most dominant in larger LLMs. Also, we find that the language we use to communicate with the models affects their political views. Additionally, we analyze the influence of a model's origin and release date and compare the results to the outcome of the recent vote of the Bundestag. Our results imply that LLMs are prone to exhibiting political bias. Large corporations with the necessary means to develop LLMs, thus, knowingly or unknowingly, have a responsibility to contain these biases, as they can influence each voter's decision-making process and inform public opinion in general and at scale.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates political biases in large language models (LLMs) concerning the German Bundestag election. It uses the Wahl-O-Mat, a tool assessing the alignment of individuals' political views with party positions, to quantify biases in various popular open-source LLMs. The authors find a consistent bias towards left-leaning parties, particularly pronounced in larger LLMs.  They also explore the influence of factors like language (German vs. English prompts), model size, and release date on these biases. The study indicates that larger LLMs exhibit more pronounced left-leaning biases and that the language used in prompts affects political viewpoints. They emphasize the responsibility of corporations developing LLMs to mitigate these biases, given their potential to influence voters' decision-making.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper extends prior work on political bias in LLMs by:

    *   Including newer and less-studied LLMs (DeepSeek R1, Simplescaling S1, Llama 3).
    *   Focusing on the German political landscape using the Wahl-O-Mat.
    *   Quantifying the LLMs' Left/Right positioning, which is a step towards more measurable analysis.
    *   Investigating the impact of model origin (American vs. Chinese).

    While the core idea of assessing political bias in LLMs isn't entirely new, the specific context, the inclusion of recent models, and the quantitative approach to Left/Right positioning add a degree of novelty.

*   **Significance:**

    *   **Strength:** The findings highlight the inherent political biases in LLMs, raising concerns about their potential impact on public opinion and voter decisions. The quantitative analysis, especially the Left/Right positioning metric, provides a framework for evaluating and comparing the political inclinations of different models. It is particularly important to investigate LLM's biases with specific, well-defined, regional political contexts.

    *   **Weakness:** The study's scope is limited to a single political context (German Bundestag election). The generalizability of the findings to other political systems and cultures needs further investigation. The practical impact on actual voting behavior is difficult to determine based solely on this analysis. The prompt is relatively simple and could impact the model's response. Furthermore, even if there is a left-leaning bias, there is no analysis regarding what types of data, during the training and fine-tuning phases, might have caused this issue.

    *   **Potential Influence:** The paper can contribute to ongoing discussions about responsible AI development and the need for bias mitigation strategies in LLMs. It raises awareness among developers, researchers, and policymakers about the potential for LLMs to inadvertently influence political discourse.

*   **Rigour:** The methodology is well-defined and uses a standardized metric.  The experimental setup (prompts, model selection) is clearly explained.  However, there might be limitations in inferring real-world impact based only on the output generated in an isolated experimental environment.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a valuable contribution to the field by providing a well-executed and quantified analysis of political biases in LLMs within a specific political context. While the scope is limited and the direct impact on voting behavior is difficult to assess, the study identifies important factors influencing these biases and offers a framework for further research. The extension to newer models and the focus on the German political landscape are significant additions.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### YABLoCo: Yet Another Benchmark for Long Context Code Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04406v1)
- **Authors**: Aidar Valeev, Roman Garaev, Vadim Lomshakov, Irina Piontkovskaya, Vladimir Ivanov, Israel Adewuyi
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models demonstrate the ability to solve various programming tasks, including code generation. Typically, the performance of LLMs is measured on benchmarks with small or medium-sized context windows of thousands of lines of code. At the same time, in real-world software projects, repositories can span up to millions of LoC. This paper closes this gap by contributing to the long context code generation benchmark (YABLoCo). The benchmark featured a test set of 215 functions selected from four large repositories with thousands of functions. The dataset contained metadata of functions, contexts of the functions with different levels of dependencies, docstrings, functions bodies, and call graphs for each repository. This paper presents three key aspects of the contribution. First, the benchmark aims at function body generation in large repositories in C and C++, two languages not covered by previous benchmarks. Second, the benchmark contains large repositories from 200K to 2,000K LoC. Third, we contribute a scalable evaluation pipeline for efficient computing of the target metrics and a tool for visual analysis of generated code. Overall, these three aspects allow for evaluating code generation in large repositories in C and C++.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "YABLoCo: Yet Another Benchmark for Long Context Code Generation":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces YABLoCo, a new benchmark designed to evaluate the performance of Large Language Models (LLMs) in generating code within long-context, real-world software repositories.  The benchmark focuses on C and C++ code, languages less represented in existing benchmarks. It includes 215 functions selected from four large open-source repositories, along with metadata such as function bodies, docstrings, signatures, and call graphs. The paper also presents a scalable evaluation pipeline and a tool for visual code analysis.  The authors evaluate the performance of several baseline LLMs (CodeLlama, DeepSeek Coder, and GPT-4) on YABLoCo, both with and without "oracle" context (i.e., using all relevant functions called within the target function).

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper offers a valuable contribution by addressing a gap in existing code generation benchmarks, namely the focus on C/C++ and the ability to evaluate LLMs in long-context, real-world scenarios. The creation of a dataset from large repositories of C/C++ code and providing a specific long-context generation setting with metadata is a plus.  While other repository-level benchmarks exist, their focus is often on Python and Java, or on code completion rather than full function generation. The provided evaluation pipeline also is a valuable addition.

*   **Significance:** The benchmark has the potential to significantly impact the development and evaluation of LLMs for code. By focusing on long-context code generation, YABLoCo can help researchers and practitioners to develop models capable of working with complex, interdependent codebases – a crucial step towards truly useful code assistants. The evaluation of LLMs on challenging, real-world repositories should provide useful insights for model development. The paper's experiments with oracle context provide an interesting starting point for investigating how best to leverage context in code generation. The benchmark's ability to use visual analysis tools is a strength in terms of model evaluation.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Focus on C/C++ fills a crucial gap.
    *   Long-context focus aligns with real-world scenarios.
    *   Real-world repositories increase relevance.
    *   Detailed metadata facilitates various evaluation strategies.
    *   Scalable evaluation pipeline ensures reproducibility.
    *   Open source release of the dataset and evaluation tools enhances accessibility.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Limited number of repositories (four) may restrict generalization.
    *   Potential data leakage due to pre-training on Stack datasets (acknowledged by the authors).
    *   Usage of existing tests may be suboptimal, as higher test coverage will decrease the pass@k metric.
    *   Selection approach relies on repository popularity, which can lead to skewed results, potentially favoring more superficial code.
    *   Synthetic generation of Docstrings decreased model performance, thus this aspect can be improved.

*   **Justification for Score:** The paper has its strengths and weaknesses, but the novelty and significance of this paper justifies a score of **7**. While the limitations regarding data leakage and testing methodology are valid concerns, the focus on long-context C/C++ code and the provision of a scalable evaluation pipeline represent important and valuable contributions to the field of code generation with LLMs. YABLoCo provides the community with a valuable test bed to evaluate the long-context skills for code models.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### OBLIVIATE: Robust and Practical Machine Unlearning for Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04416v1)
- **Authors**: Xiaoyu Xu, Minxin Du, Qingqing Ye, Haibo Hu
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) trained over extensive corpora risk memorizing sensitive, copyrighted, or toxic content. To address this, we propose OBLIVIATE, a robust unlearning framework that removes targeted data while preserving model utility. The framework follows a structured process: extracting target tokens, building retain sets, and fine-tuning with a tailored loss function comprising three components -- masking, distillation, and world fact. Using low-rank adapters (LoRA), it ensures efficiency without compromising unlearning quality. We conduct experiments on multiple datasets, including the Harry Potter series, WMDP, and TOFU, using a comprehensive suite of metrics: forget quality (new document-level memorization score), model utility, and fluency. Results demonstrate its effectiveness in resisting membership inference attacks, minimizing the impact on retained data, and maintaining robustness across diverse scenarios.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces OBLIVIATE, a framework for machine unlearning in large language models (LLMs). The framework aims to remove specific data from LLMs while preserving model utility and fluency. OBLIVIATE consists of a structured process involving target token extraction, retain set construction, and fine-tuning with a tailored loss function. This loss function combines masking, distillation, and a world fact component. Low-rank adapters (LoRA) are used to ensure efficiency. Experiments are conducted on several datasets using metrics for forget quality, model utility, and fluency. The results demonstrate that OBLIVIATE is effective in resisting membership inference attacks (MIAs), minimizing the impact on retained data, and maintaining robustness across diverse scenarios.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Approach:** OBLIVIATE provides a well-structured and comprehensive framework. The combined masking, distillation, and world fact loss seems promising for achieving both strong unlearning and utility preservation.
    *   **Focus on Practicality:** The use of LoRA makes the framework more practical for LLMs by reducing computational demands. The framework attempts to balance the tension between thoroughness and real-world applicability.
    *   **Document-Level Memorization Metric:** Introduction of Document-level Remnant Memorization Accuracy (DRMA) is a valuable contribution to evaluation metrics.
    *   **Context-Aware Unlearning:**  The distillation and world fact losses allows for a more targeted approach in removing unwanted content.
    *   **Rigorous Evaluation:** The paper conducts a comprehensive evaluation using multiple datasets and metrics, offering strong empirical evidence. MIAs are considered to evaluate forget quality.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Reliance on GPT-4 for Token Identification:** The framework depends on GPT-4 for identifying target tokens and extracting generic documents which introduces retrieval instability and may be less reliable. This dependency limits the replicability of the work and adds a cost component.
    *   **Limited Fluency in some Instances:** There is a reported limited ability to maintain fluency with more sensitive prompts.
    *   **Small Dataset Limitations:** Demonstrates limited effectiveness on smaller datasets which can impact real-world applications.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**

    *   **Masked loss for complete suppression of unlearning data:** It introduces a masking technique that directly suppresses the generation of unlearning data which leads to "aggressive" forgetting.
    *   **Context-Aware Unlearning:** Balancing forgetting and utility is always difficult and this framework balances that with knowledge distillation with general factual data.
    *   **Practical Applicability:** The focus on efficient fine-tuning using LoRA contributes to the practical applicability of the framework.

*   **Justification of Score:**

    I assign a score of **7.5**.  Here's why:

    *   While the combination of masking, distillation, and world fact loss provides a robust framework, its dependence on GPT-4 for target token identification slightly reduces its novelty from a purely technical perspective. Furthermore, while the idea is implemented with an LLM, and the performance results are shown on LLMs, the implementation itself is largely agnostic of LLMs. This limits the impact to LLMs, since the technique has a lot of potential impact in other machine learning areas.
    *   The focus on practical implementation and rigorous evaluation enhance the significance of the work. The inclusion of MIA resistance testing is also a plus.
    *   The novelty lies more in the *combination* of techniques and the comprehensive evaluation rather than radical new methods.  The contribution is incremental but valuable.
    * The results are convincing and the ablation study highlights the need for each component. It also provides detailed ablation to highlight the design choices of the framework.

    OBLIVIATE represents a solid contribution to the field of machine unlearning. The framework and its evaluation have the potential to influence future research and development in this area.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 0/10

### Localized Diffusion Models for High Dimensional Distributions Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04417v1)
- **Authors**: Georg A. Gottwald, Shuigen Liu, Youssef Marzouk, Sebastian Reich, Xin T. Tong
- **Abstract**: Diffusion models are the state-of-the-art tools for various generative tasks. However, estimating high-dimensional score functions makes them potentially suffer from the curse of dimensionality (CoD). This underscores the importance of better understanding and exploiting low-dimensional structure in the target distribution. In this work, we consider locality structure, which describes sparse dependencies between model components. Under locality structure, the score function is effectively low-dimensional, so that it can be estimated by a localized neural network with significantly reduced sample complexity. This motivates the localized diffusion model, where a localized score matching loss is used to train the score function within a localized hypothesis space. We prove that such localization enables diffusion models to circumvent CoD, at the price of additional localization error. Under realistic sample size scaling, we show both theoretically and numerically that a moderate localization radius can balance the statistical and localization error, leading to a better overall performance. The localized structure also facilitates parallel training of diffusion models, making it potentially more efficient for large-scale applications.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a "Localized Diffusion Model" (LDM) for generating high-dimensional distributions. The core idea is to exploit locality structure (sparse dependencies between components) in the target distribution to mitigate the curse of dimensionality (CoD) often encountered in standard diffusion models. The LDM trains a localized score function, leveraging the fact that under locality, each component of the score function depends only on a small subset of variables. The paper provides theoretical analysis showing that such localization allows diffusion models to circumvent the CoD, although it introduces a localization error. The authors show how to balance statistical and localization error. They also highlight the potential for parallel training of LDMs. Numerical experiments on time series data are presented to validate the theoretical results.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The key novelty lies in explicitly incorporating locality structure into the diffusion model framework to address the curse of dimensionality. While other works have leveraged low-dimensional structure (manifold hypothesis, Gaussian mixtures), the focus on locality and sparse dependencies is a valuable contribution. The theoretical analysis of the trade-off between statistical and localization error is also a significant advance. The algorithmic aspect concerning parallel training is also relevant.

*   **Significance:** Diffusion models are very popular but their applicability to high dimensions needs to be improved. This paper provides insights on how to leverage sparse dependency structure. The theoretical analysis on how localization can reduce statistical error is useful. The algorithm is also useful.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear and well-motivated problem statement.
    *   Solid theoretical analysis of approximation and generalization error.
    *   The parallel training aspect can be important in practice.
    *   Experiments validate the theoretical findings.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The reliance on specific regularity conditions (log-concavity, smoothness) could limit its applicability.
    *   The experimental evaluation is relatively narrow, focusing primarily on time series data. It could have been improved by also including experiments with image data.
    *   The assumption on knowing the dependency graph G or being able to estimate it is not fully explored, which is a limiting factor for application. The numerical experiments lack a discussion on how sensitive LDM is to errors in learning/estimating the dependency graph.

*   **Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a valuable contribution by introducing a novel approach to address the CoD in diffusion models by explicitly incorporating locality structure. The theoretical analysis is sound, and the numerical results support the main claims. However, certain limitations prevent this paper from being considered a truly exceptional contribution. The score does not reflect the high importance of diffusion models in many fields and should be considered as a standalone score only. In particular, the lack of robustness analysis for errors in the dependency graph hinders the practical application of the LDM algorithm.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### LONGER: Scaling Up Long Sequence Modeling in Industrial Recommenders
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04421v1)
- **Authors**: Zheng Chai, Qin Ren, Xijun Xiao, Huizhi Yang, Bo Han, Sijun Zhang, Di Chen, Hui Lu, Wenlin Zhao, Lele Yu, Xionghang Xie, Shiru Ren, Xiang Sun, Yaocheng Tan, Peng Xu, Yuchao Zheng, Di Wu
- **Abstract**: Modeling ultra-long user behavior sequences is critical for capturing both long- and short-term preferences in industrial recommender systems. Existing solutions typically rely on two-stage retrieval or indirect modeling paradigms, incuring upstream-downstream inconsistency and computational inefficiency. In this paper, we present LONGER, a Long-sequence Optimized traNsformer for GPU-Efficient Recommenders. LONGER incorporates (i) a global token mechanism for stabilizing attention over long contexts, (ii) a token merge module with lightweight InnerTransformers and hybrid attention strategy to reduce quadratic complexity, and (iii) a series of engineering optimizations, including training with mixed-precision and activation recomputation, KV cache serving, and the fully synchronous model training and serving framework for unified GPU-based dense and sparse parameter updates. LONGER consistently outperforms strong baselines in both offline metrics and online A/B testing in both advertising and e-commerce services at ByteDance, validating its consistent effectiveness and industrial-level scaling laws. Currently, LONGER has been fully deployed at more than 10 influential scenarios at ByteDance, serving billion users.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the LONGER paper:

**Summary:**

The paper presents LONGER, a novel Transformer-based architecture for modeling ultra-long user behavior sequences in industrial recommender systems. LONGER addresses the computational challenges of processing such long sequences by introducing several key components: (1) Global Tokens, which act as anchors for global information aggregation; (2) Token Merge, a mechanism to reduce sequence length and computational complexity by grouping and compressing adjacent tokens using InnerTransformers to preserve intra-group dependencies; (3) Hybrid attention that combines cross causal-attention to capture salient sequence parts and multiple layers of self-causal attention to capture higher-order interactions; and (4) multiple system-level optimizations, like GPU-synchronous parameter updates, mixed-precision training, and KV cache serving. The authors demonstrate LONGER's effectiveness through offline experiments on a large-scale industrial dataset and online A/B tests on Douyin's advertising and e-commerce platforms, showing consistent improvements over strong baselines.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents several novel architectural and engineering contributions to the field of long sequence modeling for recommender systems. The combination of global tokens with token merge and a hybrid attention mechanism presents a unique approach to efficiently handling long sequences. The engineering optimizations for training and serving are also significant contributions, addressing the practical challenges of deploying large models in industrial settings. However, the individual components are not entirely novel. Global tokens are similar to CLS tokens from BERT. The token merge bears similarities with other methods to reduce attention complexity by reducing sequence length such as strided attention or sliding window attention.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance lies in its ability to bridge the gap between academic research and industrial application. Ultra-long sequence modeling is a critical challenge in recommender systems, and LONGER provides a practical and effective solution that can be deployed at scale. The performance gains demonstrated in both offline and online experiments are substantial and indicate the potential for significant business impact. The paper includes enough architectural details that another party could re-create this model. This should help other companies implement similar architectures into their models.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Strong empirical results:** The paper provides extensive experimental results, including offline evaluations and online A/B tests, demonstrating LONGER's effectiveness across different domains.
    *   **Comprehensive architecture:** LONGER integrates several novel components to address the challenges of long sequence modeling, resulting in a well-rounded and effective architecture.
    *   **System-level optimizations:** The paper highlights the importance of system-level optimizations for deploying large models in industrial settings, providing valuable insights for practitioners.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Incremental novelty:** While the combination of components is novel, the individual components may not be completely groundbreaking. Global Tokens are related to CLS tokens and other attention reduction strategies have been implemented previously.
    *   **Limited theoretical analysis:** The paper focuses primarily on empirical results, with limited theoretical analysis of the proposed architecture.
    *   **Lack of comparative analysis:** It could have been valuable to provide more detailed comparative analysis with other existing long sequence modeling techniques, such as those based on memory networks or hierarchical attention mechanisms.

*   **Impact:** The paper has the potential to influence the field of recommender systems by providing a practical and effective solution for modeling ultra-long user behavior sequences. The proposed architecture and engineering optimizations can be adopted by other researchers and practitioners to improve the performance and scalability of their recommender systems. Other companies may implement similar structures.

**Score: 7.5**

**Justification:** The LONGER paper presents a valuable contribution to the field of recommender systems. While individual components are not groundbreaking, the novel combination of global tokens, token merge with InnerTransformers, hybrid causal attention, and system-level optimizations provides a practical and effective solution for modeling ultra-long user behavior sequences. The strong empirical results and industrial-level deployment of LONGER demonstrate its potential for significant business impact. Therefore, a score of 7.5 is warranted, acknowledging the incremental yet important novelty and practical significance of the work. The lack of extensive theoretical analysis and comparative analysis contributes to the sub-9 score.
- **Classification**: cs.IR
- **Score**: 7/10

### Theoretical Guarantees for LT-TTD: A Unified Transformer-based Architecture for Two-Level Ranking Systems
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04434v1)
- **Authors**: Ayoub Abraich
- **Abstract**: Modern recommendation and search systems typically employ multi-stage ranking architectures to efficiently handle billions of candidates. The conventional approach uses distinct L1 (candidate retrieval) and L2 (re-ranking) models with different optimization objectives, introducing critical limitations including irreversible error propagation and suboptimal ranking. This paper identifies and analyzes the fundamental limitations of this decoupled paradigm and proposes LT-TTD (Listwise Transformer with Two-Tower Distillation), a novel unified architecture that bridges retrieval and ranking phases. Our approach combines the computational efficiency of two-tower models with the expressivity of transformers in a unified listwise learning framework. We provide a comprehensive theoretical analysis of our architecture and establish formal guarantees regarding error propagation mitigation, ranking quality improvements, and optimization convergence. We derive theoretical bounds showing that LT-TTD reduces the upper limit on irretrievable relevant items by a factor that depends on the knowledge distillation strength, and prove that our multi-objective optimization framework achieves a provably better global optimum than disjoint training. Additionally, we analyze the computational complexity of our approach, demonstrating that the asymptotic complexity remains within practical bounds for real-world applications. We also introduce UPQE, a novel evaluation metric specifically designed for unified ranking architectures that holistically captures retrieval quality, ranking performance, and computational efficiency.
- **Summary**: Okay, I have analyzed the provided text. Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces LT-TTD (Listwise Transformer with Two-Tower Distillation), a novel architecture designed to unify the retrieval and ranking stages in large-scale recommendation and search systems. It addresses the limitations of traditional cascaded approaches, which suffer from error propagation and conflicting optimization objectives. LT-TTD combines the efficiency of two-tower models with the expressivity of transformers using a knowledge distillation bridge. The paper provides a theoretical analysis of the architecture, demonstrating improvements in error propagation, ranking quality, and optimization convergence. It also introduces a new evaluation metric, UPQE, tailored for unified ranking models.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Addresses a significant problem:** The paper tackles a well-known problem in large-scale retrieval systems – the inefficiencies and limitations of cascaded architectures.
    *   **Novel architecture:** LT-TTD presents a reasonably novel architecture that integrates two-tower models and transformers using knowledge distillation.
    *   **Theoretical analysis:** The theoretical analysis provides a rigorous justification for the benefits of the proposed architecture, including bounds on error propagation, optimality of multi-objective optimization, and convergence analysis. This is a substantial strength.
    *   **UPQE metric:** The introduction of the UPQE metric is a valuable contribution, as it addresses the need for evaluation metrics that are specifically designed for unified ranking models.
    *   **Clear and well-written:** The paper is generally well-written and clearly explains the proposed architecture and its theoretical properties.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Practical Validation:** The paper lacks experimental results. The theoretical guarantees are impressive, but the practical performance of LT-TTD in real-world scenarios remains unclear. Simulations or benchmark datasets would be needed to validate the performance. It's unknown if all the theoretical results hold up.
    *   **Complexity:** The architecture appears to be quite complex, which could make it challenging to implement and deploy in practice. A more detailed discussion of implementation challenges would be beneficial.
    *   **Hyperparameter Sensitivity:** The reliance on multiple hyperparameters (e.g., weights for different loss components in multi-objective optimization, UPQE hyperparameters) could make the system difficult to tune.
    *   **Incremental Novelty:** While the architecture has novel components, one could argue that it's an incremental improvement over existing techniques (two-tower models, transformers, knowledge distillation). A more persuasive argument about its disruptive potential would strengthen the paper.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**

The novelty lies primarily in the *integration* of existing techniques into a unified architecture with a specific focus on mitigating the limitations of cascaded ranking systems. The two-tower models, transformers, and knowledge distillation are all well-established. However, the way they are combined and optimized is unique. The theoretical analysis elevates the significance of the paper, providing a strong rationale for the approach. The potential impact on the field is that it proposes a method to replace a classic and foundational approach to ranking.

Overall, the paper represents a significant contribution to the field, due to it providing rigorous theoretical guarantees of the LT-TTD as well as introducing a novel ranking model which combines the strengths of various state-of-the-art models. However, the paper lacks experimental validation of it's effectiveness.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:**

I assigned a score of 7.5 due to the theoretical contributions and the promise of the proposed architecture. The theoretical analysis is solid and provides a strong justification for the approach. The UPQE metric is a valuable addition to the evaluation toolkit. However, the absence of empirical validation significantly limits the paper's impact. Without experimental results, it's impossible to assess the practical feasibility and effectiveness of LT-TTD compared to existing methods. The paper could have been improved through an analysis of publicly-available benchmark datasets.

The paper would have scored much higher with strong experimental results. While the theoretical justifications are substantial, the value is significantly reduced without any form of validation of it's performance.

- **Classification**: cs.IR
- **Score**: 7/10

### Towards Effectively Leveraging Execution Traces for Program Repair with Code LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04441v1)
- **Authors**: Mirazul Haque, Petr Babkin, Farima Farmahinifarahani, Manuela Veloso
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) show promising performance on various programming tasks, including Automatic Program Repair (APR). However, most approaches to LLM-based APR are limited to the static analysis of the programs, while disregarding their runtime behavior. Inspired by knowledge-augmented NLP, in this work, we aim to remedy this potential blind spot by augmenting standard APR prompts with program execution traces. We evaluate our approach using the GPT family of models on three popular APR datasets. Our findings suggest that simply incorporating execution traces into the prompt provides a limited performance improvement over trace-free baselines, in only 2 out of 6 tested dataset / model configurations. We further find that the effectiveness of execution traces for APR diminishes as their complexity increases. We explore several strategies for leveraging traces in prompts and demonstrate that LLM-optimized prompts help outperform trace-free prompts more consistently. Additionally, we show trace-based prompting to be superior to finetuning a smaller LLM on a small-scale dataset; and conduct probing studies reinforcing the notion that execution traces can complement the reasoning abilities of the LLMs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the effectiveness of augmenting LLM-based automatic program repair (APR) prompts with execution traces.  It explores different ways to incorporate these traces, including simply adding them, optimizing their format for LLMs, and using confidence-based prompt selection. The study uses the GPT family of models and evaluates the approach on Refactory, RunBugRun, and HumanEval-Java datasets.  The key findings are that simply adding execution traces does not consistently improve APR performance, that longer/more complex traces can be detrimental, and that LLM-optimized traces offer the most consistent improvements.  The paper also demonstrates that prompting with traces is superior to finetuning a smaller LLM on the same data and includes probing studies to understand how LLMs process trace information.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The idea of using execution traces to augment LLM-based APR is not entirely novel. Existing work has explored using traces for *pretraining* code LLMs (e.g., TraceFixer), but this paper's focus on using traces directly in the *prompt* of pretrained LLMs during APR is a distinguishing factor. This is important because it does not involve any further finetuning to solve a particular task. The various strategies for integrating traces (collated, LLM-optimized, confidence-based selection) adds incremental novelty.
* **Significance:**  APR is a significant area in software engineering, and the results provide valuable insights into how best to leverage runtime information within LLMs for code repair. The finding that naive trace integration can be ineffective is important, as it prevents others from pursuing a dead-end approach. The demonstration that LLM-optimized traces can lead to consistent improvements is potentially impactful. The comparison against fine-tuning, albeit on a limited dataset, is a useful benchmark.
* **Strengths:**
    * **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper explores a variety of prompting strategies, trace representations, and datasets.
    * **Well-Defined Research Questions:** The research questions are clear and address specific aspects of trace-based APR.
    * **Probing Studies:**  The inclusion of probing studies to understand LLM behavior is a strength. It attempts to go beyond simply reporting performance numbers to understanding *why* certain approaches work or don't work.
    * **Practical Relevance:** The focus on practical APR tasks and using readily available LLMs (GPT-3.5, GPT-4) enhances the relevance of the work.
* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Limited Datasets:** The datasets used, while popular, are still somewhat constrained in terms of the complexity of the bugs and the scale of the code.  The reliance on algorithmic datasets might not fully generalize to real-world software projects with complex dependencies.
    * **Limited Fine-tuning Comparison:** The comparison with fine-tuning is limited by the small dataset and model size of the fine-tuned model. This makes the comparison less convincing.
    * **Lack of Ablation Studies on Optimization Process:** The paper mentions LLM-optimized traces but doesn't detail the ablation of different optimization strategies. How much does the LLM affect the trace quality?
    * **Traces Definition:** The trace generation process and format is too simple and it is only focused on variables' states.

* **Impact:** The paper's impact will likely be moderate. It provides useful guidance for researchers and practitioners working on LLM-based APR. It will probably push work into more informed ways of integrating runtime information. The limitations in dataset size and fine-tuning comparison limit the generalizability and significance.
* **Clarity:** The paper is well-written and organized. The methodology and results are clearly explained.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper offers a valuable exploration of integrating execution traces into LLM-based APR, with clear demonstrations and analyses. While the general approach is not completely novel, its insights into the nuances of trace integration, and its comparison against finetuning add unique value. It also has a clear methodology and well-organized experiments.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 6/10

### M2Rec: Multi-scale Mamba for Efficient Sequential Recommendation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04445v1)
- **Authors**: Qianru Zhang, Liang Qu, Honggang Wen, Dong Huang, Siu-Ming Yiu, Nguyen Quoc Viet Hung, Hongzhi Yin
- **Abstract**: Sequential recommendation systems aim to predict users' next preferences based on their interaction histories, but existing approaches face critical limitations in efficiency and multi-scale pattern recognition. While Transformer-based methods struggle with quadratic computational complexity, recent Mamba-based models improve efficiency but fail to capture periodic user behaviors, leverage rich semantic information, or effectively fuse multimodal features. To address these challenges, we propose \model, a novel sequential recommendation framework that integrates multi-scale Mamba with Fourier analysis, Large Language Models (LLMs), and adaptive gating. First, we enhance Mamba with Fast Fourier Transform (FFT) to explicitly model periodic patterns in the frequency domain, separating meaningful trends from noise. Second, we incorporate LLM-based text embeddings to enrich sparse interaction data with semantic context from item descriptions. Finally, we introduce a learnable gate mechanism to dynamically balance temporal (Mamba), frequency (FFT), and semantic (LLM) features, ensuring harmonious multimodal fusion. Extensive experiments demonstrate that \model\ achieves state-of-the-art performance, improving Hit Rate@10 by 3.2\% over existing Mamba-based models while maintaining 20\% faster inference than Transformer baselines. Our results highlight the effectiveness of combining frequency analysis, semantic understanding, and adaptive fusion for sequential recommendation. Code and datasets are available at: https://anonymous.4open.science/r/M2Rec.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "M²Rec: Multi-scale Mamba for Efficient Sequential Recommendation" proposes a novel sequential recommendation framework to address the limitations of existing methods in capturing multi-scale patterns, leveraging semantic information, and fusing multimodal features efficiently. The core idea is to enhance the Mamba architecture with Fast Fourier Transform (FFT) for explicit modeling of periodic patterns, incorporate LLM-based text embeddings for semantic enrichment, and introduce an adaptive gating mechanism for dynamic feature fusion.  Experimental results on several datasets demonstrate state-of-the-art performance, surpassing Mamba-based baselines while maintaining competitive inference speed compared to Transformer-based methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a combination of techniques that, while individually known, haven't been integrated in this specific way for sequential recommendation.  The key novelty lies in the fusion strategy: specifically, the synergy between Mamba, FFT, LLMs, and the adaptive gating mechanism. The explicit modeling of periodicity using FFT in the context of Mamba is a notable contribution. It addresses a key weakness of vanilla Mamba, which is primarily designed for temporal relationships and not frequency analysis. The use of LLMs is less novel, but the gate mechanism to dynamically balance LLM, Mamba, and FFT features contributes to the novelty.

*   **Significance:** The work addresses crucial challenges in sequential recommendation: computational efficiency and the ability to capture complex user behaviors. The computational efficiency achieved by using Mamba is significant, given the quadratic complexity of Transformer models.  The explicit modeling of periodic patterns addresses a gap in existing Mamba-based approaches, which typically focus on raw temporal dependencies. Combining this with semantic information from LLMs and an intelligent fusion mechanism offers a more comprehensive model. The empirical results support the claim of state-of-the-art performance, making the approach potentially impactful.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive approach:**  The method integrates multiple techniques to address distinct challenges.
    *   **Empirical validation:**  Extensive experiments on diverse datasets demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed approach.
    *   **Efficiency:**  The use of Mamba ensures efficient computation, which is vital for practical applications.
    *   **Clear explanation:** The paper clearly explains the technical details and motivations behind each component of the framework.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Complexity:** The framework is relatively complex, which could make it difficult to implement and debug.  The need to tune several interacting components (Mamba, FFT, LLMs, gating mechanism) could be a practical challenge.
    *   **LLM dependency:** The reliance on LLMs might raise concerns about computational cost and the need for large pre-trained models.  While the gate mechanism attempts to mitigate this, the overall system complexity is increased.
    *   **Limited exploration of FFT configurations:** Although FFT is used, the experimental section did not explore parameters of FFT in depth (other than the cut-off frequency). How much performance does it gain by different parameters in FFT (window, overlap, normalization)?
    *   **Scalability issues**: While claiming efficiency due to mamba, no discussions has been put into how FFT will effect with long sequences. As the method introduces an additional FFT process, how this will impact on efficiency is not clearly explained.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper could influence future research by encouraging the integration of frequency analysis techniques with state-space models for sequential recommendation. It also provides a useful framework for combining temporal, semantic, and periodic information in a principled way.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper introduces a reasonably novel and impactful approach to sequential recommendation by intelligently combining Mamba, FFT, LLMs, and a gating mechanism. While the individual techniques aren't entirely new, their synergistic combination and demonstrated effectiveness warrant a good score. However, the complexity of the framework, the dependency on LLMs, and the limited exploration of FFT configurations hold it back from a higher rating. The paper shows that the system is effective, and there are limited discussions on how to use FFT with the long sequence scenario.
Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.IR
- **Score**: 7/10

### Miipher-2: A Universal Speech Restoration Model for Million-Hour Scale Data Restoration
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04457v1)
- **Authors**: Shigeki Karita, Yuma Koizumi, Heiga Zen, Haruko Ishikawa, Robin Scheibler, Michiel Bacchiani
- **Abstract**: Training data cleaning is a new application for generative model-based speech restoration (SR). This paper introduces Miipher-2, an SR model designed for million-hour scale data, for training data cleaning for large-scale generative models like large language models. Key challenges addressed include generalization to unseen languages, operation without explicit conditioning (e.g., text, speaker ID), and computational efficiency. Miipher-2 utilizes a frozen, pre-trained Universal Speech Model (USM), supporting over 300 languages, as a robust, conditioning-free feature extractor. To optimize efficiency and minimize memory, Miipher-2 incorporates parallel adapters for predicting clean USM features from noisy inputs and employs the WaneFit neural vocoder for waveform synthesis. These components were trained on 3,000 hours of multi-lingual, studio-quality recordings with augmented degradations, while USM parameters remained fixed. Experimental results demonstrate Miipher-2's superior or comparable performance to conventional SR models in word-error-rate, speaker similarity, and both objective and subjective sound quality scores across all tested languages. Miipher-2 operates efficiently on consumer-grade accelerators, achieving a real-time factor of 0.0078, enabling the processing of a million-hour speech dataset in approximately three days using only 100 such accelerators.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the Miipher-2 paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Miipher-2, a universal speech restoration (SR) model designed for cleaning million-hour-scale speech datasets.  The goal is to improve the quality of training data used for large generative models like large language models (LLMs).  Miipher-2 addresses challenges like generalizing to unseen languages, operating without explicit conditioning (text or speaker ID), and achieving computational efficiency. The core idea is to leverage a pre-trained Universal Speech Model (USM) as a frozen feature extractor and then train parallel adapters (PAs) and a modified WaveFit vocoder on a smaller dataset of studio-quality recordings with synthetic degradations.  Experiments demonstrate that Miipher-2 achieves comparable or superior performance to existing SR models on various metrics and operates efficiently on consumer-grade hardware, allowing for the processing of massive datasets within a reasonable timeframe.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The paper's novelty lies in the integration of several existing components in a specific way to address the unique challenges of million-hour scale speech data cleaning. While individually, USM, parallel adapters, and WaveFit are not novel, their combination for this specific purpose, with adaptations for efficiency, is. The key idea of leveraging a frozen, large-scale USM for zero-shot generalization to unseen languages is interesting. Applying SR for data cleaning, particularly for LLM-scale datasets, is a relatively recent development, further contributing to the paper's novelty.

*   **Significance:**  The significance of the paper lies in its potential to improve the quality of training data for large generative models. High-quality data is critical for the performance of these models, and SR techniques like Miipher-2 offer a way to clean noisy web-scraped datasets.  The efficiency of Miipher-2 is also significant, as it enables the processing of datasets that were previously infeasible to clean.  This can lead to better generative models, especially in low-resource languages where high-quality data is scarce.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Computational Efficiency:** The paper convincingly demonstrates significant improvements in computational efficiency. The reduced memory footprint and faster real-time factor are critical for large-scale applications.
    *   **Generalization:** The use of the USM allows for good performance in unseen languages without requiring language-specific training data. This is a major strength in multilingual scenarios.
    *   **Ablation Studies:** The paper includes ablation studies that justify the design choices (e.g., comparing against Miipher-USM).
    *   **Dataset Distillation:** The Miipher-2-P experiments highlight the potential to use SR-cleaned data to train subsequent models, suggesting a useful data distillation pipeline.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Subjective Evaluation:** While objective metrics are thorough, the subjective evaluation is limited to English, with a relatively small number of participants. Further multilingual subjective testing would strengthen the claims.
    *   **Reliance on Synthetic Data:** The training of adapters and WaveFit relies on synthetic noise and reverberation. While this is a common practice, the paper doesn't deeply explore how well the model generalizes to real-world degradations beyond what is simulated.
    *   **Lack of Code Release:**  The lack of code and model release is a significant limitation. It prevents independent verification and adoption by the broader research community. The stated reason due to misuse risks is understandable, but alternative solutions like a limited access release or a more detailed reproduction guide could be considered.

*   **Impact:** The potential impact is substantial, as improved data quality leads to better generative models, especially in scenarios with data scarcity. This could influence training methodologies for LLMs and TTS systems.

*   **Critical Score Justification:** The combination of existing techniques is clever and appropriate for the problem. The results are convincingly presented and generally strong. However, some key weaknesses hold it back from a higher score. The limited subjective evaluation and the lack of code release hamper the community's ability to fully assess and build upon this work. These limitations reduce the overall impact.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.SD
- **Score**: 7/10

### Spectral and Temporal Denoising for Differentially Private Optimization
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04468v1)
- **Authors**: Hyeju Shin, Kyudan Jung, Seongwon Yun, Juyoung Yun
- **Abstract**: This paper introduces the FFT-Enhanced Kalman Filter (FFTKF), a differentially private optimization method that addresses the challenge of preserving performance in DP-SGD, where added noise typically degrades model utility. FFTKF integrates frequency-domain noise shaping with Kalman filtering to enhance gradient quality while preserving $(\varepsilon, \delta)$-DP guarantees. It employs a high-frequency shaping mask in the Fourier domain to concentrate differential privacy noise in less informative spectral components, preserving low-frequency gradient signals. A scalar-gain Kalman filter with finite-difference Hessian approximation further refines the denoised gradients. With a per-iteration complexity of $\mathcal{O}(d \log d)$, FFTKF demonstrates improved test accuracy over DP-SGD and DiSK across MNIST, CIFAR-10, CIFAR-100, and Tiny-ImageNet datasets using CNNs, Wide ResNets, and Vision Transformers. Theoretical analysis confirms that FFTKF maintains equivalent privacy guarantees while achieving a tighter privacy-utility trade-off through reduced noise and controlled bias.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Spectral and Temporal Denoising for Differentially Private Optimization":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a new differentially private (DP) optimization method called FFT-Enhanced Kalman Filter (FFTKF). FFTKF combines frequency-domain noise shaping using the Fast Fourier Transform (FFT) with Kalman filtering to improve gradient quality while preserving DP guarantees. The key idea is to concentrate DP noise in high-frequency components (considered less informative) of the gradient using FFT and a shaping mask. Then, a scalar-gain Kalman filter further refines the denoised gradients by leveraging temporal correlations. The authors demonstrate improved test accuracy of FFTKF over DP-SGD and DiSK (a prior Kalman Filter-based DP optimizer) across several datasets (MNIST, CIFAR-10, CIFAR-100, Tiny-ImageNet) and model architectures (CNNs, Wide ResNets, Vision Transformers).  A theoretical analysis is provided to support the claim of maintaining privacy guarantees while achieving a better privacy-utility tradeoff.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Novelty:** Combining FFT-based noise shaping with Kalman filtering in the context of DP optimization seems like a novel approach. It addresses a crucial problem in DP learning which is the degradation of model utility due to noise injection.
    *   **Empirical Results:**  The experimental results are compelling.  FFTKF consistently outperforms DP-SGD and DiSK across a diverse set of datasets and model architectures, particularly under tight privacy budgets. This is a strong indicator of the effectiveness of the proposed method. The ablation studies help to understand the impact of FFTKF parameters.
    *   **Theoretical Analysis:**  The theoretical analysis supports the claims of maintaining privacy guarantees and achieving a better privacy-utility tradeoff. The lemmas and theorems provide a formal justification for the method's effectiveness.
    *   **Computational Efficiency:** The authors emphasize the O(d log d) per-iteration complexity, which is a crucial aspect for scalability, especially with high dimensional models.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Incremental Improvement:** While the results show improved performance over DiSK, the improvements seem to be only marginal in some benchmarks. A more thorough investigation could be performed in order to clearly highlight the performance improvements achieved by FFTKF.
    *   **Limited Novelty of Kalman Filter Implementation:** The paper uses a scalar-gain Kalman filter similar to DiSK which somewhat diminishes the novelty of the design. Perhaps more intricate Kalman filter implementation could provide better performance improvements.
    *   **Parameter Sensitivity:**  Although ablation studies are presented, the optimal parameter settings for the high-frequency shaping mask (rho and lambda) are not rigorously explored. How to select the appropriate values for these parameters may be critical to achieving good performance in practice.
    *   **Limited Practicality:** While the FFTKF has its merits, it also inherits some limitations of Kalman filter-based methods. Specifically, the finite difference Hessian approximation needs to be recomputed at each iteration which makes it hard to scale to very large models.

*   **Significance:**
    *   The paper addresses a critical problem in differentially private learning.  Improving the privacy-utility tradeoff is essential for the broader adoption of DP in real-world applications.
    *   The approach is likely to inspire further research into combining signal processing techniques with state estimation methods for DP optimization.
    *   FFTKF can potentially be incorporated into existing DP learning frameworks, providing a more efficient and accurate gradient estimation method.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel combination of FFT-based noise shaping and Kalman filtering for DP optimization, supported by both empirical results and theoretical analysis. However, the improvements achieved are somewhat marginal compared to prior works. The performance improvement compared to DiSK is not substantial in some benchmarks.

Overall, the paper introduces a valuable contribution to the field by offering a computationally efficient method that can be integrated with existing frameworks to enhance their privacy-utility tradeoff. It also has some weaknesses in practical implementation, and the novelty of the Kalman filtering portion is not significant.
Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### TrajEvo: Designing Trajectory Prediction Heuristics via LLM-driven Evolution
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04480v1)
- **Authors**: Zhikai Zhao, Chuanbo Hua, Federico Berto, Kanghoon Lee, Zihan Ma, Jiachen Li, Jinkyoo Park
- **Abstract**: Trajectory prediction is a crucial task in modeling human behavior, especially in fields as social robotics and autonomous vehicle navigation. Traditional heuristics based on handcrafted rules often lack accuracy, while recently proposed deep learning approaches suffer from computational cost, lack of explainability, and generalization issues that limit their practical adoption. In this paper, we introduce TrajEvo, a framework that leverages Large Language Models (LLMs) to automatically design trajectory prediction heuristics. TrajEvo employs an evolutionary algorithm to generate and refine prediction heuristics from past trajectory data. We introduce a Cross-Generation Elite Sampling to promote population diversity and a Statistics Feedback Loop allowing the LLM to analyze alternative predictions. Our evaluations show TrajEvo outperforms previous heuristic methods on the ETH-UCY datasets, and remarkably outperforms both heuristics and deep learning methods when generalizing to the unseen SDD dataset. TrajEvo represents a first step toward automated design of fast, explainable, and generalizable trajectory prediction heuristics. We make our source code publicly available to foster future research at https://github.com/ai4co/trajevo.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the TRAJEVO paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces TRAJEVO, a novel framework that automates the design of trajectory prediction heuristics using Large Language Models (LLMs) and evolutionary algorithms. Unlike traditional handcrafted heuristics (which lack accuracy) and deep learning approaches (which are computationally expensive, lack explainability, and have generalization issues), TRAJEVO iteratively generates, evaluates, and refines prediction heuristics directly from trajectory data. Key components of TRAJEVO include a Cross-Generation Elite Sampling strategy to maintain population diversity and a Statistics Feedback Loop that enables the LLM to analyze heuristic performance. The authors demonstrate that TRAJEVO outperforms existing heuristic methods on the ETH-UCY dataset and exhibits remarkable generalization to the unseen SDD dataset, while maintaining computational efficiency and interpretability.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper demonstrates a novel integration of LLMs and evolutionary algorithms for the specific problem of trajectory prediction heuristic design. This approach departs from both traditional handcrafted heuristics and end-to-end deep learning. The Cross-Generation Elite Sampling and Statistics Feedback Loop are also novel contributions, tailored to the task at hand. The idea of *automatically* designing interpretable heuristics is a significant departure from traditional approaches.

*   **Significance:** The potential significance of TRAJEVO is multi-faceted:

    *   **Explainability:** The generated heuristics are Python code snippets, inherently more explainable than black-box neural networks. This is crucial in safety-critical applications like autonomous driving and robotics.
    *   **Efficiency:** TRAJEVO-generated heuristics are significantly faster than deep learning models, making them suitable for resource-constrained environments.
    *   **Generalization:** The paper presents compelling evidence of superior cross-dataset generalization compared to both heuristics and deep learning methods, a critical challenge in deploying trajectory prediction systems in the real world.
    *   **Automation:** TRAJEVO automates the heuristic design process, potentially democratizing the development of trajectory prediction systems and reducing the reliance on human experts.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Well-defined problem and approach:** The paper clearly defines the problem and presents a well-structured and explained framework.
    *   **Strong experimental results:** The experimental results demonstrate the effectiveness of TRAJEVO on multiple datasets and compared against a wide range of baselines, including both heuristic and deep learning methods. The cross-dataset generalization results are particularly compelling.
    *   **Ablation study:** The ablation study provides valuable insights into the contribution of each component of the TRAJEVO framework.
    *   **Thorough analysis and discussion:** The paper offers a detailed analysis of the results, including a discussion of limitations and future directions.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **In-distribution Accuracy:** While showing impressive generalization, the paper acknowledges the heuristics don't consistently achieve the absolute lowest error metrics compared to state-of-the-art deep learning *within* the training distribution (ETH-UCY). This represents a gap that could be addressed with further research.
    *   **Input Data Complexity:** The current framework relies primarily on positional history as input. Integrating richer sensor data (agent types, semantic maps, perception outputs) would significantly enhance the framework's applicability to real-world robotic systems.
    *   **Downstream Task Evaluation:** The paper evaluates TRAJEVO based on trajectory prediction metrics. Evaluating performance directly on downstream robotic tasks (e.g., navigation, planning) would provide more direct evidence of its practical value.
    *   **Dependence on LLM:** The performance of TRAJEVO is inherently tied to the capabilities of the underlying LLM (Gemini 2.0 Flash in this case). Future work could explore the sensitivity of TRAJEVO to different LLMs and investigate strategies for mitigating the impact of LLM biases.

*   **Potential Influence:** TRAJEVO has the potential to significantly impact the field of trajectory prediction by offering a compelling alternative to both traditional heuristics and deep learning methods. Its explainability, efficiency, and generalization capabilities make it particularly well-suited for real-world robotic applications. The automation of heuristic design could also lower the barrier to entry for researchers and practitioners in this field.
*   **Long-term impact:** TRAJEVO may also inspire new research directions at the intersection of LLMs, evolutionary algorithms, and robotic perception.

**Score:** 7

**Rationale:** While TRAJEVO doesn't completely surpass the in-distribution accuracy of highly specialized deep learning models, its unique combination of automation, explainability, efficiency, and impressive generalization represents a substantial advance in the field. The innovative use of LLMs for heuristic design and the well-designed framework justify a score of 7. The limitations discussed above provide clear directions for future research that could further enhance TRAJEVO's impact and justify a higher score.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### CAD-Llama: Leveraging Large Language Models for Computer-Aided Design Parametric 3D Model Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04481v1)
- **Authors**: Jiahao Li, Weijian Ma, Xueyang Li, Yunzhong Lou, Guichun Zhou, Xiangdong Zhou
- **Abstract**: Recently, Large Language Models (LLMs) have achieved significant success, prompting increased interest in expanding their generative capabilities beyond general text into domain-specific areas. This study investigates the generation of parametric sequences for computer-aided design (CAD) models using LLMs. This endeavor represents an initial step towards creating parametric 3D shapes with LLMs, as CAD model parameters directly correlate with shapes in three-dimensional space. Despite the formidable generative capacities of LLMs, this task remains challenging, as these models neither encounter parametric sequences during their pretraining phase nor possess direct awareness of 3D structures. To address this, we present CAD-Llama, a framework designed to enhance pretrained LLMs for generating parametric 3D CAD models. Specifically, we develop a hierarchical annotation pipeline and a code-like format to translate parametric 3D CAD command sequences into Structured Parametric CAD Code (SPCC), incorporating hierarchical semantic descriptions. Furthermore, we propose an adaptive pretraining approach utilizing SPCC, followed by an instruction tuning process aligned with CAD-specific guidelines. This methodology aims to equip LLMs with the spatial knowledge inherent in parametric sequences. Experimental results demonstrate that our framework significantly outperforms prior autoregressive methods and existing LLM baselines.
- **Summary**: The paper "CAD-Llama: Leveraging Large Language Models for Computer-Aided Design Parametric 3D Model Generation" presents a novel framework, CAD-Llama, that enhances large language models (LLMs) for generating parametric 3D CAD models based on text instructions. The core contributions include: (1) a hierarchical annotation pipeline for 3D CAD models, capturing structured information and textual descriptions of shapes and geometry, (2) an adaptive pretraining paradigm combined with instruction tuning to align LLMs with CAD sequence modeling, and (3) experimental results demonstrating improved performance compared to prior autoregressive methods and LLM baselines in various CAD-related tasks. The framework translates CAD command sequences into a structured format called SPCC, incorporating hierarchical semantic descriptions. CAD-Llama uses this structured data for adaptive pretraining and instruction tuning of an open-source LLM.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its structured approach to adapting LLMs for parametric CAD generation. While prior work has explored LLMs for CAD, CAD-Llama's hierarchical annotation pipeline and SPCC format are significant steps towards bridging the gap between natural language understanding and the precise nature of CAD parameters. The adaptive pretraining strategy further enhances the model's ability to capture fine-grained differences between similar CAD models.  However, the dependence on a VLM like GPT-4 for annotation, although practically effective, somewhat diminishes the novelty since a considerable portion of the contribution is offloaded to another large model. The concept of structuring CAD sequences as a more readily ingestible "code" is a good idea, but not groundbreaking.

**Significance:** The potential impact of this work is notable. CAD generative modeling is a significant research area, and more effective methods that leverage LLMs and have the ability to follow complex text instructions can find widespread applications. This work could enable more intuitive and accessible CAD design processes. The fact that the method outperforms existing parametric CAD generation models as well as general LLMs supports its significance. A weakness in its significance, however, is the relatively limited demonstration of CAD editing capabilities. While "addition" and "deletion" tasks were conducted, these editing capabilities are not fully as significant compared to other CAD models that allow a diverse range of editing capabilities from B-reps to text.

**Strengths:**
*   The hierarchical annotation pipeline and SPCC format create a structured representation for CAD data, making it more amenable to LLMs.
*   The adaptive pretraining strategy improves learning efficiency by grouping similar CAD models.
*   Experimental results demonstrate significant performance gains over existing methods in various CAD tasks.
*   Clear and well-organized presentation.

**Weaknesses:**
*   Reliance on GPT-4 for the annotation stage introduces potential biases and cost considerations.
*   While the results on various tasks (text-to-CAD, unconditional generation) are good, the reliance on a pre-trained and commercially available VLM model limits the novelty of the technical approach. The core idea is excellent, but the execution is significantly reliant on other models.
*   Evaluation metrics for CAD editing (addition and deletion) lack depth.
*   The scale and complexity of the generated CAD models, while improved, still appear somewhat limited compared to what professional CAD software can produce.
*   Limited demonstrations of the CAD editing capabilities.
*   No discussion is provided regarding the limitations of SPCC.

**Justification:**

The paper presents a well-executed approach for a challenging problem. It offers tangible improvements in CAD model generation using LLMs. However, the dependence on GPT-4 for annotation, somewhat limited scope in CAD editing capabilities, and the incremental nature of certain components (adapting LLMs) temper its impact. Further, it remains unclear what kinds of CAD models are most suitable or unsuitable for the SPCC-based approach. Given these aspects, the paper presents an evolutionary step in the usage of LLMs for CAD design rather than a revolutionary breakthrough.
