### Efficient Flow Matching using Latent Variables
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04486v1)
- **Authors**: Anirban Samaddar, Yixuan Sun, Viktor Nilsson, Sandeep Madireddy
- **Abstract**: Flow matching models have shown great potential in image generation tasks among probabilistic generative models. Building upon the ideas of continuous normalizing flows, flow matching models generalize the transport path of the diffusion models from a simple prior distribution to the data. Most flow matching models in the literature do not explicitly model the underlying structure/manifold in the target data when learning the flow from a simple source distribution like the standard Gaussian. This leads to inefficient learning, especially for many high-dimensional real-world datasets, which often reside in a low-dimensional manifold. Existing strategies of incorporating manifolds, including data with underlying multi-modal distribution, often require expensive training and hence frequently lead to suboptimal performance. To this end, we present \texttt{Latent-CFM}, which provides simplified training/inference strategies to incorporate multi-modal data structures using pretrained deep latent variable models. Through experiments on multi-modal synthetic data and widely used image benchmark datasets, we show that \texttt{Latent-CFM} exhibits improved generation quality with significantly less training ($\sim 50\%$ less in some cases) and computation than state-of-the-art flow matching models. Using a 2d Darcy flow dataset, we demonstrate that our approach generates more physically accurate samples than competitive approaches. In addition, through latent space analysis, we demonstrate that our approach can be used for conditional image generation conditioned on latent features.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Efficient Flow Matching using Latent Variables":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Latent-CFM, a novel approach to improve flow matching generative models by explicitly incorporating data structure using pre-trained deep latent variable models (LVMs). Latent-CFM leverages LVMs to learn a lower-dimensional latent space representation of the data, which is then used to condition the flow matching process. This aims to address the inefficiency of standard flow matching models when dealing with high-dimensional data residing on lower-dimensional manifolds. The authors demonstrate that Latent-CFM improves generation quality, reduces training time, and enables conditional image generation based on latent features, outperforming state-of-the-art flow matching methods on synthetic, image benchmark datasets (MNIST, CIFAR10), and a 2D Darcy flow dataset.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its effective combination of flow matching with pre-trained deep latent variable models.  While conditioning flow matching on latent spaces has been explored before, this work differentiates itself by:

    *   Utilizing *pre-trained* LVMs, potentially leading to more stable training and efficient extraction of relevant data features. This helps sidestep the expensive joint training of VAE and flow matching models (like VRFM) reducing computational overhead.
    *   Specifically targeting scenarios where high-dimensional data resides on a lower-dimensional manifold, a common characteristic of real-world data, allowing for more efficient flows.
    *   Demonstrating improved performance and training efficiency compared to competing flow matching approaches on several benchmark datasets.
    *   Highlighting Latent-CFM’s ability to generate physically accurate results.

*   **Significance:** The work addresses a crucial limitation of standard flow matching – the inability to efficiently model complex data structures. By leveraging LVMs, Latent-CFM enhances generation quality and reduces training costs, making flow matching more practical for real-world applications, especially in scientific domains where underlying physical constraints often induce manifold structures. The ability to generate samples conditioned on latent features also opens up new possibilities for controlled generation and interpretability. The observed improvements in physical plausibility of generated data, demonstrated through the Darcy flow experiment, are significant for scientific computing applications.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear problem definition:** The paper clearly articulates the limitations of standard flow matching.
    *   **Well-motivated approach:** The use of LVMs is logically justified.
    *   **Effective method:** The Latent-CFM approach demonstrates tangible improvements in generation quality, training efficiency, and conditional generation capabilities.
    *   **Comprehensive experiments:** The experiments cover a range of datasets and provide convincing evidence of the method's effectiveness, including both synthetic and real-world datasets.
    *   **Strong results:** The results are convincingly demonstrated using appropriate evaluation metrics (FID, Wasserstein-2).

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Dependency on pre-trained models:** The approach relies on the availability of suitable pre-trained LVMs. While transfer learning is common, the performance may be limited by the quality of the pre-trained model. It would be beneficial to show the method is robust to different LVM architectures or performance of the LVM.
    *   **Limited theoretical analysis:** While the proposition of upper bound is included, it would be strengthened by further analysis, perhaps exploring the properties of the latent space and its relationship to the flow matching process.
    *   **Parameter tuning:** There is some discussion of hyperparameter settings for Latent-CFM, but a more thorough sensitivity analysis would be valuable.

*   **Potential Impact:** Latent-CFM has the potential to impact generative modeling by making flow matching more applicable and efficient for a broader range of datasets, especially in scientific domains. Its ability to generate physically plausible data and offer interpretable latent representations could significantly benefit scientific applications.

**Overall:**

The paper presents a solid contribution to the field of generative modeling. Latent-CFM offers a practical and effective approach to improve flow matching by explicitly modeling data structure, addressing a key limitation of existing methods. The experimental results are convincing, and the potential impact is significant, particularly for scientific applications. However, the reliance on pre-trained LVMs is a potential constraint that warrants further exploration.

### Defining and Quantifying Creative Behavior in Popular Image Generators
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04497v2)
- **Authors**: Aditi Ramaswamy, Hana Chockler, Melane Navaratnarajah
- **Abstract**: Creativity of generative AI models has been a subject of scientific debate in the last years, without a conclusive answer. In this paper, we study creativity from a practical perspective and introduce quantitative measures that help the user to choose a suitable AI model for a given task. We evaluated our measures on a number of popular image-to-image generation models, and the results of this suggest that our measures conform to human intuition.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper proposes quantitative measures for evaluating task-oriented creativity in generative vision models, specifically `img2img` models. The authors argue that existing work on creativity in AI often focuses on large language models or `text2img` generation, leaving a gap in understanding `img2img` creativity. They introduce metrics for assessing *satisfaction of prompt requirements*, *cohesion*, and *diversity* in generated images, based on artifact spaces and the concepts of novelty, usefulness, and typicality. They implement these measures using an iterative chain process, inspired by the "telephone game", where outputs are fed back as inputs to the model. They apply these measures to popular `img2img` models like Stable Diffusion 3, Kandinsky 2.2, and Flux, varying the input temperature (strength) to analyze its impact.  The study reveals that models rely heavily on textual descriptions for coherent creative image synthesis and a higher input temperature (strength) has a measurable effect on the creativity factors.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper presents a *moderately* novel approach. While the individual components of the evaluation (artifact detection, similarity measures, temperature variation) aren't entirely new, their combination into a chain-based quantitative evaluation focused specifically on `img2img` models does offer a fresh perspective. The iterative chain approach is somewhat inspired, but also quite basic (reminiscent of genetic algorithms), and its value lies in how it's used to measure the defined criteria. Addressing `img2img` model creativity which they identify as an under-researched area contributes to the field, but it is not a groundbreaking shift.

* **Significance:** The significance is *potentially medium*. Quantifying creativity, even task-oriented creativity, is a challenging and valuable goal. If the proposed metrics prove to be robust and generalize well, they could provide a useful tool for developers and users of generative models.  The ability to objectively compare models along these dimensions is a positive step. The analysis regarding the effect of input temperature is also valuable, providing empirical data to support or refute prevailing hypotheses. However, the reliance on existing object detection and word similarity tools introduces a vulnerability to their errors and biases, limiting the impact of the results, which may be sensitive to the quality of these tools.

* **Strengths:**
    * Clear problem definition and motivation.
    * Well-defined metrics grounded in existing creativity literature.
    * Empirical evaluation of popular models.
    * Analysis of the impact of input temperature.
    * Open-source code and data contribute to reproducibility.
    * Iterative approach creates a unique perspective.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * Dependence on object detection and word similarity calculation tools. The metrics are only as good as the performance of these external components, making the framework sensitive to errors in identifying concepts.
    * Dataset is too simple. Restricting seed images to single subject limits the evaluation.
    * Limited scope of the chain process. While inspired, the process is very basic, and may not be representative of all image generation iterative processes.
    * The creativity scores are quite low across the board, calling into question the effectiveness of the proposed metrics in capturing creative output.
    * The concept of measuring "creativity" is inherently subjective, which means that the proposed mathematical interpretation is only a proxy for this phenomenon. This can be seen as a weakness, or as an inevitable outcome of choosing to interpret subjectivity in a quantifiable way.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper could influence future research by:
    * Encouraging more work on quantitative creativity evaluation.
    * Promoting the development of `img2img`-specific evaluation methods.
    * Providing a baseline for comparing future models and techniques.
    * It can encourage future work to be more thorough by building upon their iterative approach.

* **Conclusion:**  While the paper has strengths in its problem definition and empirical evaluation, its limitations, particularly the reliance on existing object detection tools and a simplified dataset, limit its overall impact. The novelty is incremental rather than revolutionary.

### Pangu Ultra MoE: How to Train Your Big MoE on Ascend NPUs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04519v1)
- **Authors**: Yehui Tang, Yichun Yin, Yaoyuan Wang, Hang Zhou, Yu Pan, Wei Guo, Ziyang Zhang, Miao Rang, Fangcheng Liu, Naifu Zhang, Binghan Li, Yonghan Dong, Xiaojun Meng, Yasheng Wang, Dong Li, Yin Li, Dandan Tu, Can Chen, Youliang Yan, Fisher Yu, Ruiming Tang, Yunhe Wang, Botian Huang, Bo Wang, Boxiao Liu, Changzheng Zhang, Da Kuang, Fei Liu, Gang Huang, Jiansheng Wei, Jiarui Qin, Jie Ran, Jinpeng Li, Jun Zhao, Liang Dai, Lin Li, Liqun Deng, Peifeng Qin, Pengyuan Zeng, Qiang Gu, Shaohua Tang, Shengjun Cheng, Tao Gao, Tao Yu, Tianshu Li, Tianyu Bi, Wei He, Weikai Mao, Wenyong Huang, Wulong Liu, Xiabing Li, Xianzhi Yu, Xueyu Wu, Xu He, Yangkai Du, Yan Xu, Ye Tian, Yimeng Wu, Yongbing Huang, Yong Tian, Yong Zhu, Yue Li, Yufei Wang, Yuhang Gai, Yujun Li, Yu Luo, Yunsheng Ni, Yusen Sun, Zelin Chen, Zhe Liu, Zhicheng Liu, Zhipeng Tu, Zilin Ding, Zongyuan Zhan
- **Abstract**: Sparse large language models (LLMs) with Mixture of Experts (MoE) and close to a trillion parameters are dominating the realm of most capable language models. However, the massive model scale poses significant challenges for the underlying software and hardware systems. In this paper, we aim to uncover a recipe to harness such scale on Ascend NPUs. The key goals are better usage of the computing resources under the dynamic sparse model structures and materializing the expected performance gain on the actual hardware. To select model configurations suitable for Ascend NPUs without repeatedly running the expensive experiments, we leverage simulation to compare the trade-off of various model hyperparameters. This study led to Pangu Ultra MoE, a sparse LLM with 718 billion parameters, and we conducted experiments on the model to verify the simulation results. On the system side, we dig into Expert Parallelism to optimize the communication between NPU devices to reduce the synchronization overhead. We also optimize the memory efficiency within the devices to further reduce the parameter and activation management overhead. In the end, we achieve an MFU of 30.0% when training Pangu Ultra MoE, with performance comparable to that of DeepSeek R1, on 6K Ascend NPUs, and demonstrate that the Ascend system is capable of harnessing all the training stages of the state-of-the-art language models. Extensive experiments indicate that our recipe can lead to efficient training of large-scale sparse language models with MoE. We also study the behaviors of such models for future reference.
- **Summary**: This paper presents Pangu Ultra MoE, a sparse large language model with 718 billion parameters, trained on Ascend NPUs. The work focuses on optimizing the training process for such large MoE models on the Ascend hardware. The paper describes a two-pronged approach: (1) model architecture design via simulation to identify efficient configurations for Ascend NPUs and (2) system optimization, focusing on expert parallelism, memory management (fine-grained recomputation and tensor swapping), and load balancing. The authors achieve an MFU of 30.0% when training Pangu Ultra MoE on 6K Ascend NPUs, and compare its performance to DeepSeek R1. They also investigate expert specialization, router scale and expert co-activation patterns.

**Evaluation:**

The paper has several strengths. First, the comprehensive exploration of MoE model architecture suitable for Ascend NPUs through simulation is valuable. While architecture search methods are common, their application to MoE models on specific hardware is less explored. Second, the detailed system-level optimizations, including hierarchical EP All-to-All communication, adaptive pipe overlap, and fine-grained recomputation, directly address the challenges of training large sparse models on Ascend NPUs. Third, the achievement of a 30.0% MFU is a significant accomplishment, demonstrating the capability of the Ascend system. Finally, the MoE analysis reveals interesting behaviors and provides insights for further research. The comparison to existing models including Qwen and Deepseek provides solid empirical context.

However, the paper also has some weaknesses. The major weakness is the limited novelty. Many of the techniques, like model parallelization strategies, communication optimization, and memory saving techniques, are not particularly new in themselves. The novelty lies primarily in *how they are applied and tuned* for Ascend NPUs specifically in the context of a large MoE model. This makes the paper more of an engineering contribution than a theoretical breakthrough. More direct comparison with existing model parallelism implementations would be beneficial. The lack of comparison of the model's performance to similar models trained on alternative hardware (e.g. GPUs) makes it difficult to fully assess the Ascend-specific optimizations. It is difficult to isolate the architectural and systems contributions. The analysis of the MoE structure could have been more insightful with clearer connection to the performance benefits. Finally, the "Pangu Ultra MoE" doesn't appear to actually be open-source for the community to use, making replicability impossible.

Overall, the paper presents a valuable engineering effort to train a large MoE model on Ascend NPUs. The simulation-driven approach and detailed system optimizations are the most valuable contributions. The performance gains are noteworthy, but limited novelty and lack of code release make it a less impactful contribution to the broader field.

### Comparative Analysis of Carbon Footprint in Manual vs. LLM-Assisted Code Development
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04521v1)
- **Authors**: Kuen Sum Cheung, Mayuri Kaul, Gunel Jahangirova, Mohammad Reza Mousavi, Eric Zie
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLM) have significantly transformed various domains, including software development. These models assist programmers in generating code, potentially increasing productivity and efficiency. However, the environmental impact of utilising these AI models is substantial, given their high energy consumption during both training and inference stages. This research aims to compare the energy consumption of manual software development versus an LLM-assisted approach, using Codeforces as a simulation platform for software development. The goal is to quantify the environmental impact and propose strategies for minimising the carbon footprint of using LLM in software development. Our results show that the LLM-assisted code generation leads on average to 32.72 higher carbon footprint than the manual one. Moreover, there is a significant correlation between task complexity and the difference in the carbon footprint of the two approaches.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the carbon footprint of software development, comparing a manual coding approach with an LLM-assisted approach using Codeforces as a platform. The study aims to quantify the environmental impact of using LLMs like GPT-4 in coding tasks and propose strategies for minimizing the carbon footprint. The results indicate that LLM-assisted code generation leads to a significantly higher carbon footprint (on average, 32.72 times higher) than manual coding. The authors also found a significant correlation between task complexity (as defined by Codeforces problem difficulty) and the carbon footprint difference between the two approaches. They suggest optimizing LLM queries and using more energy-efficient models to reduce the environmental impact of LLM-assisted development.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper addresses a timely and increasingly relevant topic: the environmental impact of AI, specifically LLMs, in software development. While there have been prior studies on the carbon footprint of LLMs, this paper distinguishes itself by focusing on the *comparative* carbon footprint of LLM-assisted *versus* manual coding *within a structured programming task context.* It goes beyond simply measuring the emissions of LLMs and directly contrasts their usage against traditional methods. This is a relatively novel contribution, as many previous studies examined the sustainability of generated code or the end-to-end footprint of LLM training, but not the actual coding process.

*   **Significance:** The findings highlight a crucial trade-off: while LLMs promise increased productivity and potential automation, they currently come with a significant environmental cost. The quantification of this trade-off is valuable for researchers, software developers, and organizations that need to make informed decisions about adopting LLM-assisted tools. Demonstrating a correlation between task complexity and the increased carbon footprint is significant, suggesting that LLMs might be less environmentally justifiable for complex tasks, thereby offering concrete areas where future development could prioritize optimization strategies.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Quantitative Analysis:** The paper provides a clear and well-defined methodology for measuring and comparing carbon footprints, using formulas and providing data on power consumption, carbon intensity, and time spent on various tasks.
    *   **Codeforces Platform:** Using Codeforces provides access to a large dataset of submissions, enabling a statistically sound comparison.
    *   **Transparency and Replicability:** The authors provide their code and experimental data, promoting transparency and enabling others to replicate or extend their work.
    *   **Clearly Defined Research Questions and Objectives:** The study has focused research questions with well-defined objectives, making the scope of research clear.
    *   **Addresses Identified Research Gap:** The paper makes a contribution to filling a previously identified gap in understanding the environmental impact of LLM in comparison to manual coding.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scope of Activities:** The study focuses primarily on coding, debugging, and testing, neglecting other phases of the software development lifecycle (e.g., design, requirements gathering). Although justified by data availability, this limits the generalizability of the results.
    *   **Assumptions and Estimations:** Several aspects of the analysis rely on estimations and assumptions (e.g., power consumption of laptops, percentage of time spent on debugging, lifetime number of LLM queries), which introduce uncertainty. While the authors acknowledge these limitations, the accuracy of the results depends heavily on the validity of these assumptions.
    *   **Single LLM and Task Domain:** The study is limited to GPT-4 and competitive programming tasks.  The results may not generalize to other LLMs or different types of software development projects (e.g., web development, data science). Other LLMs may provide a different footprint and GPT4 might not be optimized for the competitive programming domain in question.
    *   **Simplified Prompting:** The paper uses basic zero-shot prompting. Advanced prompting techniques or fine-tuning the LLM for specific tasks could potentially improve its efficiency and reduce its carbon footprint.
    *   **Geographical Specificity:** The carbon intensity value used is based on the UK average. Given that LLM inference can be executed in various global locations, the results may vary considerably depending on the electricity grid's carbon intensity at the time.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper can stimulate further research in several directions:
    *   Investigating the carbon footprint of different LLMs and prompting strategies.
    *   Developing energy-efficient AI models specifically for software development.
    *   Exploring methods for reducing the carbon footprint of individual steps in the LLM process (e.g., reducing query size, optimizing inference).
    *   Developing tools and methodologies for measuring and tracking the carbon footprint of software projects that utilize LLMs.

**Rationale for Score:**

The paper addresses a highly relevant and timely issue, provides a clear quantitative analysis with useful comparisons between the environmental costs of traditional methods of coding and LLM based processes, and has replicability due to the release of experimental data and code. Although the assumptions and estimations could be improved and the scope of activities and the LLM used for the study are very limited, I believe this paper serves as a key component of a growing body of research that seeks to investigate and understand the sustainability of the software development process, specifically with reference to LLMs.

### Text2CT: Towards 3D CT Volume Generation from Free-text Descriptions Using Diffusion Model
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04522v1)
- **Authors**: Pengfei Guo, Can Zhao, Dong Yang, Yufan He, Vishwesh Nath, Ziyue Xu, Pedro R. A. S. Bassi, Zongwei Zhou, Benjamin D. Simon, Stephanie Anne Harmon, Baris Turkbey, Daguang Xu
- **Abstract**: Generating 3D CT volumes from descriptive free-text inputs presents a transformative opportunity in diagnostics and research. In this paper, we introduce Text2CT, a novel approach for synthesizing 3D CT volumes from textual descriptions using the diffusion model. Unlike previous methods that rely on fixed-format text input, Text2CT employs a novel prompt formulation that enables generation from diverse, free-text descriptions. The proposed framework encodes medical text into latent representations and decodes them into high-resolution 3D CT scans, effectively bridging the gap between semantic text inputs and detailed volumetric representations in a unified 3D framework. Our method demonstrates superior performance in preserving anatomical fidelity and capturing intricate structures as described in the input text. Extensive evaluations show that our approach achieves state-of-the-art results, offering promising potential applications in diagnostics, and data augmentation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the Text2CT paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Text2CT, a novel 3D text-conditional generative model that synthesizes high-resolution CT volumes from free-text descriptions. Unlike previous methods that rely on structured text inputs, Text2CT can handle diverse and unstructured textual prompts.  It uses a 3D compression network (adopted from MAISI) to manage memory demands and a text-conditional latent diffusion model (LDM) to generate the 3D volumes in a compact latent space. A key contribution is a new text prompt formulation, leveraging large language models (LLMs) to augment and diversify radiology reports into various text inputs, ultimately improving model generalizability and applicability in real-world clinical scenarios. Experimental results demonstrate Text2CT's superior performance compared to existing methods in terms of image quality, anatomical realism, and text alignment. The paper also investigates the use of synthetic data generated by Text2CT for data augmentation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in its ability to generate high-resolution 3D CT volumes from free-text descriptions using a unified 3D framework. Existing methods are either limited to fixed-format text inputs, lower resolution outputs or rely on hybrid 2D/3D architectures. The use of LLMs to augment the prompts and address the diversity of input texts found in real-world clinical scenarios is also a significant contribution.

*   **Significance:** The ability to generate realistic 3D CT volumes from text has significant potential for medical diagnostics, data augmentation, and personalized treatment planning. The method's adaptability to diverse textual descriptions makes it more clinically relevant than previous approaches. The data augmentation experiments also provide evidence of the model's potential to address data scarcity issues. The results show improvements in a downstream classification task.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   The model tackles a significant challenge in medical imaging: generating realistic 3D volumes from text.
    *   The use of a 3D compression network effectively addresses memory constraints.
    *   The LLM-based prompt augmentation strategy enhances the model's flexibility and real-world applicability.
    *   The experimental results demonstrate superior performance compared to existing methods on several metrics.
    *   Human expert evaluations validate the clinical relevance of the generated volumes.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The model performance depends on the quality of the input text. The limitations section itself makes mention of issues related to dependence on text quality. While they tried to tackle prompt diversity, the quality of the text prompt can still be a bottleneck.
    *   The high computational demands may restrict its use in resource-constrained settings.
    *   While the paper shows the utility of synthetic data for augmentation, there is room for more in-depth analysis of the impact of data biases.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in medical image synthesis, data augmentation, and personalized medicine.  The approach could lead to the development of more sophisticated tools for medical diagnostics and treatment planning. Also, the idea of using LLMs to augment input text prompts might also push more research into leveraging LLMs to improve deep learning model input.

**Justification for Score:**

Text2CT is a significant step forward in the field of text-conditional medical image generation. The model addresses key limitations of existing methods and demonstrates superior performance on several metrics. The integration of LLMs for prompt augmentation is a clever approach that enhances the model's practicality. However, the computational demands and reliance on text quality are limitations that need to be addressed in future work. The potential influence on medical imaging and data augmentation is strong.
