### Fight Fire with Fire: Defending Against Malicious RL Fine-Tuning via Reward Neutralization
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04578v1)
- **Authors**: Wenjun Cao
- **Abstract**: Reinforcement learning (RL) fine-tuning transforms large language models while creating a vulnerability we experimentally verify: Our experiment shows that malicious RL fine-tuning dismantles safety guardrails with remarkable efficiency, requiring only 50 steps and minimal adversarial prompts, with harmful escalating from 0-2 to 7-9. This attack vector particularly threatens open-source models with parameter-level access. Existing defenses targeting supervised fine-tuning prove ineffective against RL's dynamic feedback mechanisms. We introduce Reward Neutralization, the first defense framework specifically designed against RL fine-tuning attacks, establishing concise rejection patterns that render malicious reward signals ineffective. Our approach trains models to produce minimal-information rejections that attackers cannot exploit, systematically neutralizing attempts to optimize toward harmful outputs. Experiments validate that our approach maintains low harmful scores (no greater than 2) after 200 attack steps, while standard models rapidly deteriorate. This work provides the first constructive proof that robust defense against increasingly accessible RL attacks is achievable, addressing a critical security gap for open-weight models.
- **Summary**: Okay, I've reviewed the paper. Here's a summary and a critical evaluation:

**Summary:**

The paper "Fight Fire with Fire: Defending Against Malicious RL Fine-Tuning via Reward Neutralization" addresses the vulnerability of large language models (LLMs) to malicious reinforcement learning (RL) fine-tuning attacks. It demonstrates that RL fine-tuning can efficiently dismantle safety guardrails in LLMs, requiring minimal steps and adversarial prompts. The paper argues that existing defenses designed for supervised fine-tuning are ineffective against RL's dynamic feedback mechanisms. To counter this, the authors introduce "Reward Neutralization," a novel defense framework specifically designed to establish rejection patterns that render malicious reward signals ineffective. The approach trains models to produce minimal-information rejections, preventing attackers from exploiting reward differentials.  Experimental results validate that this approach maintains low harmful scores even after extensive RL attacks, contrasting sharply with the rapid degradation of standard models.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The primary novelty of this paper lies in:

1.  **Identifying the Vulnerability:** Explicitly highlighting and demonstrating the effectiveness of RL fine-tuning as an attack vector against LLM safety, especially within the context of open-source models where parameter access is readily available. While the concept of adversarial fine-tuning exists, focusing on RL as a specific and powerful attack vector on safety alignment is a contribution.

2.  **Developing Reward Neutralization:**  Proposing a novel defense strategy (Reward Neutralization) tailored to counteract RL-based attacks. This is a departure from existing defenses primarily targeting supervised fine-tuning. Targeting the reward signal itself, instead of individual harmful outputs or input manipulation, is a key innovative aspect.

3.  **Empirical Validation:** Providing substantial experimental evidence demonstrating the effectiveness of Reward Neutralization across different model architectures and harm domains. The comparison against standard models clearly illustrates the benefits of the proposed defense.

**Significance:**

The paper has several aspects of significance:

1.  **Addressing a Critical Security Gap:** The research directly addresses a critical security vulnerability in LLMs, particularly in the context of open-source models where malicious actors have greater control over model parameters.  This has important real-world implications as more LLMs are open-sourced.

2.  **Practical Defense Framework:** The proposed Reward Neutralization approach offers a practical and potentially scalable method for defending against RL-based attacks.  The paper provides a clear description of the implementation and training process, increasing its likelihood of adoption and further development by other researchers.

3.  **Informing Future Research:** This work could significantly influence future research directions in LLM security and safety alignment.  It highlights the need for defenses that are specifically tailored to the dynamic and adaptive nature of RL-based attacks, moving beyond static dataset-based defenses.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Problem Definition:**  The paper clearly articulates the problem of RL fine-tuning attacks and the limitations of existing defenses.
*   **Well-Defined Solution:** The Reward Neutralization approach is clearly defined and well-motivated.
*   **Thorough Experiments:** The experimental evaluation is comprehensive, covering different model architectures, harm domains, and attack scenarios.
*   **Convincing Results:**  The results convincingly demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed defense, showcasing a significant improvement in robustness compared to standard models.
*   **Reproducibility:** Provides details needed for reproducibility

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Scope:** The evaluation focuses on specific harm domains (biochemical hazards and cybercrime).  While these are relevant, it would be beneficial to explore the effectiveness of Reward Neutralization across a broader range of harmful behaviors.
*   **Scalability Concerns:** While the experimental setup is well-defined, the paper lacks a discussion on the scalability of the approach to very large LLMs. Training RL agents, even for defense, can be computationally intensive and there may be practical limitations.
*   **Limited Analysis of Attack Adaptation:** The paper focuses on a fixed attack strategy.  Further investigation into how attackers might adapt their strategies to circumvent Reward Neutralization is warranted. The approach could potentially be susceptible to adversarial reward design, where attackers carefully craft reward functions that exploit subtle weaknesses in the rejection patterns.
*   **Reliance on GRPO:** While the authors mention that the approach generalizes to other RL methods, the experiments primarily rely on GRPO. Expanding the evaluation to include other RL algorithms would strengthen the claim of generality.
* The table showing responses after RL finetuning seems to indicate that the responses are somewhat nonsensical ("As a responsible Bulletin Premiere Saved"). Although this may be safer, it indicates there is probably some degradation to overall LLM function.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a significant contribution to the field of LLM security by identifying a critical vulnerability and proposing a novel defense framework. The experimental results are compelling, and the approach offers a practical solution for safeguarding open-weight models. However, the limitations related to scope, scalability, attack adaptation, and the limitations on the LLM performance prevent it from achieving a higher score.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### SlideItRight: Using AI to Find Relevant Slides and Provide Feedback for Open-Ended Questions
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04584v1)
- **Authors**: Chloe Qianhui Zhao, Jie Cao, Eason Chen, Kenneth R. Koedinger, Jionghao Lin
- **Abstract**: Feedback is important in supporting student learning. While various automated feedback systems have been implemented to make the feedback scalable, many existing solutions only focus on generating text-based feedback. As is indicated in the multimedia learning principle, learning with more modalities could help utilize more separate channels, reduce the cognitive load and facilitate students' learning. Hence, it is important to explore the potential of Artificial Intelligence (AI) in feedback generation from and to different modalities. Our study leverages Large Language Models (LLMs) for textual feedback with the supplementary guidance from other modality - relevant lecture slide retrieved from the slides hub. Through an online crowdsourcing study (N=91), this study investigates learning gains and student perceptions using a 2x2 design (i.e., human feedback vs. AI feedback and with vs. without relevant slide), evaluating the clarity, engagement, perceived effectiveness, and reliability) of AI-facilitated multimodal feedback. We observed significant pre-to-post learning gains across all conditions. However, the differences in these gains were not statistically significant between conditions. The post-survey revealed that students found the slide feedback helpful in their learning process, though they reported difficulty in understanding it. Regarding the AI-generated open-ended feedback, students considered it personalized and relevant to their responses, but they expressed lower trust in the AI feedback compared to human-generated feedback.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "SlideItRight: Using AI to Find Relevant Slides and Provide Feedback for Open-Ended Questions" investigates the use of Large Language Models (LLMs) to generate textual feedback for student responses, supplemented by relevant lecture slides retrieved from a slide repository. The study uses a 2x2 design (human vs. AI feedback, with and without relevant slides) in a crowdsourcing setting to assess learning gains and student perceptions. While the study finds significant pre-to-post learning gains across all conditions, there are no statistically significant differences between conditions. Students find the slide feedback helpful but also reported difficulty in understanding it. They considered AI-generated feedback personalized and relevant but had less trust in it compared to human-generated feedback.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Relevant Problem:** The paper tackles a relevant problem in online learning: providing scalable and effective feedback, moving beyond purely text-based methods to incorporate multimedia elements.
    *   **Multimodal Approach:** Leveraging both text (LLM-generated feedback) and visuals (relevant slides) is a good application of the multimedia learning principle and seeks to reduce cognitive overload.
    *   **Rigorous Experiment Design:** The 2x2 factorial design and the use of pre- and post-tests with Likert scale surveys provides a well-defined framework for assessing the impact of different feedback modalities. The IRB approval confirms ethical considerations.
    *   **Practical Implementation:**  The creation of the SlideItRight system, which is an automated feedback system that integrates AI-generated textual feedback with retrieved relevant lecture slides to enhance learning support.
    *   **Insights into Student Perceptions:** The study provides valuable insights into how students perceive AI-generated feedback (personalization but lower trust) and slide-based feedback (helpfulness but comprehension issues).

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Lack of Significant Results:** The core weakness is the absence of statistically significant differences in learning gains between the experimental conditions. While the trend points to a positive impact of combining AI and slides, this needs to be confirmed with a larger sample size.
    *   **Limited Generalizability:** The study uses a crowdsourcing platform (Prolific) and is based on a single-domain context (instructional design principles). This restricts the generalizability of the findings to more diverse populations and learning settings.
    *   **Cognitive Load Concerns:** The paper acknowledges the potential for cognitive overload, particularly with the combined AI and slide feedback. However, there is no direct measurement of cognitive load in the study, which would have strengthened the analysis.
    *   **Potential for bias:** The recruitment from Prolific and the fixed compensation could introduce biases related to motivation and engagement, impacting the depth of participants' interaction with the feedback.
    *   **Lack of comparison of feedback vs control group:** It is unknown whether all feedback systems (AI, slide, human) are compared to a baseline.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**

    *   **Incremental Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in the integration of LLM-generated feedback with retrieval-augmented slide information within an online learning context. RAG is also a well-defined concept that is implemented. It builds upon existing research on AI-driven feedback generation and multimodal learning. However, the lack of significant improvement over human or basic AI feedback diminishes its groundbreaking impact. While the implementation itself is a valuable contribution, the study does not demonstrate a substantial advantage over existing approaches.
    *   **Modest Significance:** The paper is significant because it highlights the potential and challenges of AI-augmented learning. It provides useful guidance for future research by demonstrating the importance of considering student perceptions, managing cognitive load, and establishing trust when designing AI-based feedback systems.  The negative results also provide valuable insights for researchers in the field.
    *   **Limited Impact:** Given the lack of statistically significant improvement in learning gains, the study's immediate impact on educational practice is limited. However, its findings could inform the development of more effective AI-based feedback systems if the issues highlighted in the study are addressed.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a well-designed study investigating an important problem in online learning. The use of LLMs for feedback generation with retrieval-augmented lecture slides is a promising approach. However, the lack of statistically significant results limits the immediate impact of the study. While the insights into student perceptions are valuable, the overall contribution is incremental rather than transformative. There is also a generalizability issue due to the study design.

Score: 6

*Reasoning:*
* **Meets expectations:** It is not a groundbreaking study with exceptional novelty or transformative impact, nor is it flawed, poorly designed, or irrelevant to the field. A score of 6.5 reflects that the paper addresses a relevant problem, provides useful insights, but needs further development and validation before it can have a substantial impact on educational practice or research.
- **Classification**: cs.HC
- **Score**: 6/10

### ZeroSearch: Incentivize the Search Capability of LLMs without Searching
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04588v1)
- **Authors**: Hao Sun, Zile Qiao, Jiayan Guo, Xuanbo Fan, Yingyan Hou, Yong Jiang, Pengjun Xie, Fei Huang, Yan Zhang
- **Abstract**: Effective information searching is essential for enhancing the reasoning and generation capabilities of large language models (LLMs). Recent research has explored using reinforcement learning (RL) to improve LLMs' search capabilities by interacting with live search engines in real-world environments. While these approaches show promising results, they face two major challenges: (1) Uncontrolled Document Quality: The quality of documents returned by search engines is often unpredictable, introducing noise and instability into the training process. (2) Prohibitively High API Costs: RL training requires frequent rollouts, potentially involving hundreds of thousands of search requests, which incur substantial API expenses and severely constrain scalability. To address these challenges, we introduce ZeroSearch, a reinforcement learning framework that incentivizes the search capabilities of LLMs without interacting with real search engines. Our approach begins with lightweight supervised fine-tuning to transform the LLM into a retrieval module capable of generating both relevant and noisy documents in response to a query. During RL training, we employ a curriculum-based rollout strategy that incrementally degrades the quality of generated documents, progressively eliciting the model's reasoning ability by exposing it to increasingly challenging retrieval scenarios. Extensive experiments demonstrate that ZeroSearch effectively incentivizes the search capabilities of LLMs using a 3B LLM as the retrieval module. Remarkably, a 7B retrieval module achieves comparable performance to the real search engine, while a 14B retrieval module even surpasses it. Furthermore, it generalizes well across both base and instruction-tuned models of various parameter sizes and is compatible with a wide range of RL algorithms.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the ZEROSEARCH paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ZEROSEARCH, a novel reinforcement learning (RL) framework designed to improve the search capabilities of large language models (LLMs) without requiring interaction with real-world search engines.  The framework tackles the challenges of uncontrolled document quality and high API costs typically associated with RL training on live search engines. ZEROSEARCH leverages a lightweight supervised fine-tuning (SFT) stage to transform an LLM into a retrieval module capable of generating both relevant and noisy documents. A curriculum-based rollout strategy is then employed during RL training, incrementally degrading the quality of the generated documents to progressively challenge the model's reasoning ability.  Experiments demonstrate that ZEROSEARCH effectively enhances the search capabilities of LLMs, even using smaller models (3B) as retrieval modules, and scaling up to performance comparable to or even surpassing real search engines. The framework is also shown to be generalizable across different model architectures (base vs. instruction-tuned) and compatible with various RL algorithms.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of simulating a search engine using an LLM is a clever way to circumvent the limitations of real-world search engine interaction. The use of curriculum learning to progressively introduce noise into the retrieval process is a valuable contribution. While the individual components (SFT, RL, curriculum learning) are not entirely novel, their combination within the ZEROSEARCH framework, specifically tailored to the problem of LLM search, constitutes a significant advance.
*   **Significance:**  The paper addresses a critical issue in LLM research: how to equip models with the ability to access and reason with external information effectively and efficiently. ZEROSEARCH tackles the major bottlenecks of cost and instability associated with existing RL-based search approaches.  The performance results are compelling, demonstrating that the simulated search engine can achieve comparable or even superior performance to real search engines.  This has the potential to democratize research in this area by removing the barrier of high API costs. The generalizability across different model types and RL algorithms further enhances its value.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Addresses a practical problem:**  The high cost and variability of real-world search APIs pose a significant barrier to research. ZEROSEARCH offers a viable and scalable solution.
    *   **Well-designed framework:** The combination of SFT and curriculum learning is thoughtfully designed to train robust search capabilities.
    *   **Strong empirical results:** The experiments are thorough and demonstrate consistent improvements over baseline methods.
    *   **Generalizability:**  The framework works with different model architectures and RL algorithms.
    *   **Potential for impact:** Could significantly lower the barrier to entry for research on LLMs with search capabilities.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Dependency on GPU resources:** While cheaper than API calls, deploying the simulation LLM still requires substantial GPU resources, which is a limitation for some researchers. The paper acknowledges this.
    *   **Complexity:** The framework involves multiple stages (SFT, RL, curriculum learning), which adds to the complexity of implementation and tuning.
    *   **Evaluation metrics:** While EM is a standard metric, a more nuanced evaluation of the quality of the retrieved documents and their impact on the final answer would strengthen the paper.
    *   **Limited ablation studies:** More ablation studies on the different components of the framework (e.g., the specific reward function) would provide further insights.
*   **Potential Influence:**  ZEROSEARCH has the potential to become a widely adopted framework for training LLMs with search capabilities due to its scalability, cost-effectiveness, and generalizability. It could spur further research into simulated environments for RL training.

**Justification of Score:**

I am assigning a score of **8**. The paper presents a novel and well-designed framework that tackles a significant problem in the field. The empirical results are compelling, and the potential for impact is high. While there are some limitations, the strengths of the paper outweigh the weaknesses. The work makes a substantial contribution to the area of LLMs with search capabilities, offering a practical and scalable alternative to existing methods.
Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### MonoCoP: Chain-of-Prediction for Monocular 3D Object Detection
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04594v2)
- **Authors**: Zhihao Zhang, Abhinav Kumar, Girish Chandar Ganesan, Xiaoming Liu
- **Abstract**: Accurately predicting 3D attributes is crucial for monocular 3D object detection (Mono3D), with depth estimation posing the greatest challenge due to the inherent ambiguity in mapping 2D images to 3D space. While existing methods leverage multiple depth cues (e.g., estimating depth uncertainty, modeling depth error) to improve depth accuracy, they overlook that accurate depth prediction requires conditioning on other 3D attributes, as these attributes are intrinsically inter-correlated through the 3D to 2D projection, which ultimately limits overall accuracy and stability. Inspired by Chain-of-Thought (CoT) in large language models (LLMs), this paper proposes MonoCoP, which leverages a Chain-of-Prediction (CoP) to predict attributes sequentially and conditionally via three key designs. First, it employs a lightweight AttributeNet (AN) for each 3D attribute to learn attribute-specific features. Next, MonoCoP constructs an explicit chain to propagate these learned features from one attribute to the next. Finally, MonoCoP uses a residual connection to aggregate features for each attribute along the chain, ensuring that later attribute predictions are conditioned on all previously processed attributes without forgetting the features of earlier ones. Experimental results show that our MonoCoP achieves state-of-the-art (SoTA) performance on the KITTI leaderboard without requiring additional data and further surpasses existing methods on the Waymo and nuScenes frontal datasets.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MonoCoP, a novel monocular 3D object detection method. Recognizing that 3D attributes (size, angle, depth) are inter-correlated, the authors propose a Chain-of-Prediction (CoP) approach. MonoCoP predicts these attributes sequentially and conditionally, using three key designs: AttributeNet (AN) to learn attribute-specific features, an explicit chain to propagate these features, and residual connections for feature aggregation. The method achieves state-of-the-art performance on KITTI, Waymo, and nuScenes datasets, particularly improving depth estimation for distant objects. The authors argue that their sequential and conditional prediction approach mitigates inaccuracies and instabilities arising from the inter-correlations of 3D attributes, outperforming parallel prediction methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of using a chain-of-prediction for monocular 3D object detection, inspired by the Chain-of-Thought concept from LLMs, is relatively novel. Existing methods typically predict 3D attributes in parallel. The specific designs of AttributeNet, feature propagation chain, and residual aggregation are also reasonable and demonstrate clear design choices. However, the basic building blocks such as MLP and residual connection are standard, which reduces the novelty score.

*   **Significance:** The problem of depth estimation in monocular 3D detection is well-established, and improving its accuracy is significant for applications like autonomous driving. The paper provides a clear rationale for why sequential prediction should be better than parallel prediction, which is supported by the quantitative and qualitative results. Achieving state-of-the-art results on multiple datasets is a good indication of the method's effectiveness. However, the paper doesn't address the computational overhead of the sequential prediction compared to parallel methods, which could be a limitation. The paper also lacks some mathematical rigor on the model formulation of attribute nets, the connection of sequential models, and feature aggregation.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Clear problem statement and motivation.
    *   Well-designed CoP architecture with understandable components.
    *   State-of-the-art results on multiple benchmark datasets.
    *   Ablation studies demonstrating the importance of each component.
    *   Qualitative results illustrating the improvement in depth estimation.
    *   Discussion of inter-correlation of 3D attributes with supporting analysis.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   Incremental novelty in some designs (e.g., the AttributeNet is based on common MLPs).
    *   Lack of computational complexity analysis (runtime, memory).
    *   Limited discussion of failure cases or limitations of the approach.
    *   The inspiration from LLMs is somewhat superficial. The CoP in this paper is a fixed architecture, unlike the dynamic reasoning in LLMs.
    *   Weak model description of sequential feature propagation and aggregation.
    *   Lack of mathematical rigor on the model formulation

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in monocular 3D object detection by highlighting the importance of considering inter-correlations between attributes and exploring sequential prediction approaches. It provides a strong baseline and motivates further investigation into similar techniques.

**Score: 6.5**

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a solid contribution to monocular 3D object detection. The core idea of the Chain-of-Prediction is novel, and the experiments convincingly demonstrate its effectiveness. The method achieves state-of-the-art results, demonstrating its potential impact on the field. However, the novelty of the individual components is incremental, and the paper lacks a computational complexity analysis. It also lacks some mathematical rigor and discussion on potential failure cases. Therefore, while the paper is significant, the score is 6.5 due to the limitations outlined above.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### OmniGIRL: A Multilingual and Multimodal Benchmark for GitHub Issue Resolution
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04606v1)
- **Authors**: Lianghong Guo, Wei Tao, Runhan Jiang, Yanlin Wang, Jiachi Chen, Xilin Liu, Yuchi Ma, Mingzhi Mao, Hongyu Zhang, Zibin Zheng
- **Abstract**: The GitHub issue resolution task aims to resolve issues reported in repositories automatically. With advances in large language models (LLMs), this task has gained increasing attention, and several benchmarks are proposed to evaluate the issue resolution ability of LLMs. However, existing benchmarks have three main limitations. First, current benchmarks focus on a single programming language, limiting the evaluation of issues from repositories across different languages. Second, they usually cover a narrow range of domains, which may fail to represent the diversity of real-world issues. Third, existing benchmarks rely solely on textual information in issue descriptions, overlooking multimodal information such as images in issues. In this paper, we propose OmniGIRL, a GitHub Issue ResoLution benchmark that is multilingual, multimodal, and multi-domain. OmniGIRL includes 959 task instances, which are collected from repositories across four programming languages (i.e., Python, JavaScript, TypeScript, and Java) and eight different domains. Our evaluation shows that current LLMs show limited performances on OmniGIRL. Notably, the best-performing model, GPT-4o, resolves only 8.6% of the issues. Besides, we find that current LLMs struggle to resolve issues requiring understanding images. The best performance is achieved by Claude-3.5-Sonnet, which resolves only 10.5% of the issues with image information. Finally, we analyze the reasons behind current LLMs' failure on OmniGIRL, providing insights for future improvements.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "OmniGIRL: A Multilingual and Multimodal Benchmark for GitHub Issue Resolution":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces OmniGIRL, a new benchmark designed to evaluate the capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs) in resolving GitHub issues.  OmniGIRL addresses limitations in existing benchmarks by being multilingual (Python, JavaScript, TypeScript, Java), multi-domain (covering 8 different repository types), and multimodal (incorporating text and image information). The paper details the construction process of the benchmark, its statistics, and presents baseline results of state-of-the-art LLMs evaluated on OmniGIRL, showing limited performance, especially on issues requiring image understanding. The paper also includes a failure analysis to understand the challenges faced by current LLMs when dealing with OmniGIRL.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in the *combination* of several desirable characteristics into a single benchmark.  While previous benchmarks exist for GitHub issue resolution, they often focus on a single programming language or lack multimodal input. The integration of multilingual support, diverse domains, and image data into a single benchmark is a significant step forward. However, each element is not entirely new on its own, so the novelty is incremental, not groundbreaking.

**Significance:**  The significance of OmniGIRL stems from its potential to drive progress in the field of automated issue resolution. The benchmark:
*   **Provides a more realistic evaluation:**  By incorporating multiple programming languages and domains, OmniGIRL provides a more comprehensive test of an LLM's ability to generalize to real-world software repositories.
*   **Highlights the importance of multimodality:**  The inclusion of images helps to highlight the limitations of current LLMs in processing non-textual information, thus motivating research on multimodal reasoning in software engineering.
*   **Facilitates comparative studies:** OmniGIRL provides a common ground for researchers to compare the performance of different issue resolution techniques across different languages and domains.
*   **Offers a valuable resource:** The open-sourced dataset creation and data collection code allows other researchers to adapt, expand, and extend the benchmark, which is valuable to the community.

**Strengths:**

*   **Comprehensive and Well-Documented:** The paper provides a clear and detailed description of the benchmark construction process, including data collection, filtering, and environment setup.
*   **Multilingual and Multimodal:** The benchmark successfully addresses the limitations of existing benchmarks by incorporating multiple programming languages and image information.
*   **Rigorous Evaluation:** The authors perform a thorough evaluation of several state-of-the-art LLMs on OmniGIRL, providing valuable insights into their strengths and weaknesses.
*   **Actionable Insights:** The failure analysis provides specific directions for future research, such as improving LLMs' ability to handle cross-file modifications and understand visual information.
*   **Open Resource:** Availability of the data and data collection code is a boon for the community.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Incremental Novelty:** While the combination of features is novel, the individual components are not groundbreaking on their own.
*   **Limited Multimodal Input:** Though it has multimodal input, only 19 instances actually contain useful images and the impact of that seems negligible. The image modality could be significantly improved by a more balanced dataset.
*   **Baseline Methods:** The paper chose fairly standard existing baselines, but didn't do any fine-tuning or novel methods.
*   **Generalization beyond GitHub:** The benchmark is specifically tailored to GitHub issues.  It is not immediately clear how well the findings generalize to other software development contexts.
*   **Cost Analysis:** The authors include a cost analysis of evaluating task instances, but a cost analysis of *building* the OmniGIRL benchmark would have been beneficial to understand the resources required for creating similar benchmarks.
*   **Website information:** They mention that "We plan to evaluate potential approaches to incorporate web-based information into issue resolution." but don't actually do it for this paper.

**Potential Influence:**

OmniGIRL has the potential to significantly influence the field by becoming a standard benchmark for GitHub issue resolution. It can drive further research in multimodal software engineering and encourages the development of LLMs with a broader range of capabilities. However, it is essential to recognize that its influence will depend on its adoption by the research community. Addressing the weaknesses listed above will also greatly enhance its impact.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a valuable and well-executed benchmark that addresses several limitations of existing benchmarks for GitHub issue resolution. While its novelty is incremental, its comprehensiveness, multimodal nature, and actionable insights make it a significant contribution. The limited amount of multimodal examples, limited baseline method selection, and specific tailoring to GitHub are drawbacks.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 7/10

### Score Distillation Sampling for Audio: Source Separation, Synthesis, and Beyond
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04621v1)
- **Authors**: Jessie Richter-Powell, Antonio Torralba, Jonathan Lorraine
- **Abstract**: We introduce Audio-SDS, a generalization of Score Distillation Sampling (SDS) to text-conditioned audio diffusion models. While SDS was initially designed for text-to-3D generation using image diffusion, its core idea of distilling a powerful generative prior into a separate parametric representation extends to the audio domain. Leveraging a single pretrained model, Audio-SDS enables a broad range of tasks without requiring specialized datasets. In particular, we demonstrate how Audio-SDS can guide physically informed impact sound simulations, calibrate FM-synthesis parameters, and perform prompt-specified source separation. Our findings illustrate the versatility of distillation-based methods across modalities and establish a robust foundation for future work using generative priors in audio tasks.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Audio-SDS, an adaptation of Score Distillation Sampling (SDS) for text-conditioned audio diffusion models. Instead of training separate models for different audio tasks, Audio-SDS leverages a single pre-trained text-to-audio diffusion model and optimizes parameters of explicit audio representations (e.g., FM synthesizers, physical impact simulations, multi-source mixtures) to align with text prompts. The authors propose modifications to the standard SDS approach for audio, including a decoder-based SDS update, spectrogram emphasis, and multi-step denoising. They demonstrate the framework on FM synthesizer tuning, physically informed impact model calibration, and prompt-guided source separation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** While the core idea of SDS is not new, its adaptation to the audio domain is a valuable contribution. The modifications made to the SDS algorithm—specifically, the decoder-based approach, spectrogram emphasis, and multistep denoising—are significant in addressing audio-specific challenges. These modifications make SDS feasible for audio tasks by overcoming instabilities and improving the quality of the synthesized audio.

*   **Significance:** The work significantly expands the applicability of pre-trained diffusion models. By using a single pre-trained diffusion model for multiple audio tasks, Audio-SDS avoids the need for training separate models on domain-specific datasets, which can be a limiting factor in audio processing. The demonstrations on FM synthesis, impact sound synthesis, and source separation show the versatility of the approach. The unification of data-driven priors with explicit parametrizations is a powerful concept that can enable new approaches to audio editing and synthesis.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Generality:** The framework's ability to handle different audio tasks (synthesis, separation, editing) with a single pre-trained model is a significant strength.
    *   **Data efficiency:** Avoiding the need for task-specific datasets is a considerable advantage, particularly in domains where such data is scarce or expensive to obtain.
    *   **Controllability:** The use of explicit parameterizations (e.g., physics-based models) allows for fine-grained control over the generated audio, combining the power of diffusion models with the interpretability of traditional audio processing techniques.
    *   **Decoder-based Implementation:**  The decision to avoid differentiating through the encoder of the diffusion model addresses a crucial instability issue and is a key technical contribution.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Reliance on Pre-trained Models:** The performance of Audio-SDS is limited by the capabilities and biases of the pre-trained diffusion model.
    *   **Limited Evaluation Metrics:** The reliance on subjective metrics and the absence of a detailed comparison of running times with classical methods might limit the conclusions about the effectiveness of the approach.
    *   **Model Coverage Limitations:** As the authors themselves acknowledge, there are inherent limitations due to the pre-trained model's coverage. Complex sounds or specific environments might not be well represented.

*   **Potential Impact:** This work provides a valuable framework for future research on generative audio models. It could lead to new tools and techniques for audio editing, synthesis, and sound design.  It sets a strong foundation for subsequent explorations of distillation-based techniques in audio, and offers insights to the intersection between physics-based and data-driven approaches. The work could impact the areas of music production, VR/AR, game development, and accessibility by making high-quality audio processing more accessible and controllable.

*   **Score Justification:**

    I assign a score of 7. The core idea is not entirely novel, as it builds on existing SDS methods. However, the adaptation to audio and the proposed audio-specific modifications are valuable. The strengths of the paper lie in the generality of the approach and the experimental evidence demonstrating its usefulness across different audio tasks. The limitations related to model coverage and certain aspects of the evaluation prevent it from achieving a higher score. The novelty of the *combination* of techniques for audio, the practical nature of the problems addressed, and the impact that the integration of data-driven diffusion models with classical audio signal processing makes to the field's landscape gives this work a strong argument for a high impact despite being an *adaptation* of already well-established techniques.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.SD
- **Score**: 7/10

### PrimitiveAnything: Human-Crafted 3D Primitive Assembly Generation with Auto-Regressive Transformer
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04622v1)
- **Authors**: Jingwen Ye, Yuze He, Yanning Zhou, Yiqin Zhu, Kaiwen Xiao, Yong-Jin Liu, Wei Yang, Xiao Han
- **Abstract**: Shape primitive abstraction, which decomposes complex 3D shapes into simple geometric elements, plays a crucial role in human visual cognition and has broad applications in computer vision and graphics. While recent advances in 3D content generation have shown remarkable progress, existing primitive abstraction methods either rely on geometric optimization with limited semantic understanding or learn from small-scale, category-specific datasets, struggling to generalize across diverse shape categories. We present PrimitiveAnything, a novel framework that reformulates shape primitive abstraction as a primitive assembly generation task. PrimitiveAnything includes a shape-conditioned primitive transformer for auto-regressive generation and an ambiguity-free parameterization scheme to represent multiple types of primitives in a unified manner. The proposed framework directly learns the process of primitive assembly from large-scale human-crafted abstractions, enabling it to capture how humans decompose complex shapes into primitive elements. Through extensive experiments, we demonstrate that PrimitiveAnything can generate high-quality primitive assemblies that better align with human perception while maintaining geometric fidelity across diverse shape categories. It benefits various 3D applications and shows potential for enabling primitive-based user-generated content (UGC) in games. Project page: https://primitiveanything.github.io
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "PrimitiveAnything: Human-Crafted 3D Primitive Assembly Generation with Auto-Regressive Transformer" introduces a novel framework for decomposing complex 3D shapes into simpler geometric primitives. It frames this task as a sequence generation problem, leveraging a transformer-based architecture trained on a large dataset of human-crafted 3D primitive assemblies. The framework includes a shape-conditioned primitive transformer for autoregressive generation and an ambiguity-free parameterization scheme for multiple primitive types. The authors demonstrate that their method can generate high-quality primitive assemblies that align better with human perception than existing geometric optimization or category-specific learning-based methods. It benefits various 3D applications and shows potential for enabling primitive-based user-generated content.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in reframing the problem of 3D shape abstraction as a sequence generation task, specifically using an autoregressive transformer model. While the use of transformers for 3D generation is not entirely new (MeshAnything being a notable predecessor), applying it to primitive assembly and learning directly from human-crafted abstractions is a significant departure from traditional geometric fitting or direct regression approaches. The proposed ambiguity-free parameterization scheme is another novel contribution that improves the robustness and accuracy of the learning process.

* **Significance:** The work holds significant potential for various applications in 3D computer vision and graphics. Primitive-based representations offer advantages in semantic understanding, robotic manipulation, scene understanding, and interactive modeling systems. A model that learns from human intuition and generalizes well across diverse shape categories has the potential to simplify complex 3D content creation pipelines and enable new forms of user interaction. The potential for creating lightweight 3D models suitable for real-time multiplayer game environments is particularly noteworthy.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Human-centric approach:**  Learning from human-crafted abstractions is a key strength, allowing the model to capture intuitive shape decomposition logic rather than just optimizing for geometric fidelity.
    * **Generalization:**  The ability to generalize across diverse shape categories, unlike previous category-specific methods, is a significant advantage.
    * **Technical Design:** The carefully designed ambiguity-free parameterization scheme and the cascaded primitive decoder contribute to the robustness and performance of the model.
    * **Comprehensive Evaluation:**  The paper includes extensive quantitative and qualitative comparisons against state-of-the-art methods, demonstrating the superiority of the proposed approach. The user study adds further credibility by validating the alignment with human perception.
    * **Ablation Studies:** Provides strong insight and proves the effectiveness of the individual components.
* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Primitive limitations:**  The current implementation is limited to three primitive types (cuboids, elliptical cylinders, and ellipsoids). While the modular design allows for the addition of new primitive types, the expressiveness of the representation is still constrained.
    * **Out-of-Distribution Performance:** As the authors acknowledge, the method struggles with objects containing topological structures rarely seen in training.
    * **Annotation Diversity:** The variety of annotation styles could also introduce some inconsistencies in the training data, potentially affecting the model's performance.
    * **Lack of texture:** Only focus on geometry and not appearance modelling.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in 3D shape abstraction, content generation, and scene understanding. The sequence generation approach could inspire new methods for representing and manipulating 3D scenes. The focus on human-centric abstractions could lead to more intuitive and user-friendly 3D modeling tools.

**Score:** 8

**Justification:**

The paper presents a significant and novel approach to 3D shape abstraction with a clear potential impact. The framework's ability to learn from human-crafted abstractions and generalize across shape categories represents a notable advance over existing methods. The technical design is well-reasoned and the experimental evaluation is thorough. While the limitations regarding primitive types and out-of-distribution performance exist, these are clearly acknowledged and offer directions for future research. The novelty of the autoregressive approach to shape abstraction, combined with the solid experimental results and clear potential for real-world applications, warrants a high score. However, the current limitations prevent it from achieving a score in the 9-10 range.

- **Classification**: cs.GR
- **Score**: 8/10

### EchoInk-R1: Exploring Audio-Visual Reasoning in Multimodal LLMs via Reinforcement Learning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04623v1)
- **Authors**: Zhenghao Xing, Xiaowei Hu, Chi-Wing Fu, Wenhai Wang, Jifeng Dai, Pheng-Ann Heng
- **Abstract**: Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) have advanced perception across text, vision, and audio, yet they often struggle with structured cross-modal reasoning, particularly when integrating audio and visual signals. We introduce EchoInk-R1, a reinforcement learning framework that enhances such reasoning in MLLMs. Built upon the Qwen2.5-Omni-7B foundation and optimized with Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO), EchoInk-R1 tackles multiple-choice question answering over synchronized audio-image pairs. To enable this, we curate AVQA-R1-6K, a dataset pairing such audio-image inputs with multiple-choice questions derived from OmniInstruct-v1. EchoInk-R1-7B achieves 85.77% accuracy on the validation set, outperforming the base model, which scores 80.53%, using only 562 reinforcement learning steps. Beyond accuracy, EchoInk-R1 demonstrates reflective reasoning by revisiting initial interpretations and refining responses when facing ambiguous multimodal inputs. These results suggest that lightweight reinforcement learning fine-tuning enhances cross-modal reasoning in MLLMs. EchoInk-R1 is the first framework to unify audio, visual, and textual modalities for general open-world reasoning via reinforcement learning. Code and data are publicly released to facilitate further research.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces EchoInk-R1, a reinforcement learning (RL) framework to enhance audio-visual reasoning in multimodal large language models (MLLMs). The framework uses the Qwen2.5-Omni-7B as the base model and employs Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO). The authors curate a new dataset, AVQA-R1-6K, derived from OmniInstruct-v1, specifically designed for audio-visual question answering. Experimental results demonstrate that EchoInk-R1 significantly improves accuracy compared to the baseline model with limited RL steps, showing reflective reasoning capabilities. The authors release the code and data publicly.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its specific combination of elements rather than groundbreaking individual components.
    *   **Audio-Visual Reasoning via RL:** Applying reinforcement learning to improve audio-visual reasoning within a large language model is a logical step following recent advances in RL for text and visual-language models. However, the literature survey shows that other works already explore either audio+text, vision+text, or other forms of multimodal reasoning. The *combination* of audio-visual and textual modalities *within a reinforcement learning framework, for general open-world reasoning* is the incremental innovation.
    *   **Dataset Curation:**  The creation of AVQA-R1-6K, specifically designed for audio-visual reasoning, is a valuable contribution, given the lack of suitable datasets. However, it relies on a subset of an existing dataset (OmniInstruct), thus its inherent novelty is somewhat lessened.
    *   **GRPO Application:**  Using GRPO is a reasonable choice given its demonstrated effectiveness in other RL-based language model tasks. However, this is an adaptation rather than a genuinely new methodological approach.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a relevant and important problem: improving the cross-modal reasoning capabilities of MLLMs.
    *   **Performance Improvement:** The reported accuracy gains over the baseline (Qwen2.5-Omni-7B) are significant, demonstrating the effectiveness of the approach, especially given the relatively small number of RL steps.
    *   **Qualitative Analysis:** The "aha moments" showcased through qualitative analysis (Figures 1 & 2), where the model self-corrects based on multimodal cues, is compelling and supports the claim of improved reasoning.
    *   **Community Resource:**  The public release of the code and dataset facilitates further research in this area.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Incremental Innovation:** The approach is largely an adaptation of existing techniques (GRPO, dataset curation) to a new problem (audio-visual reasoning).
    *   **Dataset Size:** While the creation of AVQA-R1-6K is a positive step, the dataset size is relatively small (around 6K samples) compared to other datasets used for training large language models, which may limit the generalizability of the model and impact the future scope for scaling it.
    *   **Baseline Comparison:** While the performance improvement compared to Qwen2.5-Omni-7B is clear, it would benefit from a comparison with other existing models designed for multimodal reasoning, to position the performance better in the landscape.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper represents a valuable, but incremental, contribution to the field. It takes existing RL techniques (GRPO) and applies them to a relevant problem (audio-visual reasoning), supported by a newly created dataset. The performance gains and qualitative analysis are compelling, demonstrating the potential of RL for this task. However, the lack of significant methodological innovation and the modest dataset size limit the overall impact.  While the released code and data are beneficial to the community, the incremental nature of the approach restricts its potential influence.

### Retrieval Augmented Generation Evaluation for Health Documents
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04680v1)
- **Authors**: Mario Ceresa, Lorenzo Bertolini, Valentin Comte, Nicholas Spadaro, Barbara Raffael, Brigitte Toussaint, Sergio Consoli, Amalia Muñoz Piñeiro, Alex Patak, Maddalena Querci, Tobias Wiesenthal
- **Abstract**: Safe and trustworthy use of Large Language Models (LLM) in the processing of healthcare documents and scientific papers could substantially help clinicians, scientists and policymakers in overcoming information overload and focusing on the most relevant information at a given moment. Retrieval Augmented Generation (RAG) is a promising method to leverage the potential of LLMs while enhancing the accuracy of their outcomes. This report assesses the potentials and shortcomings of such approaches in the automatic knowledge synthesis of different types of documents in the health domain. To this end, it describes: (1) an internally developed proof of concept pipeline that employs state-of-the-art practices to deliver safe and trustable analysis for healthcare documents and scientific papers called RAGEv (Retrieval Augmented Generation Evaluation); (2) a set of evaluation tools for LLM-based document retrieval and generation; (3) a benchmark dataset to verify the accuracy and veracity of the results called RAGEv-Bench. It concludes that careful implementations of RAG techniques could minimize most of the common problems in the use of LLMs for document processing in the health domain, obtaining very high scores both on short yes/no answers and long answers. There is a high potential for incorporating it into the day-to-day work of policy support tasks, but additional efforts are required to obtain a consistent and trustworthy tool.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided document:

**Summary:**

The report details the development and evaluation of RAGEv, a Retrieval Augmented Generation (RAG) pipeline designed for processing healthcare documents and scientific papers.  It addresses the challenges and limitations of using Large Language Models (LLMs) in the healthcare domain, including issues related to factuality, bias, explainability, and data privacy. The authors describe the architecture of RAGEv, the strategies employed to improve accuracy, and the creation of a benchmark dataset (RAGEv-Bench) for evaluating the system. The evaluation includes automatic performance metrics on the PubMedQA dataset, as well as manual usability checks using three hand-crafted datasets related to Horizon Europe projects, Virtual Human Twins, and Antimicrobial Resistance. The report concludes that careful implementation of RAG techniques can mitigate common LLM issues, yielding high performance on both short and long answer tasks, and proposes future improvements focusing on query processing, specialized models, and advanced evaluation metrics.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper presents an internally developed RAG pipeline (RAGEv) with specific focus to evaluate it for Health related data and documents.  The novelty lies in the comprehensive end-to-end approach, including the implementation, evaluation, and creation of a benchmark dataset tailored for the healthcare domain. While RAG is not a novel concept *per se*, the domain-specific tailoring, and the combination of both automated and manual evaluation on real-world use cases lend a degree of novelty.  The attention to usability and trustworthiness in the context of policy support is a valuable addition. However, the technical contributions on the architectural side of the system seem to be using existing methodologies (Chunking strategy, Embeddings) rather than innovating new methods.

**Significance:** The significance of this work lies in its potential to address the information overload faced by clinicians, scientists, and policymakers in the healthcare domain.  By providing a trustworthy and accurate means of processing large volumes of scientific literature and policy documents, RAGEv could facilitate evidence-based decision-making and accelerate the translation of research findings into practice.  The usability testing reveals valuable insights into the practical challenges of deploying LLMs in real-world scenarios, informing future development efforts.  The RAGEv-Bench dataset contributes to the community by providing a resource for evaluating LLM-based systems in the healthcare domain.

**Strengths:**

*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper employs a multi-faceted evaluation approach, combining automatic performance metrics with manual usability checks, providing a holistic assessment of the system's capabilities.
*   **Domain Focus:** The specific focus on healthcare documents and policy support addresses a critical need in a field where accuracy and trustworthiness are paramount.
*   **Usability Emphasis:** The usability testing component provides valuable insights into the practical challenges of deploying LLMs in real-world scenarios, informing future development efforts.
*   **Reproducibility:** The creation of RAGEv-Bench promotes reproducibility and facilitates comparison with other methods.
*   **Emphasis on Trustworthiness:** Addresses factuality, bias, and data privacy, important considerations for LLMs in healthcare.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Technical Depth:** The technical details of the RAG implementation could be more in-depth, particularly regarding the specific choices of embedding models, chunking strategies, and prompt engineering techniques. More direct comparison to SOTA methods would strengthen the claims.
*   **Scalability Concerns:** The report acknowledges scalability limitations of the current RAGEv system as an "Information System," which raises concerns about its ability to handle large-scale deployments.
*   **Generalizability:**  While the domain focus is a strength, it also limits the generalizability of the findings to other domains. Additional discussion on the transferability of the approach would be beneficial.
*   **Novelty of Methods Used:** The individual components of the pipeline, such as using specific embedding models or chunking techniques, may not be inherently novel in themselves. The originality mainly stems from applying and evaluating these methods in a specific context.

**Justification:**

The paper delivers a solid contribution by thoroughly detailing the implementation and testing of a RAG-based system specifically designed for health documents. It provides useful insights on its strengths and weaknesses, the practical implementation considerations, and importantly, the usability for its users. The authors were critical in their approach and testing. However, it is important to note that the contribution of the work is mostly based on the application of the methodology on the data, rather than the methodology itself. Due to this, a high mark cannot be given.

### Lay-Your-Scene: Natural Scene Layout Generation with Diffusion Transformers
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04718v1)
- **Authors**: Divyansh Srivastava, Xiang Zhang, He Wen, Chenru Wen, Zhuowen Tu
- **Abstract**: We present Lay-Your-Scene (shorthand LayouSyn), a novel text-to-layout generation pipeline for natural scenes. Prior scene layout generation methods are either closed-vocabulary or use proprietary large language models for open-vocabulary generation, limiting their modeling capabilities and broader applicability in controllable image generation. In this work, we propose to use lightweight open-source language models to obtain scene elements from text prompts and a novel aspect-aware diffusion Transformer architecture trained in an open-vocabulary manner for conditional layout generation. Extensive experiments demonstrate that LayouSyn outperforms existing methods and achieves state-of-the-art performance on challenging spatial and numerical reasoning benchmarks. Additionally, we present two applications of LayouSyn. First, we show that coarse initialization from large language models can be seamlessly combined with our method to achieve better results. Second, we present a pipeline for adding objects to images, demonstrating the potential of LayouSyn in image editing applications.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "Lay-Your-Scene" (LayouSyn), a new text-to-layout generation pipeline for natural scenes.  LayouSyn uses open-source, lightweight language models to extract scene elements from text prompts and combines this with a novel aspect-aware diffusion Transformer architecture.  The model is trained in an open-vocabulary manner for conditional layout generation.  Experiments demonstrate that LayouSyn outperforms existing methods on spatial and numerical reasoning benchmarks. The paper also presents applications of LayouSyn for coarse initialization using larger language models (LLMs) and for adding objects to existing images.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The combination of open-source LLMs for prompt understanding with an aspect-aware diffusion Transformer for layout generation has merit. Specifically, the aspect-aware diffusion Transformer architecture is novel. Addressing the limitations of closed-source LLMs (opacity, cost, latency) in controllable image generation pipelines is a practical contribution.

*   **Significance:** Controllable image generation relies on plausible scene layouts, and automating layout generation from text is a key step in the field.  The emphasis on spatial and numerical reasoning is also crucial for generating believable layouts. The demonstrated applications (coarse initialization with LLMs and object addition) are relevant and could stimulate further research. Demonstrating superior performance on challenging benchmarks is a definite strength.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Use of open-source components makes the approach more accessible and reproducible.
    *   Strong experimental results on challenging benchmarks (NSR-1K Spatial).
    *   Demonstration of practical applications highlights the utility of the method.
    *   Ablation studies provide insights into the contribution of different components (architecture, scaling factor, description set generation).

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   While the text mentions addressing limitations of closed-source LLMs, it is not entirely clear to what extent these are truly addressed. The reliance on other large language models (e.g., for object detection in object addition) remains.
    *   The evaluation metric L-FID, while standard, can be abstract. More human evaluations would strengthen the findings.
    *   The paper claims state-of-the-art performance, but the improvement over existing methods might not always be overwhelmingly large. The "up to" in "50.1% improvement" could be misleading.
    *   Some parts of the paper's framing are somewhat incremental (e.g., building on diffusion Transformers, using noise schedule scaling).

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence controllable image generation, especially by enabling more flexible and accessible pipelines.  The emphasis on open-source components could encourage wider adoption and further research. The demonstrated applications (particularly object addition) could inspire new image editing tools.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a solid contribution to the field. While it builds on existing techniques, it does so with a clear motivation (open-source, controllable layout generation) and presents a novel architecture tailored to the task.  The experimental results are convincing, and the applications are relevant. The main weaknesses relate to the incremental nature of some contributions and the limited scope of certain evaluations. Therefore, it warrants a solid score above average but below truly groundbreaking.

### SOAEsV2-7B/72B: Full-Pipeline Optimization for State-Owned Enterprise LLMs via Continual Pre-Training, Domain-Progressive SFT and Distillation-Enhanced Speculative Decoding
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04723v1)
- **Authors**: Jingyang Deng, Ran Chen, Jo-Ku Cheng, Jinwen Ma
- **Abstract**: This study addresses key challenges in developing domain-specific large language models (LLMs) for Chinese state-owned assets and enterprises (SOAEs), where current approaches face three limitations: 1) constrained model capacity that limits knowledge integration and cross-task adaptability; 2) excessive reliance on domain-specific supervised fine-tuning (SFT) data, which neglects the broader applicability of general language patterns; and 3) inefficient inference acceleration for large models processing long contexts. In this work, we propose SOAEsV2-7B/72B, a specialized LLM series developed via a three-phase framework: 1) continual pre-training integrates domain knowledge while retaining base capabilities; 2) domain-progressive SFT employs curriculum-based learning strategy, transitioning from weakly relevant conversational data to expert-annotated SOAEs datasets to optimize domain-specific tasks; 3) distillation-enhanced speculative decoding accelerates inference via logit distillation between 72B target and 7B draft models, achieving 1.39-1.52$\times$ speedup without quality loss. Experimental results demonstrate that our domain-specific pre-training phase maintains 99.8% of original general language capabilities while significantly improving domain performance, resulting in a 1.08$\times$ improvement in Rouge-1 score and a 1.17$\times$ enhancement in BLEU-4 score. Ablation studies further show that domain-progressive SFT outperforms single-stage training, achieving 1.02$\times$ improvement in Rouge-1 and 1.06$\times$ in BLEU-4. Our work introduces a comprehensive, full-pipeline approach for optimizing SOAEs LLMs, bridging the gap between general language capabilities and domain-specific expertise.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SOAESV2-7B/72B, a specialized series of large language models (LLMs) designed for Chinese state-owned assets and enterprises (SOAEs). It addresses limitations in existing approaches by focusing on three key areas: model capacity, reliance on domain-specific supervised fine-tuning (SFT) data, and inefficient inference acceleration for large models with long contexts. The paper proposes a three-phase framework: (1) continual pre-training to integrate domain knowledge while maintaining general capabilities, (2) domain-progressive SFT employing a curriculum-based learning strategy to optimize for domain-specific tasks, and (3) distillation-enhanced speculative decoding to accelerate inference. The authors demonstrate improved performance in domain-specific tasks while retaining general language capabilities and achieving significant speedup during inference.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper introduces a relatively comprehensive approach to domain-specific LLM development, combining continual pre-training, curriculum-based SFT, and distillation-enhanced speculative decoding. Each of these techniques is not necessarily novel in isolation, but their combination and tailored application to the SOAEs domain demonstrate innovation. The progressive SFT based on domain relevance stratification is a valuable and nuanced approach.

*   **Significance:** Developing LLMs tailored for specific industries like SOAEs is highly relevant and important for practical applications. The paper addresses key bottlenecks that hinder the effective deployment of LLMs in such contexts, particularly the trade-off between domain expertise and generalizability and the need for efficient inference. The proposed method addresses these needs well.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Framework:** The paper presents a well-defined, three-phase approach, offering a full-pipeline solution from pre-training to inference.
    *   **Performance Gains:** The experimental results demonstrate significant improvements in domain-specific performance while preserving general language capabilities, as well as enhanced inference speed.
    *   **Well-Defined Methodology:** The paper provides a clear explanation of the methodologies used, making it easy to reproduce the work. The curriculum-based approach to SFT is well-motivated.
    *   **Ablation studies** Demonstrates that the proposed domain progressive training works better than other common training schemes and validates design choices.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Benchmarking:** While the paper shows improvement in Rouge and BLEU scores for SOAE-specific data, further evaluation on diverse downstream tasks within the SOAEs domain would strengthen the claims. The evaluations focus only on general language capabilities, so domain-specific downstream tasks are important to showcase.
    *   **Scalability:** The results primarily focus on 7B and 72B models, which are substantial but not at the cutting edge of current model sizes. Analysis of resource requirements and scalability to even larger models could be beneficial.
    *   **Lack of comparison with other specific fine tuning techniques**. While the ablation studies shows some difference with single-stage fine tuning, there's no comparisons to other common fine-tuning techniques.

*   **Potential Impact:** The paper has the potential to influence the development of domain-specific LLMs, particularly in industries where a balance between general and specialized knowledge is crucial. The proposed framework provides a useful blueprint for other researchers and practitioners. The specific focus on the SOAE domain in China makes it particularly relevant in that context.

*   **Score Justification:** Considering the novelty of combining existing techniques in a tailored framework for the SOAEs domain, the significant performance gains, the well-defined methodology, and the potential for impact, but also acknowledging the limitations in benchmarking, scalability analysis, and comparisons to specific fine tuning techniques, I would assign a score of **7**. It's a solid, incremental contribution that addresses practical challenges in applying LLMs to a specific industry, but could be strengthened with more extensive evaluation and broader analysis.

### QBD-RankedDataGen: Generating Custom Ranked Datasets for Improving Query-By-Document Search Using LLM-Reranking with Reduced Human Effort
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04732v1)
- **Authors**: Sriram Gopalakrishnan, Sunandita Patra
- **Abstract**: The Query-By-Document (QBD) problem is an information retrieval problem where the query is a document, and the retrieved candidates are documents that match the query document, often in a domain or query specific manner. This can be crucial for tasks such as patent matching, legal or compliance case retrieval, and academic literature review. Existing retrieval methods, including keyword search and document embeddings, can be optimized with domain-specific datasets to improve QBD search performance. However, creating these domain-specific datasets is often costly and time-consuming. Our work introduces a process to generate custom QBD-search datasets and compares a set of methods to use in this problem, which we refer to as QBD-RankedDatagen. We provide a comparative analysis of our proposed methods in terms of cost, speed, and the human interface with the domain experts. The methods we compare leverage Large Language Models (LLMs) which can incorporate domain expert input to produce document scores and rankings, as well as explanations for human review. The process and methods for it that we present can significantly reduce human effort in dataset creation for custom domains while still obtaining sufficient expert knowledge for tuning retrieval models. We evaluate our methods on QBD datasets from the Text Retrieval Conference (TREC) and finetune the parameters of the BM25 model -- which is used in many industrial-strength search engines like OpenSearch -- using the generated data.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a process, QBD-RANKEDDATAGEN, for generating custom ranked datasets to improve query-by-document (QBD) search, particularly in domains where expert knowledge is crucial but costly to obtain. The process focuses on leveraging Large Language Models (LLMs) in the reranking step to reduce human effort. It compares different LLM-based reranking methods (pointwise and pairwise approaches, with and without instructions derived from task descriptions), emphasizing those that facilitate easier human review and validation.  The generated ranked datasets are then used to fine-tune the BM25 retrieval model, which is commonly used in the industry.  The paper evaluates the methods on TREC clinical trials and CORD-19 datasets, comparing reranking performance and the impact of fine-tuning BM25 on the generated training signal.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its focused approach to QBD dataset generation, explicitly addressing the challenge of reducing human effort by strategically incorporating LLMs in the reranking phase. While LLMs have been used for reranking before, the focus on QBD dataset creation with this specific aim, coupled with the comparative analysis of different LLM-based methods *with and without explicit human-derived instruction* is a distinct contribution. The exploration of pointwise vs. pairwise methods and their impact on human reviewability is also a valuable aspect. Prior studies mainly focus on improving retrieval models but ignore the reduction of expert effort during data creation, which is a key bottleneck in many real-world applications. The decision to fine-tune the readily available and relatively simple BM25 based on LLM-based dataset generation adds to the practicality.

*   **Significance:** The significance of this work resides in its potential to make QBD search more accessible and effective in specialized domains.  By providing a framework that reduces the burden on domain experts, the paper opens the door to creating custom search solutions where previously the cost and effort of dataset creation were prohibitive. The exploration of different LLM-based strategies sheds light on their tradeoffs in terms of performance and human reviewability.  The experimental results provide insights into the effectiveness of different approaches and highlight the importance of human validation to the data generation process. The paper directly tackles the real-world problem of lack of ranked datasets for long-form QBD.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Clear problem definition and motivation, addressing a practical challenge in QBD search.
    *   Well-defined framework (QBD-RANKEDDATAGEN) that explicitly focuses on human effort reduction.
    *   Comparative analysis of different LLM-based reranking methods, highlighting their pros and cons.
    *   Empirical evaluation on TREC datasets, providing insights into the performance of different methods and the impact of fine-tuning.
    *   Practical application in tuning BM25 parameters to enhance retrieval performance.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The performance gains from LLM reranking without human validation are inconsistent; often, they don't surpass (and sometimes even hurt) the baseline. This highlights the critical need for human-in-the-loop which is an expensive affair. More work can be done on reducing human effort.

    *   The evaluation is limited to two TREC datasets, which may not fully represent the diversity of QBD scenarios. A wider range of datasets would strengthen the generalizability of the findings. The chosen datasets are of moderate size, and the conclusions may change as larger and more complicated datasets are explored.

    *   The instructions added to LLM might not have been robust or well formulated enough. There is no in depth qualitative analysis of the impact.
    *   The hyperparameter search space for the BM25 fine-tuning can be expanded. It looks like the search happened in a small window for computational concerns.

*   **Potential Influence:**  The paper has the potential to influence the field by:

    *   Encouraging further research on QBD dataset generation and human-in-the-loop LLM workflows.
    *   Providing practical guidance for applying LLMs to reranking and fine-tuning in specialized domains.
    *   Raising awareness of the importance of human validation in LLM-based information retrieval systems.
    *   Motivating the development of more efficient and effective LLM-based methods for reranking and dataset generation.

*   **Overall:** While the empirical results are not consistently impressive and the evaluation is somewhat limited in scope, the paper makes a valuable contribution by framing the problem of QBD dataset generation as one of human effort reduction, and by providing a framework and experimental analysis to guide future research in this area. It raises important questions about the role of human expertise and validation in LLM-based information retrieval systems. The exploration and evaluation of pairwise LLM reranking methods adds a practical value to the study.
