### From First Draft to Final Insight: A Multi-Agent Approach for Feedback Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04869v1)
- **Authors**: Jie Cao, Chloe Qianhui Zhao, Xian Chen, Shuman Wang, Christian Schunn, Kenneth R. Koedinger, Jionghao Lin
- **Abstract**: Producing large volumes of high-quality, timely feedback poses significant challenges to instructors. To address this issue, automation technologies-particularly Large Language Models (LLMs)-show great potential. However, current LLM-based research still shows room for improvement in terms of feedback quality. Our study proposed a multi-agent approach performing "generation, evaluation, and regeneration" (G-E-RG) to further enhance feedback quality. In the first-generation phase, six methods were adopted, combining three feedback theoretical frameworks and two prompt methods: zero-shot and retrieval-augmented generation with chain-of-thought (RAG_CoT). The results indicated that, compared to first-round feedback, G-E-RG significantly improved final feedback across six methods for most dimensions. Specifically:(1) Evaluation accuracy for six methods increased by 3.36% to 12.98% (p<0.001); (2) The proportion of feedback containing four effective components rose from an average of 27.72% to an average of 98.49% among six methods, sub-dimensions of providing critiques, highlighting strengths, encouraging agency, and cultivating dialogue also showed great enhancement (p<0.001); (3) There was a significant improvement in most of the feature values (p<0.001), although some sub-dimensions (e.g., strengthening the teacher-student relationship) still require further enhancement; (4) The simplicity of feedback was effectively enhanced (p<0.001) for three methods.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a multi-agent approach called "G-E-RG" (Generation, Evaluation, and Regeneration) to enhance the quality of feedback generated by Large Language Models (LLMs) for student responses in an educational context.  The system involves three agents: one to generate initial feedback using various prompt engineering techniques and feedback frameworks, another to evaluate the generated feedback automatically using LLMs (acting as a judge), and a third to regenerate the feedback based on the evaluation.  The authors compare different prompt combinations (Zero-shot, RAG_CoT) and feedback frameworks (knowledge transmission-centered, learner-centered, and none) in the initial feedback generation phase. They evaluate the system's performance based on evaluation accuracy, the presence of key feedback components, feature values related to learner-centeredness, and simplicity of feedback. The results show that the G-E-RG process generally improves feedback quality across most dimensions compared to single-round feedback generation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper introduces a worthwhile iterative approach to LLM-based feedback generation.  While multi-agent systems and LLMs for feedback *generation* aren't completely novel, the specific combination of *generation*, *automated evaluation*, and subsequent *regeneration* within a feedback context is a relatively fresh contribution. Prior research, as cited, has explored LLMs for feedback and even multi-agent systems in education, but the iterative loop with automated evaluation as a core component distinguishes this work.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial problem: the challenge of providing high-quality, timely feedback to large student populations. The automation aspect is appealing given instructor workload concerns. The use of LLMs for evaluation, while having limitations, represents a practical approach towards scalability. The empirical evaluation, using real data from a graduate course, strengthens the study's relevance. The findings indicating improved feedback quality with the G-E-RG method are promising, suggesting a practical way to enhance automated feedback systems.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Well-defined Methodology:** The paper clearly describes the G-E-RG process and the various methods and frameworks used.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The use of multiple metrics (reliability, components, features, simplicity) provides a holistic assessment of feedback quality.
    *   **Real-World Data:** The study employs data from an actual graduate course, making the findings more relevant to practical educational settings.
    *   **Comparative Analysis:** The comparison of different prompt techniques and feedback frameworks adds valuable insights.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **LLM Evaluation Limitations:** The reliance on LLMs for evaluation is a potential weakness. The paper itself acknowledges that LLMs struggle with certain aspects of feedback assessment, especially "usability" and "learner independence." This could skew the regeneration process, reinforcing biases or overlooking important aspects of effective feedback. Further improvement is warranted.
    *   **Limited Scope:** The study is confined to a single graduate course at one university.  Generalizability to other disciplines, student populations, or course types remains unclear.
    *   **Feature means Improvement:** Though results indicated improvement across many features for each framework through subsequent rounds, the final feature means (ranging from 2.39/3 to 2.63/3) are not particularly high suggesting room for significant improvement still.
    *   **Fixed slides error:** Since the slides are fixed based on similarity at the beginning of the feedback loop, that the retrieved slides continue to propagate the error throughout the feedback loop.
    *   **Multi-agent pipeline resource-intensive:** Though the multi-agent pipeline may improve feedback, the authors acknowledge that the method is resource-intensive and may increase the cost.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in automated feedback generation by providing a practical and iterative framework. It could encourage the development of more sophisticated multi-agent systems for education and the exploration of alternative evaluation methods. It highlights the importance of considering learner-centered principles in automated feedback design.

*   **Room for Improvement**: Future work might focus on enhancing the LLM-based evaluation accuracy (potentially with human-in-the-loop elements), experimenting with different agent roles/interactions, and testing the system across diverse educational contexts. Exploring alternative retrieval methods for slides, or enhancing the evaluation process so that slides may change are also necessary.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:** The paper presents a novel and well-executed multi-agent framework for LLM-based feedback generation, addressing a significant challenge in education. The use of real-world data and comprehensive evaluation strengthens the study. However, the reliance on LLMs for evaluation, the limited scope, and the potential for further enhancement in specific feedback features hold the paper back from a higher score. The paper makes a meaningful contribution, but there is room to refine the approach and expand its applicability.
- **Classification**: cs.HC
- **Score**: 7/10

### GroverGPT-2: Simulating Grover's Algorithm via Chain-of-Thought Reasoning and Quantum-Native Tokenization
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04880v1)
- **Authors**: Min Chen, Jinglei Cheng, Pingzhi Li, Haoran Wang, Tianlong Chen, Junyu Liu
- **Abstract**: Quantum computing offers theoretical advantages over classical computing for specific tasks, yet the boundary of practical quantum advantage remains an open question. To investigate this boundary, it is crucial to understand whether, and how, classical machines can learn and simulate quantum algorithms. Recent progress in large language models (LLMs) has demonstrated strong reasoning abilities, prompting exploration into their potential for this challenge. In this work, we introduce GroverGPT-2, an LLM-based method for simulating Grover's algorithm using Chain-of-Thought (CoT) reasoning and quantum-native tokenization. Building on its predecessor, GroverGPT-2 performs simulation directly from quantum circuit representations while producing logically structured and interpretable outputs. Our results show that GroverGPT-2 can learn and internalize quantum circuit logic through efficient processing of quantum-native tokens, providing direct evidence that classical models like LLMs can capture the structure of quantum algorithms. Furthermore, GroverGPT-2 outputs interleave circuit data with natural language, embedding explicit reasoning into the simulation. This dual capability positions GroverGPT-2 as a prototype for advancing machine understanding of quantum algorithms and modeling quantum circuit logic. We also identify an empirical scaling law for GroverGPT-2 with increasing qubit numbers, suggesting a path toward scalable classical simulation. These findings open new directions for exploring the limits of classical simulatability, enhancing quantum education and research, and laying groundwork for future foundation models in quantum computing.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "GroverGPT-2: Simulating Grover's Algorithm via Chain-of-Thought Reasoning and Quantum-Native Tokenization":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces GroverGPT-2, an LLM-based method for simulating Grover's algorithm.  It builds upon a previous work (GroverGPT) by incorporating chain-of-thought (CoT) reasoning and a quantum-native tokenization technique. Quantum-native tokenization involves creating a vocabulary tailored to quantum assembly language (QASM) to better represent quantum circuit structure. CoT reasoning allows the model to explicitly demonstrate its simulation steps in a structured and interpretable manner. The authors train GroverGPT-2, an 8 billion parameter LLM, on a dataset of QASM circuits with corresponding CoT annotations using parameter-efficient fine-tuning. The results suggest that GroverGPT-2 can effectively learn and internalize quantum circuit logic and provide high-fidelity simulations. The paper explores the scaling of GroverGPT-2 with increasing qubit numbers and investigates the benefits of the proposed tokenization and CoT techniques.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Novelty:** The paper introduces a novel combination of CoT reasoning and quantum-native tokenization for classical simulation of quantum algorithms. The quantum-native tokenization is well-motivated, as it helps the model process quantum circuits more efficiently than general-purpose tokenizers. Combining LLMs with CoT reasoning for quantum algorithm simulation is an interesting approach to interpretability.
    *   **Methodological Rigor:** The paper presents a well-defined task and uses appropriate evaluation metrics (searching accuracy and fidelity). The experimental setup seems thorough, with ablations performed to assess the contribution of each component. They also consider both full-circuit and oracle-only inputs which is good.
    *   **Results:** The results show promising performance for GroverGPT-2 in simulating Grover's algorithm, particularly compared to baseline LLMs. The scaling experiments provide valuable insights into the limitations and potential of the approach. Demonstrating generalization capabilities beyond the training range is a positive sign.
    *   **Reproducibility:** The authors provide detailed information about the methodology, including the training data generation process, the quantum-native tokenization rules, and the LoRA configurations.
    *   **Impact:** The paper opens up avenues for exploring the capabilities of LLMs in understanding and simulating quantum algorithms. It could inspire new approaches for quantum education and research.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Algorithm Focus:** The paper focuses solely on Grover's algorithm. While a good starting point, it limits the generalizability of the conclusions. It would be beneficial to demonstrate the effectiveness of the approach on other quantum algorithms with different characteristics.
    *   **Scaling Concerns:** While the paper explores the scaling of GroverGPT-2, the scaling experiments are still limited to a relatively small number of qubits (up to 16). The practical applicability of the approach to simulating larger quantum circuits remains unclear. Also, performance starts to degrade beyond the training range.
    *   **LLM's "Understanding" of Quantum Mechanics:**  The paper claims that the LLM "understands" quantum circuit logic. While it's impressive that the model can simulate the algorithm, the extent to which it truly *understands* the underlying quantum mechanics is debatable. It's more likely that the model has learned to map QASM representations to output probability distributions based on the training data. Demonstrating a deeper understanding would require the model to solve more complex quantum reasoning tasks beyond simulation.
    *   **Computational Cost:** Though GroverGPT-2 is more efficient than classical quantum simulations, using LLMs in any real-time simulation task is currently prohibitive and would require significant advancement in the field.
    *   **Lack of Comparison to Existing Classical Simulation Methods:** The paper compares the approach to baseline LLMs and classical simulation techniques. The classical simulation methods chosen for comparison are somewhat basic (state-vector, unitary, density matrix). Comparisons against more advanced simulation methods (e.g., tensor network-based simulations or stabilizer rank methods) would better position GroverGPT-2's performance in the context of state-of-the-art classical simulation.

*   **Justification:**
    The paper offers a novel and interesting direction for exploring the interface between LLMs and quantum computing. The combination of quantum-native tokenization and chain-of-thought reasoning has the potential to improve the efficiency and interpretability of classical quantum algorithm simulation. However, limitations concerning scalability, the focus on a single algorithm, and the interpretation of the model's "understanding" of quantum mechanics prevent a higher score. A lower score would be undeserved, as the results are clearly promising and a considerable effort has been invested into both method and data curation.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: quant-ph
- **Score**: 7/10

### ConCISE: Confidence-guided Compression in Step-by-step Efficient Reasoning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04881v1)
- **Authors**: Ziqing Qiao, Yongheng Deng, Jiali Zeng, Dong Wang, Lai Wei, Fandong Meng, Jie Zhou, Ju Ren, Yaoxue Zhang
- **Abstract**: Large Reasoning Models (LRMs) perform strongly in complex reasoning tasks via Chain-of-Thought (CoT) prompting, but often suffer from verbose outputs caused by redundant content, increasing computational overhead, and degrading user experience. Existing compression methods either operate post-hoc pruning, risking disruption to reasoning coherence, or rely on sampling-based selection, which fails to intervene effectively during generation. In this work, we introduce a confidence-guided perspective to explain the emergence of redundant reflection in LRMs, identifying two key patterns: Confidence Deficit, where the model reconsiders correct steps due to low internal confidence, and Termination Delay, where reasoning continues even after reaching a confident answer. Based on this analysis, we propose ConCISE (Confidence-guided Compression In Step-by-step Efficient Reasoning), a framework that simplifies reasoning chains by reinforcing the model's confidence during inference, thus preventing the generation of redundant reflection steps. It integrates Confidence Injection to stabilize intermediate steps and Early Stopping to terminate reasoning when confidence is sufficient. Extensive experiments demonstrate that fine-tuning LRMs on ConCISE-generated data yields significantly shorter outputs, reducing length by up to approximately 50% under SimPO, while maintaining high task accuracy. ConCISE consistently outperforms existing baselines across multiple reasoning benchmarks.
- **Summary**: Here is a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "ConCISE: Confidence-guided Compression in Step-by-step Efficient Reasoning".

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the issue of verbosity and redundancy in Large Reasoning Models (LRMs) when using Chain-of-Thought (CoT) prompting. The authors argue that existing compression methods are either disruptive or ineffective in intervening during generation. The paper proposes ConCISE, a novel framework that simplifies reasoning chains by reinforcing model confidence during inference. This framework comprises two main components: Confidence Injection, which strengthens the model's belief in intermediate steps, and Early Stopping, which terminates reasoning once sufficient confidence is achieved. The authors analyze the reasoning behavior of LRMs and identify two key patterns that cause verbosity: Confidence Deficit (where the model reconsiders correct steps due to low internal confidence) and Termination Delay (where reasoning continues after a confident answer is reached). They demonstrate the effectiveness of ConCISE through experiments, showing that fine-tuning LRMs with ConCISE-generated data results in shorter outputs with maintained task accuracy.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a novel perspective by explicitly modeling and targeting model confidence as a driver of redundancy in LRM reasoning. Identifying the Confidence Deficit and Termination Delay patterns is insightful. The CONCISE framework itself, combining Confidence Injection and Early Stopping, is a distinct approach. While techniques like pruning, prompting for brevity, and RL-based training exist, this paper's confidence-guided approach appears to be a unique contribution, particularly in the way it intervenes during generation rather than solely as a post-hoc process.

*   **Significance:** The paper has the potential to be significant. Reducing the verbosity of LRMs is crucial for practical applications, improving user experience, reducing computational costs, and enabling deployment in resource-constrained environments. Maintaining high task accuracy while achieving substantial compression is a valuable goal. The demonstration of generalizability to out-of-domain tasks further enhances the paper's potential impact. The analysis of *why* redundancy arises makes CONCISE potentially more adaptable than purely empirical methods.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the problem of verbosity in LRMs and the shortcomings of existing solutions.
    *   **Insightful Analysis:** The identification of Confidence Deficit and Termination Delay provides a deeper understanding of the underlying causes of redundancy.
    *   **Novel Framework:** The ConCISE framework offers a novel and promising approach to address the problem.
    *   **Strong Empirical Results:** The experiments demonstrate the effectiveness of ConCISE in achieving significant compression while maintaining accuracy.
    *   **Generalizability:** The demonstration of strong performance across different benchmarks, including out-of-domain tasks, suggests good generalizability.
    *   **Ablation Study:** The ablation study provides insights into the relative contributions of the two components of CONCISE.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Heuristic Confidence Detector**: The confidence detection, although lightweight, is heuristic, based on a manually created pool of confidence-indicative tokens. The authors acknowledge the model dependency. The framework's robustness and optimal parameter tuning (e.g., the confidence phrase pool, the confidence detector, the thresholds), may be sensitive to model architecture or scale, which will require more thorough analyses in future work.
    *   **Manual Curation:** The confidence phrase pool is created manually. While the authors use experiments to refine the pool, a more automated or data-driven approach could be explored.
    *   **Limited model exploration:** The evaluation mainly centers around the DeepSeek model. Applying CONCISE to other families of LM architectures is crucial to evaluate CONCISE's effectiveness.
    *   **Lack of theoretical guarantee:** Although the experimental results demonstrate the practical effectiveness of CONCISE, the paper lacks theoretical analysis to justify its convergence and optimal trade-off between compression and accuracy.
    *  **Reliance on LLM-as-a-Judge:** Some aspects of evaluation relies on LLM-as-a-Judge, which may introduce biases and inaccuracies.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to influence future research on efficient reasoning with LRMs. The confidence-guided approach may inspire new methods for addressing verbosity and redundancy. The ConCISE framework provides a concrete and effective solution that can be adopted and further developed by other researchers. The identification of Confidence Deficit and Termination Delay can help other researchers better understand the behavior of LRMs and develop more targeted solutions.

*   **Rigorous Rationale for Score:** Although the concept is very insightful and the experiments show strong results, there are certain aspects that can improve the paper, which include model robustness and dependence on a hand-engineered method.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rigorous Rationale:**

The paper presents a **novel and promising** approach to a significant problem in the field of LRM reasoning. The **insightful analysis** of the causes of redundancy and the **effective ConCISE framework** contribute to the field. **Strong empirical results** and demonstration of **generalizability** support the effectiveness of the approach. However, the **reliance on a manually curated confidence phrase pool and the reliance on LLM-as-a-Judge** are limitations. Given the **impact on future research** on efficient reasoning, the paper is given a score of 7.5 to demonstrate both the innovation and room to improve on the generalizability and automated elements. Future work could improve the performance of the CONCISE mechanism with data from a variety of LM architectures.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### SpatialPrompting: Keyframe-driven Zero-Shot Spatial Reasoning with Off-the-Shelf Multimodal Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04911v1)
- **Authors**: Shun Taguchi, Hideki Deguchi, Takumi Hamazaki, Hiroyuki Sakai
- **Abstract**: This study introduces SpatialPrompting, a novel framework that harnesses the emergent reasoning capabilities of off-the-shelf multimodal large language models to achieve zero-shot spatial reasoning in three-dimensional (3D) environments. Unlike existing methods that rely on expensive 3D-specific fine-tuning with specialized 3D inputs such as point clouds or voxel-based features, SpatialPrompting employs a keyframe-driven prompt generation strategy. This framework uses metrics such as vision-language similarity, Mahalanobis distance, field of view, and image sharpness to select a diverse and informative set of keyframes from image sequences and then integrates them with corresponding camera pose data to effectively abstract spatial relationships and infer complex 3D structures. The proposed framework not only establishes a new paradigm for flexible spatial reasoning that utilizes intuitive visual and positional cues but also achieves state-of-the-art zero-shot performance on benchmark datasets, such as ScanQA and SQA3D, across several metrics. The proposed method effectively eliminates the need for specialized 3D inputs and fine-tuning, offering a simpler and more scalable alternative to conventional approaches.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary of the paper along with a critical evaluation:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SpatialPrompting, a novel zero-shot framework for spatial reasoning in 3D environments using off-the-shelf multimodal Large Language Models (LLMs).  Instead of relying on expensive 3D-specific fine-tuning and specialized 3D data inputs (like point clouds or voxels), SpatialPrompting leverages a keyframe-driven prompt generation strategy.  The framework selects a diverse and informative set of keyframes from image sequences using metrics like vision-language similarity, Mahalanobis distance, field of view, and image sharpness. These keyframes, along with their corresponding camera pose data, are then used as prompts to the LLM for spatial reasoning. The authors demonstrate state-of-the-art zero-shot performance on the ScanQA and SQA3D benchmarks, highlighting the framework's ability to understand spatial relationships and infer 3D structures without specialized training or inputs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's core novelty lies in its *training-free* approach to spatial reasoning by effectively prompting existing multimodal LLMs. Bypassing the need for 3D-specific fine-tuning or specialized 3D inputs is a significant departure from conventional methods. This simplifies the pipeline and makes spatial reasoning more accessible and scalable. The keyframe selection process is also novel, as it combines semantic and spatial information to generate informative prompts.
*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a significant bottleneck in spatial reasoning: the computational cost and complexity associated with specialized 3D datasets and fine-tuning. By demonstrating that readily available multimodal LLMs can achieve high performance with carefully crafted prompts, the paper opens up new avenues for research and practical applications. This approach could significantly reduce the barrier to entry for spatial reasoning tasks, making it more accessible to researchers and developers.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Strong Empirical Results:** The paper demonstrates state-of-the-art zero-shot performance on standard benchmarks, providing compelling evidence for the effectiveness of SpatialPrompting.
    *   **Clear and Concise Method:** The explanation of the SpatialPrompting framework is easy to follow, and the individual components are well-defined.
    *   **Ablation Studies:**  The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the contribution of each component of the framework (Keyframe extraction, camera pose, and annotation).
    *   **Qualitative Analysis:** The qualitative results visually demonstrate the framework's ability to handle complex spatial reasoning tasks, including small object localization and multi-object relationship reasoning.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Dependence on LLM Capabilities:** The framework relies heavily on the emergent reasoning capabilities of multimodal LLMs.  Performance is therefore limited by the underlying capabilities of the LLM used, and results might vary depending on the LLM employed. While the paper uses state-of-the-art LLMs, the reliance on these models constitutes a potential limitation for reproducibility and generalization as new LLMs are released.
    *   **Limited Scope of Evaluated Tasks:** While the paper demonstrates strong performance on benchmark datasets, the evaluated tasks are still relatively limited. Exploring the framework's performance on a wider range of spatial reasoning tasks, including more complex and real-world scenarios, would further strengthen the paper.
    *   **User Orientation Challenges**: The paper admits, and the analysis of misdirection demonstrates, that there remain challenges related to understanding user orientation when prompts require direction dependent answers. This should be explored more thoroughly and perhaps with a user-centered frame of reference.
    *   **Potential Practical Challenges**: While the approach seems simple, selecting the right metrics and hyperparameters for keyframe extraction may require substantial tuning in real-world environments. These practical details could present significant challenges to users aiming to implement the method.
    *   **Keyframe Extraction Computational Cost**: Despite the claim of efficiency, the keyframe extraction process does involve pre-computing distances and quality scores for all frame pairs which may become computationally expensive with large and complex video sequences. This isn't really mentioned in the paper.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to significantly influence the field by shifting the focus from specialized 3D models and fine-tuning to prompt engineering for existing multimodal LLMs.  It could inspire new research directions in spatial reasoning and enable more accessible and scalable applications.

**Rigorous Rationale:**

Despite its weaknesses in the area of accounting for user orientation, the novelty and efficiency benefits of SpatialPrompting outweigh the drawbacks related to its reliance on underlying LLM capabilities. It significantly contributes to reducing the complexity associated with other methods and has implications for a wide variety of spatial reasoning tasks. The use of readily available technologies such as pre-trained LLMs makes this both more sustainable and scalable than other, more specialized approaches. Thus a "6" is well justified.

**Score: 6**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### GlyphMastero: A Glyph Encoder for High-Fidelity Scene Text Editing
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04915v1)
- **Authors**: Tong Wang, Ting Liu, Xiaochao Qu, Chengjing Wu, Luoqi Liu, Xiaolin Hu
- **Abstract**: Scene text editing, a subfield of image editing, requires modifying texts in images while preserving style consistency and visual coherence with the surrounding environment. While diffusion-based methods have shown promise in text generation, they still struggle to produce high-quality results. These methods often generate distorted or unrecognizable characters, particularly when dealing with complex characters like Chinese. In such systems, characters are composed of intricate stroke patterns and spatial relationships that must be precisely maintained. We present GlyphMastero, a specialized glyph encoder designed to guide the latent diffusion model for generating texts with stroke-level precision. Our key insight is that existing methods, despite using pretrained OCR models for feature extraction, fail to capture the hierarchical nature of text structures - from individual strokes to stroke-level interactions to overall character-level structure. To address this, our glyph encoder explicitly models and captures the cross-level interactions between local-level individual characters and global-level text lines through our novel glyph attention module. Meanwhile, our model implements a feature pyramid network to fuse the multi-scale OCR backbone features at the global-level. Through these cross-level and multi-scale fusions, we obtain more detailed glyph-aware guidance, enabling precise control over the scene text generation process. Our method achieves an 18.02\% improvement in sentence accuracy over the state-of-the-art multi-lingual scene text editing baseline, while simultaneously reducing the text-region Fr\'echet inception distance by 53.28\%.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces GlyphMastero, a novel glyph encoder designed to improve the fidelity of scene text editing, particularly for complex scripts like Chinese. The core idea is to explicitly model the hierarchical structure of text, from individual strokes to character-level composition to the overall text line, through a glyph attention module and a feature pyramid network. This addresses limitations of previous diffusion-based methods that often produce distorted or unrecognizable characters due to insufficient representation of intricate glyph structures. The method demonstrates improved sentence accuracy and reduced text-region Fréchet inception distance compared to state-of-the-art baselines.  The paper integrates this glyph encoder into an inpainting-based diffusion model.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The key novelty lies in the explicit hierarchical modeling of glyph structures within the glyph encoder. Existing methods often use off-the-shelf OCR features, but GlyphMastero learns to extract and fuse features at different levels (stroke, character, line). This explicit modeling is a valuable contribution, especially for complex writing systems. The use of a feature pyramid network for global text structure representation is also well integrated.

*   **Significance:** Improving the quality of scene text editing is significant because it addresses a practical problem with applications in image editing, design, and content creation. The improvements demonstrated on complex scripts are particularly valuable, suggesting a broader applicability of the method. Quantitatively, the paper claims meaningful gains over prior methods. Qualitatively, the provided examples showcase superior stroke accuracy and style preservation.  The paper contributes a specialized module demonstrating that improvements can be made within the "guidance" area of text editing architectures.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear and well-motivated problem statement.
    *   Novel architecture for glyph encoding with hierarchical modeling.
    *   Strong quantitative and qualitative results, especially for complex scripts.
    *   Ablation studies demonstrating the importance of individual components.
    *   Detailed implementation details for reproducibility.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the hierarchical modeling of glyphs is novel, the underlying diffusion-based architecture and the inpainting formulation are largely based on prior work (DiffUTE). The primary contribution is the glyph encoder.
    *   The paper admits that longer text generation still lags behind shorter text, indicating a limitation of the approach. The resolution of training data likely contributes to this.
    *   The reliance on PaddleOCR-v4 might limit adoption if other OCR models offer superior performance or broader support in other frameworks. The model is dependent on the underlying OCR.
    *   The effectiveness of the proposed method relies on the initial text detection and segmentation. Failures in those initial steps could propagate to the editing stage.

*   **Potential Influence:** GlyphMastero could influence future research by highlighting the importance of explicit glyph modeling in scene text editing. It might inspire other researchers to explore different ways of representing and encoding text structures for generation tasks. However, its impact is tempered by the incremental nature of the improvement and its reliance on a specific diffusion model architecture. The focus on hierarchical modeling contributes meaningfully and offers a direction for future work. The improved results over existing methods are substantial and represent progress in the field.

**Overall:** GlyphMastero introduces a novel and effective approach for glyph encoding in scene text editing. While it builds upon existing diffusion-based methods, the explicit hierarchical modeling of glyphs is a significant contribution that improves text quality and style preservation, particularly for complex scripts. The results are compelling, and the ablation studies provide valuable insights into the importance of individual components. However, the limitations regarding longer text and OCR dependency prevent a higher score.

### Perception, Reason, Think, and Plan: A Survey on Large Multimodal Reasoning Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04921v1)
- **Authors**: Yunxin Li, Zhenyu Liu, Zitao Li, Xuanyu Zhang, Zhenran Xu, Xinyu Chen, Haoyuan Shi, Shenyuan Jiang, Xintong Wang, Jifang Wang, Shouzheng Huang, Xinping Zhao, Borui Jiang, Lanqing Hong, Longyue Wang, Zhuotao Tian, Baoxing Huai, Wenhan Luo, Weihua Luo, Zheng Zhang, Baotian Hu, Min Zhang
- **Abstract**: Reasoning lies at the heart of intelligence, shaping the ability to make decisions, draw conclusions, and generalize across domains. In artificial intelligence, as systems increasingly operate in open, uncertain, and multimodal environments, reasoning becomes essential for enabling robust and adaptive behavior. Large Multimodal Reasoning Models (LMRMs) have emerged as a promising paradigm, integrating modalities such as text, images, audio, and video to support complex reasoning capabilities and aiming to achieve comprehensive perception, precise understanding, and deep reasoning. As research advances, multimodal reasoning has rapidly evolved from modular, perception-driven pipelines to unified, language-centric frameworks that offer more coherent cross-modal understanding. While instruction tuning and reinforcement learning have improved model reasoning, significant challenges remain in omni-modal generalization, reasoning depth, and agentic behavior. To address these issues, we present a comprehensive and structured survey of multimodal reasoning research, organized around a four-stage developmental roadmap that reflects the field's shifting design philosophies and emerging capabilities. First, we review early efforts based on task-specific modules, where reasoning was implicitly embedded across stages of representation, alignment, and fusion. Next, we examine recent approaches that unify reasoning into multimodal LLMs, with advances such as Multimodal Chain-of-Thought (MCoT) and multimodal reinforcement learning enabling richer and more structured reasoning chains. Finally, drawing on empirical insights from challenging benchmarks and experimental cases of OpenAI O3 and O4-mini, we discuss the conceptual direction of native large multimodal reasoning models (N-LMRMs), which aim to support scalable, agentic, and adaptive reasoning and planning in complex, real-world environments.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper, aiming to provide a rigorous rationale for the assigned score:

**Summary:**

The paper is a comprehensive survey on Large Multimodal Reasoning Models (LMRMs). It structures the field around a four-stage developmental roadmap:
1.  **Perception-Driven Modular Reasoning**: Early efforts relying on task-specific modules for representation, alignment, and fusion.
2.  **Language-Centric Short Reasoning (System 1)**: Emergence of prompt-based and structured reasoning in multimodal LLMs, emphasizing surface-level understanding.
3.  **Language-Centric Long Reasoning (System 2)**: Exploration of extended reasoning chains and reinforcement learning to enable long-horizon thinking and planning.
4.  **Native LMRMs (N-LMRMs)**: A prospective paradigm shift where reasoning is natively integrated, emphasizing omnimodal perception, agentic behavior, and adaptive planning.

The survey identifies limitations in current models regarding omnimodal generalization, reasoning depth, and agentic behaviors. It highlights emerging trends like instruction tuning, reinforcement learning, and the development of unified representations and benchmark construction as key to future progress. It offers a taxonomy of models, datasets, and benchmarks, and projects future directions for N-LMRMs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its structured approach to surveying the evolution of LMRMs, organizing them into distinct stages characterized by design philosophies and capabilities. While previous surveys may have touched on aspects of MLLMs or reasoning, this paper offers a more holistic and chronologically organized view of the entire roadmap, filling a gap in the need for a coherent framework. Additionally, the introduction of "Native LMRMs" is forward-looking and offers a clear goal for future research.
*   **Significance:**  The significance stems from its comprehensive nature and the synthesis of trends. It addresses limitations of current models and contextualizes a vast amount of literature (540+ publications). This provides researchers with a valuable resource for understanding the landscape and identifying promising areas for future work.  The identification of two transformative capabilities 1) Multimodal Agentic Reasoning and 2) Omni-Modal Understanding and Generative Reasoning are also important directions for the community.  The reorganisation of existing datasets and benchmarks into clear categories also enhances the usefulness of the review for the community.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Scope:** The breadth of coverage across different models, datasets, and benchmarks is impressive.
    *   **Clear Structure:**  The four-stage roadmap provides a compelling narrative for the field's evolution.
    *   **Forward-Looking Perspective:**  The discussion of N-LMRMs and future research directions is valuable for guiding future development.
    *   **Practical Utility:** The reorganized dataset and benchmark information is extremely useful for researchers.
    *   **Timeliness:** The survey appears to be up-to-date (given the inclusion of papers to 2025.04) which further strengthens the significance and insights of the review.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Technical Depth on N-LMRMs**: As the N-LMRMs paradigm is prospective, the section is primarily conceptual and less grounded in concrete technical details compared to the earlier stages.  This is understandable given the early stage of development.
    *   **Potential for Over-Simplification:**  Any attempt to categorize a complex field risks oversimplification. While the stages are generally well-defined, some overlap and nuances within each stage might be missed.
    *   **Subjectivity in Category Definitions:** The assignment of models to specific stages could be subjective, requiring further refinement. However, the categories used are generally well explained and representative of the field.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has a high potential to influence the field by:
    *   **Providing a shared understanding:**  The roadmap and taxonomy will help researchers contextualize their work and understand the relationships between different approaches.
    *   **Identifying key challenges:**  The highlighting of limitations and future research directions will focus attention on critical areas.
    *   **Facilitating collaboration:**  The survey will make it easier for researchers to identify relevant work and collaborators.

**Justification for Score:**

Given its comprehensive nature, novel structuring of the field, clear articulation of current limitations, and forward-looking perspective, this survey makes a valuable contribution. While the prospective nature of the N-LMRMs section presents some limitation, the survey provides clear potential future direction for the area. The thorough taxonomy and dataset re-organization contribute to the paper being a valuable resource for researchers. Therefore, the rating is justified as:
