### X-Driver: Explainable Autonomous Driving with Vision-Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.05098v1)
- **Authors**: Wei Liu, Jiyuan Zhang, Binxiong Zheng, Yufeng Hu, Yingzhan Lin, Zengfeng Zeng
- **Abstract**: End-to-end autonomous driving has advanced significantly, offering benefits such as system simplicity and stronger driving performance in both open-loop and closed-loop settings than conventional pipelines. However, existing frameworks still suffer from low success rates in closed-loop evaluations, highlighting their limitations in real-world deployment. In this paper, we introduce X-Driver, a unified multi-modal large language models(MLLMs) framework designed for closed-loop autonomous driving, leveraging Chain-of-Thought(CoT) and autoregressive modeling to enhance perception and decision-making. We validate X-Driver across multiple autonomous driving tasks using public benchmarks in CARLA simulation environment, including Bench2Drive[6]. Our experimental results demonstrate superior closed-loop performance, surpassing the current state-of-the-art(SOTA) while improving the interpretability of driving decisions. These findings underscore the importance of structured reasoning in end-to-end driving and establish X-Driver as a strong baseline for future research in closed-loop autonomous driving.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "X-Driver: Explainable Autonomous Driving with Vision-Language Models":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces X-Driver, an end-to-end autonomous driving framework using multimodal large language models (MLLMs). X-Driver employs Chain-of-Thought (CoT) reasoning and autoregressive modeling to improve perception and decision-making. The framework is tested in the CARLA simulation environment using benchmarks like Bench2Drive. The results show improved closed-loop performance and better interpretability compared to state-of-the-art methods. The paper highlights the importance of structured reasoning in end-to-end driving and positions X-Driver as a baseline for future research in closed-loop autonomous driving. The key contributions include the integration of MLLMs and CoT for autonomous driving and a unified closed-loop framework supporting multi-modal data inputs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Interpretability:** The use of Chain-of-Thought reasoning is a significant strength. This provides transparency into the decision-making process, which is crucial for safety-critical applications like autonomous driving. This helps to address a key limitation of many end-to-end approaches, which often lack explainability.
    *   **Multi-modal Fusion:** X-Driver's ability to handle multiple modalities (vision, language) is another advantage. It surpasses traditional systems that rely on fixed-format sensor inputs and better adapts to diverse driving environments.
    *   **Closed-Loop Performance:** The improved closed-loop performance compared to existing methods is a solid result. The quantitative evaluation demonstrates the effectiveness of the proposed framework. The ablation study further emphasizes the importance of incorporating CoT.
    *   **Well-written:** The paper is clearly written and well-structured, explaining the methodology and experimental setup in sufficient detail.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Novelty:** While the combination of MLLMs and CoT for autonomous driving is promising, the individual components are not entirely novel. DriveCoT already introduced CoT in a driving context. The main novelty lies in the specific architecture and the experimental validation of its effectiveness. This aspect could be better highlighted in the introduction and related work.
    *   **Scope of Experiments:** The experiments are conducted entirely in simulation. While CARLA is a widely used simulator, the results may not directly translate to real-world performance. The paper should acknowledge the simulation-to-reality gap and discuss potential challenges in deploying X-Driver in real-world scenarios.
    *   **Comparison to Baselines:** The comparison to UniAD is good, but more discussion about *why* X-Driver outperforms it would strengthen the analysis. What specific aspects of the architecture or training procedure contribute to the superior performance?
    *   **Lack of Detail in Specific CoT Prompts:** While the paper discusses the CoT approach, it lacks a detailed presentation of the specific prompts used during training and inference. Providing these examples would enhance the reproducibility and understanding of the proposed method. The paper mentions the breakdown of the task into four subtasks but doesn't showcase the structure of the prompt data.
    *   **Hyperparameter tuning and ablation studies details** There isn't much detail of tuning the hyperparameters or ablation studies for different MLLM.
*   **Significance:**

    The paper contributes to the growing body of research on end-to-end autonomous driving using MLLMs. The focus on explainability is particularly important. If the simulation results generalize to real-world scenarios, X-Driver could lead to safer and more reliable autonomous driving systems. The paper provides a strong baseline for future research in this area.
    The paper lacks novelty from the perspective of method as using LLaVA with CoT has been explored in other tasks.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper is a solid contribution to the field, but its novelty is incremental rather than groundbreaking. The clear writing, emphasis on explainability, and strong simulation results are valuable. However, the lack of detailed prompt examples, the reliance solely on simulation, and a lack of comprehensive comparison with prior art limit the paper's overall impact. The CoT structure applied with LLMs isn't significantly novel, however, the application of CoT for autonomous driving decision-making is. The main value is the engineering approach and experiments for autonomous driving.

### MDE-Edit: Masked Dual-Editing for Multi-Object Image Editing via Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.05101v1)
- **Authors**: Hongyang Zhu, Haipeng Liu, Bo Fu, Yang Wang
- **Abstract**: Multi-object editing aims to modify multiple objects or regions in complex scenes while preserving structural coherence. This task faces significant challenges in scenarios involving overlapping or interacting objects: (1) Inaccurate localization of target objects due to attention misalignment, leading to incomplete or misplaced edits; (2) Attribute-object mismatch, where color or texture changes fail to align with intended regions due to cross-attention leakage, creating semantic conflicts (\textit{e.g.}, color bleeding into non-target areas). Existing methods struggle with these challenges: approaches relying on global cross-attention mechanisms suffer from attention dilution and spatial interference between objects, while mask-based methods fail to bind attributes to geometrically accurate regions due to feature entanglement in multi-object scenarios. To address these limitations, we propose a training-free, inference-stage optimization approach that enables precise localized image manipulation in complex multi-object scenes, named MDE-Edit. MDE-Edit optimizes the noise latent feature in diffusion models via two key losses: Object Alignment Loss (OAL) aligns multi-layer cross-attention with segmentation masks for precise object positioning, and Color Consistency Loss (CCL) amplifies target attribute attention within masks while suppressing leakage to adjacent regions. This dual-loss design ensures localized and coherent multi-object edits. Extensive experiments demonstrate that MDE-Edit outperforms state-of-the-art methods in editing accuracy and visual quality, offering a robust solution for complex multi-object image manipulation tasks.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "MDE-Edit: Masked Dual-Editing for Multi-Object Image Editing via Diffusion Models" introduces a novel framework for precisely editing multiple objects in images using diffusion models. The core idea is to address the challenges of inaccurate localization and attribute-object mismatch that arise when editing complex scenes. The framework, MDE-Edit, employs a "Masked Dual Edit" strategy, which combines two key loss functions: Object Alignment Loss (OAL), which ensures accurate object placement by aligning cross-attention with segmentation masks, and Color Consistency Loss (CCL), which enforces color consistency within masked regions while suppressing color bleeding to adjacent areas. The method optimizes the noise latent feature in diffusion models using these losses, allowing for localized and coherent edits. The paper presents qualitative and quantitative results demonstrating MDE-Edit's superior performance compared to state-of-the-art methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Addresses a Specific Problem:** The paper tackles a clearly defined and important problem in image editing: multi-object editing with localized and coherent changes. This is a relevant task with many practical applications.
*   **Novel Approach:**  The proposed Masked Dual-Edit strategy is technically sound. The combination of OAL and CCL provides a mechanism for decoupling structural and appearance edits, which addresses the identified limitations of existing methods. The concept of attention injection from the reconstruction branch is also well-designed to maintain structural integrity.
*   **Strong Empirical Validation:** The paper provides comprehensive experimental results on standard datasets. The qualitative comparisons visually demonstrate the improvements achieved by MDE-Edit. Quantitative metrics (CLIP Score, BG-LPIPS, BG-SSIM) provide further support for the method's effectiveness.  The ablation studies are important to justify the selection of OAL and CCL, and the analyses of CA maps are helpful for visualizing the contributions of each loss term.
*   **Technical Clarity:** The paper is generally well-written and explains the method in sufficient detail. The architecture is clearly illustrated in Figure 2, and the loss functions are formally defined.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Incremental Novelty:** While the combination of techniques is effective, the individual components are not entirely new.  The paper builds upon existing ideas of cross-attention manipulation, mask-based editing, and diffusion models.  The key innovation lies in the specific combination and refinement of these techniques for the multi-object editing task. The "disassembly then reassembly" strategy that OIR pioneered sounds similar to the proposed approach.
*   **Computational Cost:** Optimizing latent variables during inference, as the paper describes, can be computationally expensive. The runtime and computational requirements of MDE-Edit are not explicitly discussed. The practicality of the method for large-scale image editing tasks may be a concern.
*   **Dependence on Mask Quality:** Although the paper acknowledges the challenges associated with complex object interactions, performance will likely be dependent on the quality of the initial segmentation masks, especially when objects have intricate spatial relationships or substantial overlap. The dependence on SAM is not entirely advantageous, because the quality of that segmenter influences the method's performance.
*   **Limited Analysis of Failure Cases:** While the paper presents some failure cases, a more detailed discussion of the types of edits that MDE-Edit struggles with would be valuable.  Specifically, how does the approach deal with edits requiring significant changes in object geometry or pose? Are there object categories or scene types for which MDE-Edit performs poorly?

**Significance:**

The paper contributes to the field of image editing by providing a robust solution for the challenging task of multi-object editing. The Masked Dual-Edit strategy effectively addresses the limitations of existing methods, enabling more precise and coherent edits in complex scenes. The MDE-Edit framework has the potential to be a valuable tool for creative image manipulation and content creation. The approach provides useful insight for further explorations in multi-object editing.

**Justification of Score:**

Considering the strengths and weaknesses, the paper is a valuable contribution, but the novelty is somewhat incremental. The core idea of combining existing techniques (cross-attention manipulation, masked losses, diffusion models) in a novel way is well-executed and yields strong results. However, it lacks revolutionary concepts. The improvements are significant compared to state-of-the-art methods, but the dependence on good mask quality and potentially high computational costs could limit its impact. Therefore, a high but not exceptional score is appropriate.

### A Weighted Byzantine Fault Tolerance Consensus Driven Trusted Multiple Large Language Models Network
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.05103v1)
- **Authors**: Haoxiang Luo, Gang Sun, Yinqiu Liu, Dongcheng Zhao, Dusit Niyato, Hongfang Yu, Schahram Dustdar
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) have achieved remarkable success across a wide range of applications. However, individual LLMs often produce inconsistent, biased, or hallucinated outputs due to limitations in their training corpora and model architectures. Recently, collaborative frameworks such as the Multi-LLM Network (MultiLLMN) have been introduced, enabling multiple LLMs to interact and jointly respond to user queries. Nevertheless, MultiLLMN architectures raise critical concerns regarding the reliability and security of the generated content, particularly in open environments where malicious or compromised LLMs may be present. Moreover, reliance on centralized coordination undermines system efficiency and introduces single points of failure. In this paper, we propose a novel Trusted MultiLLMN framework, driven by a Weighted Byzantine Fault Tolerance (WBFT) blockchain consensus mechanism, to ensure the reliability, security, and efficiency of multi-LLM collaboration. In WBFT, voting weights are adaptively assigned to each LLM based on its response quality and trustworthiness, incentivizing reliable behavior, and reducing the impact of malicious nodes. Extensive simulations demonstrate that WBFT significantly improves both consensus security and efficiency compared to classical and modern consensus mechanisms, particularly under wireless network conditions. Furthermore, our evaluations reveal that Trusted MultiLLMN supported by WBFT can deliver higher-quality and more credible responses than both single LLMs and conventional MultiLLMNs, thereby providing a promising path toward building robust, decentralized AI collaboration networks.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenges of inconsistency, bias, and potential malicious activity in Large Language Models (LLMs) by proposing a Trusted Multi-LLM Network (MultiLLMN) framework. This framework leverages a Weighted Byzantine Fault Tolerance (WBFT) blockchain consensus mechanism to ensure the reliability, security, and efficiency of collaborative LLM responses. The WBFT assigns adaptive voting weights to each LLM based on response quality and trustworthiness, incentivizing reliable behavior and mitigating the impact of malicious nodes. Simulations demonstrate the superiority of WBFT in terms of security and efficiency compared to traditional consensus mechanisms. The authors show that their Trusted MultiLLMN delivers higher-quality, more credible responses compared to single LLMs and conventional MultiLLMNs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**  The core idea of using a WBFT-based blockchain to secure a MultiLLMN is moderately novel.  While blockchain for LLMs has been explored (as the paper acknowledges), applying WBFT, and especially a *weighted* version, to the *consensus* of multiple LLMs is a distinct contribution. The dynamic clustering (HSC) adds another layer of novelty, although clustering algorithms are well-established. The specific combination of these elements within the MultiLLMN context constitutes a novel contribution.

**Significance:** The work addresses a very relevant and timely problem: ensuring the trustworthiness and reliability of LLM-generated content.  The increasing reliance on LLMs in various applications makes this a critical concern.  Securing multi-LLM systems through decentralized consensus mechanisms is a significant step towards more robust and trustworthy AI. The performance evaluation demonstrates the benefits of their approach, which strengthens the paper's significance.  A significant aspect contributing to the significance is the consideration of wireless network constraints, indicating real-world applicability.

**Strengths:**

*   **Addresses a critical problem:** LLM reliability and security are paramount as LLMs become more pervasive.
*   **Well-defined framework:**  The paper presents a clear architecture and workflow for the Trusted MultiLLMN.
*   **Adaptive Weighting:** The core idea of adaptive weighting based on response quality and trust adds to the effectiveness of the system.
*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The simulations are extensive and compare the proposed WBFT-driven MultiLLMN to relevant baselines under various conditions and including different LLMs. This enhances the credibility of the results.
*   **Considers Real-World Constraints:** Addresses challenges specific to wireless network deployments, enhancing practical relevance.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Reliance on LLM's Self-Assessment:** The framework depends on LLMs' ability to assess the quality of their own responses (and others') - a capability that can be unreliable and prone to biases. While the WBFT aims to mitigate this, this foundational assumption introduces vulnerability. The specific questions designed to address this self-assessment can further be explained.
*   **Complexity:** The addition of blockchain consensus, dynamic clustering, and adaptive weighting introduces considerable complexity to the system. While the paper analyzes complexity, the deployment cost and overhead might be a barrier to practical implementation. The scalability of the proposed model must also be evaluated in more extensive experiments.
*   **Trust Model Assumptions:** While the trust model considers the "probability of malicious behavior," there is no mention of incentives for participating as honest nodes, and what happens if there are many nodes with low "trust value". This lack of incentives might deter more trustworthy nodes and affect consensus quality.
*   **Limited Practical Validation:** The evaluation relies primarily on simulations. Real-world deployments and empirical evaluations with actual user interactions would significantly strengthen the work.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper is a solid contribution with moderate novelty addressing an important and growing problem. The use of weighted BFT is a promising direction, and the evaluation provides strong evidence of the framework's potential. However, the reliance on potentially flawed LLM self-assessment, the increased system complexity, limited exploration of trust model incentives, and the lack of practical validation temper the overall impact.

### Multi-agent Embodied AI: Advances and Future Directions
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.05108v1)
- **Authors**: Zhaohan Feng, Ruiqi Xue, Lei Yuan, Yang Yu, Ning Ding, Meiqin Liu, Bingzhao Gao, Jian Sun, Gang Wang
- **Abstract**: Embodied artificial intelligence (Embodied AI) plays a pivotal role in the application of advanced technologies in the intelligent era, where AI systems are integrated with physical bodies that enable them to perceive, reason, and interact with their environments. Through the use of sensors for input and actuators for action, these systems can learn and adapt based on real-world feedback, allowing them to perform tasks effectively in dynamic and unpredictable environments. As techniques such as deep learning (DL), reinforcement learning (RL), and large language models (LLMs) mature, embodied AI has become a leading field in both academia and industry, with applications spanning robotics, healthcare, transportation, and manufacturing. However, most research has focused on single-agent systems that often assume static, closed environments, whereas real-world embodied AI must navigate far more complex scenarios. In such settings, agents must not only interact with their surroundings but also collaborate with other agents, necessitating sophisticated mechanisms for adaptation, real-time learning, and collaborative problem-solving. Despite increasing interest in multi-agent systems, existing research remains narrow in scope, often relying on simplified models that fail to capture the full complexity of dynamic, open environments for multi-agent embodied AI. Moreover, no comprehensive survey has systematically reviewed the advancements in this area. As embodied AI rapidly evolves, it is crucial to deepen our understanding of multi-agent embodied AI to address the challenges presented by real-world applications. To fill this gap and foster further development in the field, this paper reviews the current state of research, analyzes key contributions, and identifies challenges and future directions, providing insights to guide innovation and progress in this field.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper provides a survey of research in multi-agent embodied AI. It first defines embodied AI and highlights the shift from single-agent to multi-agent systems, emphasizing the increased complexity due to the need for collaboration, adaptation, and real-time learning. It reviews core techniques, including optimal control, reinforcement learning (RL), multi-agent RL (MARL), hierarchical learning, imitation learning (IL), and generative models. It then surveys existing research in both single-agent and multi-agent embodied AI, focusing on control and planning methods, learning-based methods, and generative model-based methods. The paper concludes by discussing current challenges and future research directions, including the need for new theoretical frameworks, algorithm designs, effective learning strategies, generalizable AI frameworks, and adaptation to open environments. Finally, it emphasizes the importance of rigorous evaluation and verification through new benchmarks, with a focus on areas like safety and ethical considerations.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty and Significance:** The paper tackles a crucial and timely topic.  While there are surveys on embodied AI and MARL separately, a comprehensive review specifically addressing multi-agent *embodied* AI is a significant contribution.  The survey serves to consolidate fragmented research, highlighting the unique challenges and opportunities in this specific intersection. Its strength lies in identifying the gap and offering a structured overview.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Comprehensive Coverage:**  The paper attempts to cover a wide range of methods and approaches within multi-agent embodied AI, providing a valuable overview for researchers entering the field or seeking a broader understanding.
    * **Clear Structure:** The organization is logical, moving from foundational concepts to single-agent systems, then to the more complex multi-agent domain, and finally to future directions.
    * **Timely Contribution:** The survey comes at a point where both embodied AI and multi-agent systems are experiencing significant advancements, particularly with the rise of large language models (LLMs), making its analysis particularly relevant.
    * **Identified Gaps:**  The paper accurately points out the limitations of current research, such as the over-reliance on simplified models, and the need for specialized benchmarks for multi-agent embodied AI.
    * **Future Directions:** The identified future directions offer valuable guidance for the research community.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Depth of Analysis:** The survey attempts broad coverage, which means the depth of analysis for each method or benchmark might be limited. A more focused review with deeper dives into specific sub-areas could be more impactful. It could also benefit from more critical comparison between methods. For instance, the benefits and drawbacks of different generative models are mentioned in their introductory section, but later sections that discuss these models don't critically address their strengths and weaknesses within the context of multi-agent embodied AI.
    * **Limited Critical Evaluation of Benchmarks:** While the paper summarizes several benchmarks, it doesn't provide a rigorous *critical* evaluation of their suitability for specific multi-agent embodied AI tasks.  A more in-depth discussion about the limitations and biases of existing benchmarks would enhance its value.
    * **Uneven Coverage:**  Some sub-areas might be covered in greater detail than others.  For instance, there's a stronger emphasis on LLMs than, say, more traditional coordination algorithms in MARL.  This could reflect a bias toward recent trends.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field by:
    * **Guiding Future Research:**  By identifying key challenges and future directions, it can help researchers focus their efforts on the most promising areas.
    * **Promoting Standardization:**  The emphasis on the need for new benchmarks can spur the development of standardized evaluation tools, which are crucial for comparing different approaches.
    * **Facilitating Collaboration:** By providing a common understanding of the field, the survey can foster collaboration among researchers from different backgrounds.

* **Justification for Score:**

I'm assigning a score of **7**.

**Rationale:**

The paper is valuable and timely. It effectively identifies a critical gap in the literature and provides a good overview of the landscape of multi-agent embodied AI. It identifies meaningful future directions. The identified future directions offer valuable guidance for the research community, however, it falls short of offering truly *novel* insights or critical analysis. In particular, the evaluation of benchmarks lacks depth. Moreover, some parts of the review could benefit from more critical analysis. This limits the paper's potential for transformative impact on the field.  A higher score would require the paper to offer deeper analysis, a more critical evaluation of the literature, and perhaps a more targeted focus on a specific sub-area within multi-agent embodied AI.

### Unveiling Language-Specific Features in Large Language Models via Sparse Autoencoders
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.05111v1)
- **Authors**: Boyi Deng, Yu Wan, Yidan Zhang, Baosong Yang, Fuli Feng
- **Abstract**: The mechanisms behind multilingual capabilities in Large Language Models (LLMs) have been examined using neuron-based or internal-activation-based methods. However, these methods often face challenges such as superposition and layer-wise activation variance, which limit their reliability. Sparse Autoencoders (SAEs) offer a more nuanced analysis by decomposing the activations of LLMs into sparse linear combination of SAE features. We introduce a novel metric to assess the monolinguality of features obtained from SAEs, discovering that some features are strongly related to specific languages. Additionally, we show that ablating these SAE features only significantly reduces abilities in one language of LLMs, leaving others almost unaffected. Interestingly, we find some languages have multiple synergistic SAE features, and ablating them together yields greater improvement than ablating individually. Moreover, we leverage these SAE-derived language-specific features to enhance steering vectors, achieving control over the language generated by LLMs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and evaluation of the paper, focusing on critical assessment:

**Summary:**

The paper "Unveiling Language-Specific Features in Large Language Models via Sparse Autoencoders" investigates the multilingual capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs) by employing Sparse Autoencoders (SAEs). The authors propose a new metric to assess the "monolinguality" of SAE features and identify features strongly associated with specific languages.  They demonstrate that ablating these language-specific SAE features primarily affects the performance of the LLM in the targeted language, while leaving other languages largely unaffected. Furthermore, they discover synergistic effects between certain features and leverage these language-specific features to improve steering vectors, achieving more controlled language generation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a useful application of SAEs for analyzing multilingual LLMs. Using SAEs to dissect and understand LLMs isn't entirely new (Bricken et al., Cunningham et al. are referenced), but the focus on *multilingual* capabilities and the specific metric for "monolinguality" are novel contributions. Using identified SAE features to control steering vectors is also a new approach. The idea of investigating feature synergy through ablation is a good one.
*   **Significance:** Understanding and controlling multilingual capabilities in LLMs is important. The finding that specific SAE features correspond to individual languages is significant as it provides insight into how LLMs internally represent multiple languages. Demonstrating targeted ablation is also helpful. Further, steering vector improvements are valuable practically.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Scope:** The experiments are mainly confined to the Flores-10 dataset. The results should be verified on other datasets, especially those with more realistic, diverse multilingual scenarios.
*   **Superficial Analysis:** The experiments are somewhat limited. For example, the "code-switching" analysis is very basic. A more in-depth analysis of how these features are actually *used* within the LLM would significantly enhance the paper.  Are they involved in translation, reasoning, or simply token generation?
*   **Metric for Monolinguality:** While introducing a metric is a good thing, a greater explanation of its properties or the motivation behind that specific equation would be beneficial. More broadly, the notion of "monolinguality" could be better defined and justified. It's not entirely clear that high v necessarily corresponds to a *meaningful* language-specific feature.
*   **Ablation Limitations:** While directional ablation is employed, what criteria is used when selecting the *top* two most language-specific features for every language? Are these features very consistently selected across samples?
*   **Impact of Steering Vectors:** How sensitive is this method to different configurations? How does it perform across different architectures and model sizes? Is it comparable to related methods in terms of metrics?

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Methodology:** The paper clearly outlines the methodology, making it relatively easy to understand and reproduce the experiments.
*   **Interesting Findings:** The observations regarding language-specific features and their synergistic relationships are potentially valuable. Using SAEs to manipulate language generation is a promising line of work.
*   **SAE Benefits:** The paper persuasively argues the benefits of using SAEs (specifically interpretability) in analyzing LLMs.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper is a good step towards understanding multilingual LLMs using SAEs. However, the experimental scope is limited, analysis is superficial, leaving key questions unanswered. A deeper investigation of the *functionality* of these features and a broader evaluation would significantly increase its impact. These considerations lead to a moderately positive, but not exceptional, evaluation.

### MDAA-Diff: CT-Guided Multi-Dose Adaptive Attention Diffusion Model for PET Denoising
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.05112v1)
- **Authors**: Xiaolong Niu, Zanting Ye, Xu Han, Yanchao Huang, Hao Sun, Hubing Wu, Lijun Lu
- **Abstract**: Acquiring high-quality Positron Emission Tomography (PET) images requires administering high-dose radiotracers, which increases radiation exposure risks. Generating standard-dose PET (SPET) from low-dose PET (LPET) has become a potential solution. However, previous studies have primarily focused on single low-dose PET denoising, neglecting two critical factors: discrepancies in dose response caused by inter-patient variability, and complementary anatomical constraints derived from CT images. In this work, we propose a novel CT-Guided Multi-dose Adaptive Attention Denoising Diffusion Model (MDAA-Diff) for multi-dose PET denoising. Our approach integrates anatomical guidance and dose-level adaptation to achieve superior denoising performance under low-dose conditions. Specifically, this approach incorporates a CT-Guided High-frequency Wavelet Attention (HWA) module, which uses wavelet transforms to separate high-frequency anatomical boundary features from CT images. These extracted features are then incorporated into PET imaging through an adaptive weighted fusion mechanism to enhance edge details. Additionally, we propose the Dose-Adaptive Attention (DAA) module, a dose-conditioned enhancement mechanism that dynamically integrates dose levels into channel-spatial attention weight calculation. Extensive experiments on 18F-FDG and 68Ga-FAPI datasets demonstrate that MDAA-Diff outperforms state-of-the-art approaches in preserving diagnostic quality under reduced-dose conditions. Our code is publicly available.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MDAA-Diff, a CT-Guided Multi-Dose Adaptive Attention Diffusion Model, designed for denoising low-dose PET (LPET) images. The model addresses the limitations of existing methods by considering both inter-patient variability in dose response and the complementary anatomical information available from CT images. MDAA-Diff incorporates a CT-Guided High-frequency Wavelet Attention (HWA) module to extract anatomical boundary features and a Dose-Adaptive Attention (DAA) module to dynamically integrate dose levels into the denoising process. Experiments on 18F-FDG and 68Ga-FAPI datasets demonstrate superior denoising performance compared to state-of-the-art approaches, particularly in preserving diagnostic quality under reduced-dose conditions.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper exhibits several novel aspects.
    *   The integration of CT-guided anatomical information using a High-frequency Wavelet Attention (HWA) module is a significant contribution. Existing methods often overlook the valuable spatial constraints offered by CT imaging in PET denoising. The separation of high-frequency components to enhance edge details is clever.
    *   The Dose-Adaptive Attention (DAA) module addresses a crucial gap in previous research by explicitly modeling dose-dependent relationships. This dynamic integration of dose levels into the attention mechanism provides a unique way to adapt the denoising process to varying acquisition parameters.
    *   The combination of diffusion models with multi-modal fusion and dose adaptation shows an improvement, however, there exist prior works that also combine these.

*   **Significance:**
    *   The work has significant potential to reduce radiation exposure in PET imaging by enabling accurate image reconstruction from lower doses. This is especially beneficial for sensitive populations like children and pregnant women.
    *   The proposed approach addresses a practical challenge in clinical PET imaging, where variations in patient physiology, metabolism, and tracer uptake can affect dose response. By explicitly modeling these dose-dependent relationships, MDAA-Diff offers a more robust solution for LPET denoising.
    *   The reported results show substantial improvements in both quantitative metrics (PSNR and SSIM) and qualitative visual assessments, demonstrating the practical value of the proposed method. The ablation studies provide strong evidence for the effectiveness of both the HWA and DAA modules.
    *   The comparison with several state-of-the-art methods strengthens the credibility of the findings and underscores the advancements achieved by MDAA-Diff.
    *   The code is publicly available for better verification.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the results look promising, the paper could benefit from a more in-depth analysis of the computational complexity of the proposed approach, especially the wavelet transform and attention mechanisms. This information is crucial for evaluating the practical feasibility of MDAA-Diff in clinical settings.
    *   The paper could also benefit from demonstrating the generalizability of MDAA-Diff to other PET tracers or imaging modalities. The current evaluation is limited to 18F-FDG and 68Ga-FAPI.
    *   The method improves the diffusion based denoising by including CT image features and dose adaptation, however, the improvement over the SOTA is not dramatic.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel and significant contribution to the field of low-dose PET imaging. The integration of CT-guided information and dose-adaptive mechanisms within a diffusion model framework is innovative and addresses practical challenges in clinical PET. The results demonstrate promising improvements in denoising performance, and the ablation studies support the effectiveness of the proposed modules. However, the relatively modest improvements over SOTA as well as the points on computational complexity and the need for further generalization warrant a slight reduction of the score.
