### clem:todd: A Framework for the Systematic Benchmarking of LLM-Based Task-Oriented Dialogue System Realisations
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.05445v1)
- **Authors**: Chalamalasetti Kranti, Sherzod Hakimov, David Schlangen
- **Abstract**: The emergence of instruction-tuned large language models (LLMs) has advanced the field of dialogue systems, enabling both realistic user simulations and robust multi-turn conversational agents. However, existing research often evaluates these components in isolation-either focusing on a single user simulator or a specific system design-limiting the generalisability of insights across architectures and configurations. In this work, we propose clem todd (chat-optimized LLMs for task-oriented dialogue systems development), a flexible framework for systematically evaluating dialogue systems under consistent conditions. clem todd enables detailed benchmarking across combinations of user simulators and dialogue systems, whether existing models from literature or newly developed ones. It supports plug-and-play integration and ensures uniform datasets, evaluation metrics, and computational constraints. We showcase clem todd's flexibility by re-evaluating existing task-oriented dialogue systems within this unified setup and integrating three newly proposed dialogue systems into the same evaluation pipeline. Our results provide actionable insights into how architecture, scale, and prompting strategies affect dialogue performance, offering practical guidance for building efficient and effective conversational AI systems.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "clem: todd: A Framework for the Systematic Benchmarking of LLM-Based Task-Oriented Dialogue System Realisations":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces `clem: todd`, a flexible framework for systematically benchmarking and evaluating LLM-based Task-Oriented Dialogue (TOD) systems. It leverages a self-play paradigm, where a user simulator (Player A) and a dialogue system (Player B) interact under the coordination of a Game Master.  The framework supports plug-and-play integration of various system architectures (monolithic, modular-programmed, modular-LLM), ensures consistent datasets, evaluation metrics, and compute settings, and facilitates detailed analysis across different configurations. The paper demonstrates the framework's utility by re-evaluating existing TOD systems and integrating newly proposed ones, analyzing trade-offs across architectures, model sizes, and user simulators concerning task performance and computational cost. The authors also show that clem: todd can be extended to evaluate dialogue systems on unseen and unrealistic domains beyond fixed datasets, promoting robustness testing.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in creating a *systematic* and *flexible* benchmarking framework tailored specifically for LLM-based TOD systems. While self-play is used in other LLM agent evaluation frameworks, its adaptation for TOD is beneficial, especially in addressing inconsistencies often found across existing TOD evaluations. The ability to plug-and-play different system architectures and user simulators within a consistent setup is an advantage. The extension to unseen/unrealistic domains is also valuable.
*   **Significance:** The work addresses an important need in the TOD research community. LLMs have revolutionized dialogue systems, but rigorous and comparable evaluations are lacking. `clem: todd` provides a platform for researchers to make informed decisions about system design, architecture choices, and prompting strategies. The insights on performance vs. cost trade-offs, and the impact of user simulator choice, are practically useful for building effective TOD systems. The introduction of the `US-spread` robustness metric is beneficial for comparing models.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper provides a clear description of the framework's architecture and implementation.
    *   The authors perform comprehensive experiments covering various LLM sizes, architectures, and settings.
    *   The analysis is insightful, highlighting key trade-offs and offering practical recommendations.
    *   The framework is open-source, enabling reproducibility and further development by the community.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the framework is flexible, the evaluation is limited to the MultiWOZ dataset and synthetic derivatives of it. Broader testing across other datasets would improve generalizability.
    *   The modular system design uses a classical pipeline style. Although this does enable a more rigorous control over the individual LLM components, it can potentially constrain the performance of modular systems and not represent their full potential.
    *   The evaluation only includes GPT-4o among closed-weight models. A comparison with other powerful proprietary LLMs (like Gemini and Claude) would strengthen the evaluation.
    * The current two-player interaction limitation needs extending to support multi-agent dialog scenarios.

**Score:** 7

**Justification:**

The paper presents a valuable and timely contribution to the TOD research community. `clem: todd` fills a gap by providing a structured and flexible platform for benchmarking LLM-based dialogue systems. The comprehensive experiments and actionable insights are significant strengths. However, the limitations regarding dataset diversity and the closed-weight model evaluations slightly temper the overall impact. The ability to reliably compare systems and analyze trade-offs makes the framework a solid tool for future research. The score reflects the paper's significant practical value balanced against its limitations in scope.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Conversational Process Model Redesign
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.05453v1)
- **Authors**: Nataliia Klievtsova, Timotheus Kampik, Juergen Mangler, Stefanie Rinderle-Ma
- **Abstract**: With the recent success of large language models (LLMs), the idea of AI-augmented Business Process Management systems is becoming more feasible. One of their essential characteristics is the ability to be conversationally actionable, allowing humans to interact with the LLM effectively to perform crucial process life cycle tasks such as process model design and redesign. However, most current research focuses on single-prompt execution and evaluation of results, rather than on continuous interaction between the user and the LLM. In this work, we aim to explore the feasibility of using LLMs to empower domain experts in the creation and redesign of process models in an iterative and effective way. The proposed conversational process model redesign (CPD) approach receives as input a process model and a redesign request by the user in natural language. Instead of just letting the LLM make changes, the LLM is employed to (a) identify process change patterns from literature, (b) re-phrase the change request to be aligned with an expected wording for the identified pattern (i.e., the meaning), and then to (c) apply the meaning of the change to the process model. This multi-step approach allows for explainable and reproducible changes. In order to ensure the feasibility of the CPD approach, and to find out how well the patterns from literature can be handled by the LLM, we performed an extensive evaluation. The results show that some patterns are hard to understand by LLMs and by users. Within the scope of the study, we demonstrated that users need support to describe the changes clearly. Overall the evaluation shows that the LLMs can handle most changes well according to a set of completeness and correctness criteria.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper explores the feasibility of using Large Language Models (LLMs) to facilitate conversational process model redesign (CPD).  The authors propose an approach where a user provides a redesign request in natural language, and the LLM, equipped with knowledge of process change patterns from the literature, attempts to apply the changes.  Instead of directly changing the model, the LLM first identifies a relevant change pattern, rephrases the user request to align with the pattern, and then applies the change based on the pattern's semantics. The paper evaluates this approach using a user study, assessing the quality of the LLM-redesigned models based on completeness and correctness criteria. The study analyses which existing change patterns are handled well by LLMs, identifies challenges in understanding and applying patterns, and uncovers areas where users need better support in describing their desired changes.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The paper's novelty lies in its specific combination of elements. While the individual components – LLMs, conversational interaction, process model redesign, and change patterns – are not entirely new, their integration within a structured CPD framework is innovative.  The explicit focus on leveraging existing process change patterns as a basis for LLM-driven redesign, as opposed to simply relying on the LLM's generative capabilities, is a distinct contribution. However, the incremental nature of combining existing concepts reduces the overall novelty.

**Significance:**

The paper addresses a relevant and important challenge in Business Process Management: bridging the communication gap between domain experts and process modelers. By exploring the use of LLMs to enable domain experts to directly redesign process models, the paper contributes to the broader goal of AI-augmented BPM systems. The evaluation offers practical insights into the strengths and limitations of current LLMs in this context, highlighting specific change patterns that work well and areas where further improvement is needed. This is useful information for researchers and practitioners working on similar systems. The user study offers valuable data and observations regarding the interaction between users and LLMs in a BPM context.
*The research is practically relevant for modernizing/improving existing systems.*

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Complexity:** The process models used in the evaluation are relatively simple. It's not clear how well the approach would scale to more complex, real-world process models with intricate interdependencies.
*   **Controlled Interaction:** The users did not interact directly with an agent in real time with feedback. Instead, static screenshots were used.
*   **Narrow Scope:** The selection of change patterns might not be exhaustive. The paper acknowledges this and adds a few potential extensions to the traditional set, but further expansion and categorization based on the study results would strengthen the approach.
*   **Generalizability:** The prompts used might be optimized to a certain LLM, potentially impacting generalizability of findings.

**Strengths:**

*   **Structured Approach:**  The multi-step redesign process (identify, derive, apply) provides a systematic and explainable way to leverage LLMs for process model redesign.
*   **Rigorous Evaluation:** The user study, while having limitations, provides valuable empirical data on the feasibility and challenges of the proposed approach. The use of multiple LLMs helps to generalize the findings.
*   **Practical Insights:**  The paper identifies specific change patterns that are well-suited for LLM-driven redesign and highlights areas where further research is needed, such as improved user guidance and more robust pattern implementation.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper presents a reasonably novel and significant contribution within the field of AI-augmented BPM, specifically in process model redesign. While individual components aren't revolutionary, their specific integration and the empirical evaluation provide practical value and insights. The limitations regarding model complexity and scope, but also the interesting approach in combining existing patterns in AI-driven systems, lead to a slightly higher result.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 6/10

### UKElectionNarratives: A Dataset of Misleading Narratives Surrounding Recent UK General Elections
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.05459v1)
- **Authors**: Fatima Haouari, Carolina Scarton, Nicolò Faggiani, Nikolaos Nikolaidis, Bonka Kotseva, Ibrahim Abu Farha, Jens Linge, Kalina Bontcheva
- **Abstract**: Misleading narratives play a crucial role in shaping public opinion during elections, as they can influence how voters perceive candidates and political parties. This entails the need to detect these narratives accurately. To address this, we introduce the first taxonomy of common misleading narratives that circulated during recent elections in Europe. Based on this taxonomy, we construct and analyse UKElectionNarratives: the first dataset of human-annotated misleading narratives which circulated during the UK General Elections in 2019 and 2024. We also benchmark Pre-trained and Large Language Models (focusing on GPT-4o), studying their effectiveness in detecting election-related misleading narratives. Finally, we discuss potential use cases and make recommendations for future research directions using the proposed codebook and dataset.
- **Summary**: Here is a concise summary and critical evaluation of the paper "UKElectionNarratives: A Dataset of Misleading Narratives Surrounding Recent UK General Elections":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces UKElectionNarratives, the first human-annotated dataset of misleading narratives circulated during the 2019 and 2024 UK General Elections. The authors construct a multi-level taxonomy of common misleading narratives in Europe, focusing on election integrity, economic uncertainty, foreign interference, and social issues. They annotate a dataset of 2000 tweets, benchmark Pre-trained and Large Language Models (PLMs and LLMs), specifically GPT-4o, for detecting these narratives, and discuss potential use cases for the dataset and codebook. The source code is made available for reproducibility.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary contribution is the dataset itself, as no prior dataset specifically focuses on misleading narratives in the context of UK General Elections. The taxonomy, while built upon existing research, is tailored for this specific domain. Using LLMs for detecting election-related narratives, although a growing trend, benefits from the specific dataset they have created.

*   **Significance:** The dataset addresses a crucial research need: to study and combat misleading narratives during elections. This type of dataset and research can potentially inform fact-checking efforts, media literacy programs, and the development of automated disinformation detection tools. The potential to analyze narrative patterns and their impact on public opinion is significant.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Comprehensive Taxonomy:** The multi-level taxonomy provides a structured framework for understanding and categorizing misleading narratives.
    *   **Careful Data Construction:** The three-stage annotation process, involving multiple annotators and a consolidation phase, enhances the dataset's quality.
    *   **Benchmarking with LLMs:** The experiments with GPT-4o offer a preliminary assessment of its capabilities in detecting misleading narratives, providing a baseline for future research.
    *   **Focus on Reproducibility:** Making the code and potentially dataset (with Twitter IDs, respecting data constraints) available promotes transparency and enables further research.
    *   **Ethics Statement:** Explicitly addressing ethics related to data collection, annotation and potential uses adds credibility.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Dataset Size:** 2000 tweets may be considered relatively small, especially given the number of narrative classes (32), potentially limiting the generalizability of the results.
    *   **Class Imbalance:** The imbalanced distribution of narratives could skew the performance of the evaluated models, requiring careful consideration in future research.
    *   **Limited Scope:** The dataset focuses solely on the UK and may not generalize well to other countries or election contexts.
    *   **LLM Benchmarking Depth:** While GPT-40 is evaluated, more extensive exploration of other LLMs and fine-tuning techniques could be conducted for more robust baseline performance.
    *   **Narrative complexity:** The annotations are for narratives not stance. The narratives represent more complex claims. How this dataset helps improve the current state-of-the-art in detecting narratives vs. stance is unclear

*   **Potential Influence:** The dataset has the potential to catalyze research on election disinformation, particularly within the UK context. It can also serve as a benchmark for developing and evaluating new methods for narrative detection and analysis. The taxonomy can be adapted for other countries and election cycles. However, the actual impact depends on how widely the dataset is adopted and used by the research community.
*   **Justification for score:**
    *   This contribution is quite timely, especially with the looming elections in multiple parts of the world. The contribution also tries to balance ethical challenges with reproducibility by focusing on narratives rather than the social accounts that are propagating them
    *   This contribution focuses a lot on data creation, but it leaves a lot to be desired in terms of model performance and in using this dataset and taxonomy to push the boundaries of the field. The insights from such fine grained narrative recognition has to be carefully analyzed and further validated

**Score: 6.5/10**

The paper presents a valuable and timely contribution with the creation of a new dataset for studying misleading narratives in UK elections. While the limited dataset size and scope, class imbalance and LLM benchmarking depth represent weaknesses, the dataset's potential impact on election disinformation research is significant. Also, as specified above, the contribution has lots of avenues of improvement by exploring techniques that can tackle these weaknesses in this contribution.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### Bring Reason to Vision: Understanding Perception and Reasoning through Model Merging
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.05464v1)
- **Authors**: Shiqi Chen, Jinghan Zhang, Tongyao Zhu, Wei Liu, Siyang Gao, Miao Xiong, Manling Li, Junxian He
- **Abstract**: Vision-Language Models (VLMs) combine visual perception with the general capabilities, such as reasoning, of Large Language Models (LLMs). However, the mechanisms by which these two abilities can be combined and contribute remain poorly understood. In this work, we explore to compose perception and reasoning through model merging that connects parameters of different models. Unlike previous works that often focus on merging models of the same kind, we propose merging models across modalities, enabling the incorporation of the reasoning capabilities of LLMs into VLMs. Through extensive experiments, we demonstrate that model merging offers a successful pathway to transfer reasoning abilities from LLMs to VLMs in a training-free manner. Moreover, we utilize the merged models to understand the internal mechanism of perception and reasoning and how merging affects it. We find that perception capabilities are predominantly encoded in the early layers of the model, whereas reasoning is largely facilitated by the middle-to-late layers. After merging, we observe that all layers begin to contribute to reasoning, whereas the distribution of perception abilities across layers remains largely unchanged. These observations shed light on the potential of model merging as a tool for multimodal integration and interpretation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Bring Reason to Vision: Understanding Perception and Reasoning through Model Merging" explores how to enhance the reasoning abilities of Vision-Language Models (VLMs) by merging them with Large Language Models (LLMs) specifically trained for math reasoning tasks.  The core idea is to use model merging, a technique that combines the parameters of pre-trained models, to transfer the reasoning skills of LLMs into VLMs *without* additional training. The authors perform extensive experiments across multiple VLMs, reasoning-focused LLMs, and math-related benchmarks.  They also investigate the internal mechanisms of perception and reasoning within VLMs by analyzing the effects of model merging on different layers of the network, finding evidence that perception is more heavily encoded in early layers and reasoning in later layers. Finally, the paper shows that integrating a logic-focused LLM can also improve performance on math reasoning tasks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The paper's novelty lies in applying model merging *across modalities* (i.e., combining VLMs and LLMs) to enhance reasoning capabilities. Most prior work in model merging has focused on combining models of the *same* type.  The exploration of layer-wise effects during the merging process to understand the separation (or lack thereof) of perception and reasoning is another valuable contribution. While model merging itself is not new, using it as an *interpretability tool* to analyze how abilities are distributed across network layers within *multimodal* models is a novel application. The use of the 'mask out' or KO-analysis to demonstrate layer-wise ability encoding and changes due to merging are important and show the authors are digging deeper. However, other interpretability methods may further enhance the study.

**Significance:**

The paper's findings have several significant implications:

*   **Capability Transfer:** It demonstrates a training-free method for transferring reasoning skills from LLMs to VLMs, which could alleviate the need for extensive multimodal training data, which is scarce and expensive.
*   **Model Understanding:** The layer-wise analysis provides insights into how perception and reasoning are represented within VLMs.  This understanding can guide the design of more efficient and effective VLMs in the future.
*   **Model Compositionality:** The results suggest that model merging can be a viable strategy for building complex multimodal systems by composing simpler, specialized models.
*   **Practical Impact:** Improved reasoning abilities are crucial for real-world VLM applications, such as scientific document interpretation and automated image understanding.
*   **Generalization evidence:** The paper would have been strengthened by exploring reasoning transfer to other tasks outside of the math reasoning domain.

**Strengths:**

*   **Comprehensive Experiments:** The paper includes a wide range of experiments across different models and datasets, lending strong support to its claims.
*   **Clear Presentation:** The paper is well-written and easy to understand, with clear explanations of the methods and results.
*   **Interpretability Analysis:** The layer-wise analysis is a valuable contribution to understanding how VLMs work.
*   **Reproducibility:** The authors have made their code publicly available, which promotes reproducibility.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Scope of Reasoning Transfer:** The primary focus is on math reasoning. While this is a valuable starting point, it is crucial to explore whether the approach generalizes to other types of reasoning (e.g., common-sense reasoning, logical deduction).
*   **Hyperparameter Sensitivity:** Model merging can be sensitive to hyperparameter settings (the merging weight). The paper acknowledges this but doesn't fully explore the impact of different hyperparameters on the results, especially as it applies to the internal layer analysis (knock-out ablation) method.
*   **Justification of Layer Knock-Out Method:** In the layer-wise analysis, the justification of the KO procedure is somewhat weak. What are the theoretical guarantees that this method yields the true importance of each layer? Other KO-approaches may be valuable to explore (e.g., zeroing-out parameters, etc.).
*   **Analysis Method Choices:** The authors could perhaps make other choices that can improve the analysis (e.g., feature visualizations, etc.).
*   **Limited Comparison to Finetuning:** The paper effectively shows the benefits of merge-tuning. The approach's computational/data advantages could be better highlighted with respect to end-to-end fine-tuning.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a novel and significant application of model merging, offering a promising approach to enhance VLM reasoning abilities and gain insights into their internal mechanisms. The strong experimental results and clear presentation are major strengths. However, the limited scope of reasoning transfer and the lack of a comparison to fine-tuning, as well as some weaknesses in the justification of KO ablation analyses, prevent it from achieving a higher score. While novel and impactful, there are clear avenues for future research to further strengthen the conclusions and expand the generalizability of the findings.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### ComPO: Preference Alignment via Comparison Oracles
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.05465v1)
- **Authors**: Peter Chen, Xi Chen, Wotao Yin, Tianyi Lin
- **Abstract**: Direct alignment methods are increasingly used for aligning large language models (LLMs) with human preferences. However, these methods suffer from the issues of verbosity and likelihood displacement, which can be driven by the noisy preference pairs that induce similar likelihood for preferred and dispreferred responses. The contributions of this paper are two-fold. First, we propose a new preference alignment method based on comparison oracles and provide the convergence guarantee for its basic scheme. Second, we improve our method using some heuristics and conduct the experiments to demonstrate the flexibility and compatibility of practical scheme in improving the performance of LLMs using noisy preference pairs. Evaluations are conducted across multiple base and instruction-tuned models (Mistral-7B, Llama-3-8B and Gemma-2-9B) with benchmarks (AlpacaEval 2, MT-Bench and Arena-Hard). Experimental results show the effectiveness of our method as an alternative to addressing the limitations of existing direct alignment methods. A highlight of our work is that we evidence the importance of designing specialized methods for preference pairs with distinct likelihood margin, which complements the recent findings in \citet{Razin-2025-Unintentional}.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ComPO, a novel preference alignment method for large language models (LLMs) that leverages comparison oracles to address issues such as verbosity and likelihood displacement, which are common in direct alignment methods like DPO.  ComPO specifically aims to better utilize noisy preference pairs (pairs where the preferred and dispreferred responses have similar likelihoods). The method is based on comparison oracles, treating preference pairs as outputs of a latent alignment goal.  The authors provide a convergence guarantee for the basic scheme and enhance it with heuristics to improve practical performance. Experiments across various base and instruction-tuned models and benchmarks (AlpacaEval 2, MT-Bench, Arena-Hard) demonstrate ComPO's effectiveness, particularly in mitigating verbosity and likelihood displacement.  A key aspect is highlighting the importance of specialized methods for preference pairs with small likelihood margins, complementing recent work on data curation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the use of comparison oracles within the direct preference alignment framework. While comparison oracles themselves aren't new in optimization, their specific adaptation to handle noisy preference pairs in LLM alignment is a distinct contribution.  The approach of explicitly recognizing and leveraging noisy data, rather than simply filtering it out, is also a relatively fresh perspective. The convergence guarantees provided, although limited to the basic scheme, add a theoretical underpinning. The application of this technique to LLM alignment is significant because previous research have primarily focused on either clean dataset or modifying the DPO algorithms.

*   **Significance:**  The significance stems from ComPO's potential to improve the reliability and safety of LLMs by more effectively utilizing the full spectrum of preference data.  Addressing verbosity and likelihood displacement are crucial steps towards better-aligned models. The experimental results, although relying on existing benchmarks, provide compelling evidence for ComPO's efficacy, particularly in enhancing length-controlled win rates and mitigating likelihood displacement, in DPO and SimPO. The paper also evidences the importance of speciallized method in noisy dataset, which is less explored previously.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper addresses a relevant and persistent problem in LLM alignment.
    *   The method has a clear theoretical basis (convergence guarantee, though for a simplified version).
    *   Experiments demonstrate practical improvements over existing methods (DPO, SimPO) on several benchmarks.
    *   The paper provides a clear motivation for the need of specialized methods for specific datasets.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The convergence guarantee is for a basic scheme and does not fully cover the practical implementations with heuristics used for scaling to large models.
    *   Experiments primarily rely on existing benchmarks and datasets. While comprehensive, the results could be strengthened by custom evaluations directly targeting safety and robustness against adversarial prompts.
    *   The performance improvement varies by model and benchmark, suggesting potential sensitivity to dataset characteristics and model architecture.  The Arena-Hard results are mixed, for example, demonstrating some limitations when longer responses are favored.

*   **Impact:** The paper's impact will depend on its adoption by the broader LLM alignment community. The method is relatively simple to implement and has the potential to be incorporated into existing alignment pipelines.  The emphasis on noisy data utilization could also influence future research directions in data curation and preference learning. The finding regarding the design of specialized method is also significant.

**Justification for Score:**

I am assigning a score of 7. The paper provides a novel and sound method of LLM alignment. The method uses the information contained in the noisy preference pairs in the data. This paper does the following:
1. Provides a novel and sound method for LLM alignment.
2. Provides convergence guarantee of basic scheme.
3. Provides practical enhancements.
4. Provides evaluation on multiple benchmarks.

Although, the experiments are strong. The convergence guarantee is limited to the basic method. Due to the current state of the art and recent works done with DPO, it is crucial to include a convergence guarantee. Additionally, custom experiments may be done to fully show the potential of the method.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Mogao: An Omni Foundation Model for Interleaved Multi-Modal Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.05472v1)
- **Authors**: Chao Liao, Liyang Liu, Xun Wang, Zhengxiong Luo, Xinyu Zhang, Wenliang Zhao, Jie Wu, Liang Li, Zhi Tian, Weilin Huang
- **Abstract**: Recent progress in unified models for image understanding and generation has been impressive, yet most approaches remain limited to single-modal generation conditioned on multiple modalities. In this paper, we present Mogao, a unified framework that advances this paradigm by enabling interleaved multi-modal generation through a causal approach. Mogao integrates a set of key technical improvements in architecture design, including a deep-fusion design, dual vision encoders, interleaved rotary position embeddings, and multi-modal classifier-free guidance, which allow it to harness the strengths of both autoregressive models for text generation and diffusion models for high-quality image synthesis. These practical improvements also make Mogao particularly effective to process interleaved sequences of text and images arbitrarily. To further unlock the potential of unified models, we introduce an efficient training strategy on a large-scale, in-house dataset specifically curated for joint text and image generation. Extensive experiments show that Mogao not only achieves state-of-the-art performance in multi-modal understanding and text-to-image generation, but also excels in producing high-quality, coherent interleaved outputs. Its emergent capabilities in zero-shot image editing and compositional generation highlight Mogao as a practical omni-modal foundation model, paving the way for future development and scaling the unified multi-modal systems.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper, based on the text available.

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Mogao, a unified framework for interleaved multi-modal generation. Mogao aims to improve upon existing models which are often limited to single-modal generation conditioned on multiple modalities. The core idea is to enable interleaved generation of text and images through a causal approach. This involves several architectural innovations: a deep-fusion design, dual vision encoders (VAE and ViT), interleaved rotary position embeddings, and multi-modal classifier-free guidance.  The model combines autoregressive text generation with diffusion-based image synthesis. The paper also introduces a large-scale interleaved multi-modal dataset and an efficient training strategy.  Experiments demonstrate state-of-the-art performance in multi-modal understanding and text-to-image generation, as well as emergent capabilities in zero-shot image editing and compositional generation. The authors position Mogao as a practical omni-modal foundation model, paving the way for future unified multi-modal systems.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The novelty lies primarily in the architectural combination and the interleaved training approach. While individual components like deep fusion, dual encoders, and rotary embeddings are not entirely new, their integration within this specific framework and for this specific task (interleaved generation) seems to be the key contribution. The dual CFG mechanism to address image repetition in interleaved generation also appears novel. The creation of a large, interleaved multi-modal dataset is another contribution, albeit dependent on in-house resources.

*   **Significance:** The significance of the work is considerable, if the claimed results hold up under scrutiny. The ability to generate coherent interleaved text and images has practical implications for content creation, interactive AI systems, and other applications. By demonstrating that such a model can be trained effectively and achieve state-of-the-art performance, the authors are pushing the boundaries of multi-modal AI. The architectural choices (deep fusion with decoupled layers) and training strategies (efficient complete teacher forcing) also have the potential to influence future research.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem definition: The paper clearly identifies the limitations of existing multi-modal models.
    *   Comprehensive approach: The paper addresses both architectural and training challenges.
    *   Strong empirical results: The paper claims state-of-the-art performance on multiple benchmarks. The human evaluation also suggests a notable improvement in subjective quality compared to existing solutions.
    *   In-depth ablation studies: The ablation studies justify the design choices made.
    *   Demonstrates compelling emergent capabilities: Zero-shot image editing and composition are valuable emergent abilities.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Dependency on proprietary dataset: The use of an in-house dataset, while understandable, makes it difficult for other researchers to directly replicate the results or compare against the same training data.
    *   Incremental novelty: While the *combination* of techniques is novel, the individual components are largely based on existing research. The degree of engineering effort and hyperparameter tuning required to make them work together is not fully clear from the paper.
    *   Limited details on the training data creation process: More detail on the nature of the created dataset and how it was curated would be helpful. For example, what sources were used, how was filtering and cleaning done, etc.?
    *   Potential for bias: The reliance on web data raises concerns about potential biases in the generated content. This is not addressed in the paper.

*   **Justification of Score:**
    The paper presents a solid contribution to the field of multi-modal AI. The architecture and training techniques are well-motivated, and the experimental results are promising. While the novelty of individual components may be incremental, the overall system represents a significant advance in interleaved multi-modal generation. The key weakness is the dependence on a proprietary dataset, limiting reproducibility. The paper also does not fully address potential risks relating to the potential for bias. Because of these weaknesses, the paper does not merit a score in the 9-10 range.

### DiffusionSfM: Predicting Structure and Motion via Ray Origin and Endpoint Diffusion
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.05473v1)
- **Authors**: Qitao Zhao, Amy Lin, Jeff Tan, Jason Y. Zhang, Deva Ramanan, Shubham Tulsiani
- **Abstract**: Current Structure-from-Motion (SfM) methods typically follow a two-stage pipeline, combining learned or geometric pairwise reasoning with a subsequent global optimization step. In contrast, we propose a data-driven multi-view reasoning approach that directly infers 3D scene geometry and camera poses from multi-view images. Our framework, DiffusionSfM, parameterizes scene geometry and cameras as pixel-wise ray origins and endpoints in a global frame and employs a transformer-based denoising diffusion model to predict them from multi-view inputs. To address practical challenges in training diffusion models with missing data and unbounded scene coordinates, we introduce specialized mechanisms that ensure robust learning. We empirically validate DiffusionSfM on both synthetic and real datasets, demonstrating that it outperforms classical and learning-based approaches while naturally modeling uncertainty.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the DiffusionSfM paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces DiffusionSfM, a novel end-to-end approach for Structure-from-Motion (SfM) that uses a denoising diffusion model to directly predict 3D scene geometry and camera poses from multi-view images. Unlike traditional SfM pipelines that separate pairwise matching and global optimization, DiffusionSfM unifies these into a single framework. The approach parameterizes scene geometry and cameras as pixel-wise ray origins and endpoints in a global coordinate frame, which are then predicted by a transformer-based diffusion model. To address challenges in training diffusion models with missing data and unbounded scene coordinates, the authors propose specialized mechanisms like GT mask conditioning and projective space parameterization. Experiments on synthetic and real datasets demonstrate that DiffusionSfM outperforms existing methods while naturally modeling uncertainty.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its unification of pairwise reasoning and global optimization within a diffusion-based framework for SfM. This is a significant departure from the traditional two-stage pipeline. The use of pixel-wise ray origins and endpoints as the scene representation for a diffusion model is also innovative. While RayDiffusion has explored ray representations and diffusion models for pose estimation, DiffusionSfM goes further by jointly predicting geometry and camera poses in a more fine-grained way, leading to a more complete SfM solution.  The solutions developed for the practical challenges (missing data and unbouned coordiantes) are also solid contributions.
*   **Significance:** The paper's significance stems from its potential to simplify and improve SfM pipelines. By directly predicting geometry and camera poses, it avoids the complexities of pairwise matching and global optimization, potentially leading to more robust and accurate results.  Modeling uncertainty within the SfM process is also a valuable attribute. The results showing competitive or superior performance to existing methods, especially in camera center estimation, further underscore the method's potential.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   End-to-end approach:  Unifies pairwise and global stages.
    *   Novel representation: Pixel-wise ray origins/endpoints.
    *   Addresses practical challenges: GT masking and projective space parameterization.
    *   Competitive performance:  Outperforms baselines in many cases.
    *   Explicit uncertainty modeling via diffusion process.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Computational cost: Diffusion models are computationally expensive, potentially limiting scalability.  The paper acknowledges and mitigates this somewhat with early stopping, but the method likely still trails pairwise methods in raw speed.
    *   Pixel space diffusion: While a novel choice, latent space diffusion might be more memory efficient and could unlock additional benefits.
    * Limited out-of-distribution generalization: The paper presents results on scene-level datasets. A stronger assessment of the generalization ability, specifically to new object classes or more varied environments, would be helpful.

**Justification for Score:**

While the paper presents a compelling and novel approach, the limitations regarding computational cost and the question of whether this approach truly scales to large scene models keep it from being a higher score. The experimental results are strong and the core idea of unifying SfM via a diffusion model is highly promising. The ablations provided support the choices made in the construction of the model.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10
